82. **Founder calls, Oct 8 (for the tap, label, closet and camera builds).**
    1. **"Reads as …" shows for every detected style,** smart casual included.
    2. **The closet-call test is approved** (about $0.65 on the 56 test photos) before the call reaches the app.
    3. **The vintage-sport label is "Vintage sport"** (was "Sporty"); the card reads "Reads as vintage sport".
    4. **Front-camera photos are saved in the true view** (not mirrored like the preview), so logos and text on clothes read correctly. That's the iOS default, and it helps the closet read brands.
    - **Also settled (Claude):** Mirror mode defaults to the rear camera. The front camera can't see the reflection the Mirror tip describes.
    - **Build order:** the tap + label + style rename take migration V5; the closet columns take V6.

83. **Full-resolution capture: the camera shoots 12 MP 4:3 photos; photo prep works at 2048** (founder approved the build Oct 8; mirror and Mirror-mode calls are #82.4 and its "also settled").
    - **Capture:** session preset `.photo` (was `.high`, which gave 1920×1080 video frames, so the guide and the photo were 16:9 and cropped at the sides). After every configuration commit (including a flip) the photo size is picked from the active format's own list: the largest with a long edge ≤ 4032, so 12 MP on both cameras (48 MP is wasted: nothing we write is larger than 2048, and a size not in the list crashes). JPEG explicit; Live Photo, depth, ProRAW and deferred delivery off; output prioritization `.quality`, each shot `.balanced`; flash off (the front "flash" is the white screen, the rear the torch). Video frames stay preview-sized and mirrored on the front camera so the guide matches what he sees.
    - **Front photos are saved true-view** (#82.4): `mirrorFrontPhoto = false` in `KittedCameraView.swift`; one line to flip back.
    - **Mirror mode uses the rear camera; Propped the front** (#82 "also settled"). A manual flip still overrides until the next mode change; a saved Mirror mode opens on the rear camera.
    - **Guide fix for the wider frame:** aspect fill shows only the middle ~61% of a 3:4 frame's width on an iPhone 17 Pro, so "move left/right" is judged on the on-screen x (`sx = (cx − (1 − shown)/2) / shown`, `shown = min(1, viewAspect / (W/H))`), not the buffer's.
    - **Prep:** decodes at 2048 (was 4096; same work, less memory and time), one shared CIContext, and reads the original's upright size from its properties (EXIF 5–8 swap). Writes: upload 1024 px q0.8 (locked config, unchanged), kept 2048 unblurred, thumb 400, and a NEW face-hidden 2048 px **detail copy** at `Caches/detail/<sha>.jpg` (q0.85), the source for closet crops; files older than 7 days are pruned on each prep. New fields: source/upload/kept/detail sizes, upload bytes, Vision ms, total ms; refusals also return the source size.
    - **Face hiding re-checked on the Mac** with the new Prep code (scratch Swift build of `enum Prep`): all 52 photos in `inputs/fitcheck-photos` + `inputs/mirror-fit-check-1`, as-is and upscaled to 12 MP stand-ins. Status and number of hidden regions identical to the old 4096 code on every photo (51 sent, 1 two-person refusal); a contact sheet of the 51 uploads shows every face hidden; the chest-down mirror shot is still sent with nothing to hide. Mac prep of a 12 MP stand-in: about 120 ms.
    - **Simulator (sample sheet → Choose from your photos):** a 12 MP JPEG and a HEIC with EXIF orientation 6 (both with GPS + camera model added) gave `[prep] ok · source 2688×4032 → upload 683×1024 (153 KB) · kept 1365×2048 · detail 1365×2048 · vision 360 ms · total 990 ms`, then a real Gemini read (8.0 in 7.9 s). Upload and detail face-hidden, the kept photo unblurred; no GPS, EXIF comment or camera model left in any file. The live capture path (`[camera] ready …`, `[camera] captured … 3024×4032 · … MB · … ms`) can only run on the phone.
    - **Older native builds keep working with the new JS:** `takePhotoFrom()` accepts the old bare-uri result, and the new prep fields are optional. The phone gets 12 MP only after `tools/phone/install.sh`.
    - **Tools:** `tools/phone/pull.sh` also copies `Library/Caches/detail` into `$OUT/detail`. `extraction-test/run_test.py --media-res {default,high,ultra_high}` adds Gemini 3's per-part `media_resolution` next to the image (recorded in the manifest; non-default uses the v1alpha endpoint) for a later sharper-image test; default sends nothing, so the locked config is unchanged.


## Oct 8 · Closet accuracy (style-council session; numbered from #90 so parallel builders' entries don't collide)

90. **Closet matching engine (phase 1) and the closet-call test.**
    - **Engine** (`@kitted/core` closetColor/closetShade/closetIdentity, commit a9cd8d3): a deterministic identity score with vetoes.
      - Light grey vs charcoal: new item. Zip vs pullover: new. Nike vs plain: ask. Jeans vs "sweatpants": ask, and after he confirms, an alias makes it auto-match.
      - Two items he called different are never re-asked.
      - Shade text maps to a fixed ShadeKey, so the model's raw words are never shown.
      - `displayHex` stays inside the family band.
      - 38 tests pass. Not wired into the app yet: `closetPlan` still merges by family until the tap work frees `fitcheck/service.ts`. Migration V6 is reserved for the closet tables.
      - **Builder's design changes:**
        - a shoe-profile one-step gap costs −2;
        - matching logo fields +1;
        - a shade word naming the item's exact color counts as exact;
        - brand vs no-logo always asks.
    - **Closet-call test** (`extraction-test/closet_test.py`, `prompt_closet_v1.txt`, `schema_closet_v1.json`, `results/closet1/`; founder-approved #82): **every pass bar met** on 56 photos × 3 runs.
      - 0 index-alignment errors; lightness 95%; shade key 92% (raw text 72%); details Jaccard 0.94; logo 98%; box IoU median 0.98; 4.3 s median; about 360 output tokens.
      - The founder's hoodie read "medium grey", mid, Nike, hood/front pouch/drawstring on all 3 runs.
    - **Next:** wire the closet call (after the score card shows) + V6 + the engine into the check flow, then crops after the founder reinstalls with full-res capture (#83).

91. **Built: closet accuracy phase 2, the closet call and the engine wired into the app** (design from the planning agent, approved by the founder Oct 8: the separate closet call after the score card, detailed graphic tiles now, crops in phase 3).
    - **What he sees:** the score card exactly as before; a moment later its closet row reads `Added 3 to Closet · 1 question` (or `Checking the details…` while the closet call is out, at most 12 s). S11 lists new pieces with their new names and detailed tiles, asks `Same as your khaki chinos?` with both tiles (`Yes, same one` · `No, a new one` · `It's my …`), and lists `Already in your closet` with `Not the same`. The Closet tab shows the detailed tiles (newest first) and a card per open question.
    - **The founder's case (demo `ph1`, answered Jeans):** `Medium grey Nike hoodie` (grey family, medium step, Nike mark, pouch/drawstrings/cuffs drawn), `Black jeans` with the type confirmed on its sighting and the item, `Black slides`; score 6.5 unchanged. A later grey hoodie read as charcoal with no logo is its own piece `Charcoal hoodie` (hex #4A4D55 vs #9CA0A6; −2 near color, −2 no logo vs his Nike one → new, not even a question).
    - **Closet call** (`services/closetSource/`): closet1 prompt + schema synced byte-for-byte from extraction-test (`scripts/sync-gemini.mjs`, tested); stubs built only from v3 enum values; every field validated (index in range and unique, sane boxes, shade ≤ 3 plain words, logo text ≤ 3 words of `[A-Za-z0-9 &'.-]`); 12 s timeout; one retry 2 s later on network / 5xx / 429 / non-STOP / invalid; `[closet] 4.3 s · in … / out … tokens` in the Metro log. Live only in a DEV build with the key, for a real photo, with consent (the same gate as the reading); demo photos replay **real closet1 answers** (`assets/demo-closet/closet-readings.json`, re-indexed from `results/closet1/raw.json` run 0 by `scripts/make-demo-closet.mjs`: no new calls, $0). Stored in `readings` (source `closet`, by image hash), so the same photo reuses it.
    - **When:** full check → after scoring (fire and forget); re-check → only the fixed pieces go through the engine, the rest add a sighting to the first check's pieces (no extra wear: same outfit, same day); shoe snap → footwear only, into the first check's summary. Offline, failed or slower than 12 s → written from the v3 fields through the same engine (`state: 'fallback'`); a late answer, or one catch-up on the next foreground within 24 h, adds the details (re-matches only if he hasn't touched S11 for that check and nothing else linked its new pieces).
    - **Storage V6:** `closet_items` + `shade`, `profile` (aliases, distinctFrom, confirmed, name flags), `crop_file`/`crop_score` (phase 3), `art`, `merged_into`, `maybe_same_as`, `updated_at`; new `closet_observations` (one row per sighting; existing items backfilled with one each, tested on SQLite). `closetSummary` grows `links`, `asks`, `state` (old readers of matched/added/undone unchanged). The seed's 16 items carry closet fields (`make_seed.py`; never scored; asserts pass) and one `seed` sighting each.
    - **Answers:** Yes → the sighting moves to the existing piece (worn +1; a different type read becomes an alias), the flagged copy is folded in and hidden; No → both stay and are never asked about again (distinctFrom); re-answerable (`Change`). Not the same → that sighting becomes its own piece. Undo marks the sighting undone.
    - **Corrections follow the stored link** (fixes the bug where `saveCorrection` re-matched by family and could recolor the wrong piece; 04 §7): still fits (no veto) or seen once → patch; no longer fits a piece seen 2+ times → the sighting moves (engine), the old piece loses a wear and keeps its look. The tap answer feeds the sighting's `user_confirmed` the same way (Skip learns nothing).
    - **Drift guard:** a piece's shown color, shade, brand, sole and wash change only when 2 newer sightings agree, or he confirms; details show when at least half the described sightings have them.
    - **The score never changes because of the closet** (tested: before/after the call, after Yes, Not the same, Undo and a re-score): checks are scored first, against the closet as it was; a check's own new pieces (and, for a re-check, its first check's) are excluded from its fixes, and every fit-check write keeps the closet's part of the row (`checkWrites.ts` queue).
    - **Tests:** `npm run check` green: 151 core, 242 app (new: closetService 19, closetSource 17, itemName 9, artSpec 8, V6 on SQLite, seed sightings, a copy check that every closet name in every demo photo uses only fixed-table words). Demo screens **37** (`Same as your khaki chinos?`) and **38** (Closet with detailed tiles + one question); screenshots in `screenshots/closet/` (git-ignored).

92. **Settled in the build (Claude; differs from the design or fills its gaps):**
    - **Demo closet answers are real, not hand-authored:** the closet1 test already called all 12 demo photos + ph1, so they were re-indexed for $0. p01b/p01a's sneakers weren't in that test's stubs (its reading said shoes hidden), so they stay "not seen" (v3 fields), like a real miss.
    - **Core `reconcileReading` takes `sightings`** (the stored history replaces the item's own garment), so the sighting that created a piece isn't counted twice.
    - **Names:** the color word is the app's own (`labels.ts`: "Beige overshirt" stays beige, chinos say khaki) with core `shadeWords`' step and finish in front ("Medium grey", "Heather grey", "Washed black"); never "pale"/"deep"; denim keeps its wash word and only takes a finish ("Faded mid-wash jeans"). When two would share a name, the **older keeps it** and the newer gets the detail word (`zip`, `pullover`, `cargo`…), else a number; seed names and names he set never change. A brand outside the known list needs 2 sightings and one plain word. An `other` type reads as top / layer / bottoms / shoes.
    - **A Yes after the quick tap:** if this sighting's type is one he confirmed (jeans) and the piece's type wasn't, the piece takes his word (type changed + confirmed) instead of only an alias; Change puts it back.
    - **A type correction renames an auto-named piece**, so the toast says `Saved to your black sweatpants.` (the piece's new name), not the old one.
    - **Wrong item → remove** now takes that piece back out (as Undo) when this was its only sighting.
    - **A correction while the closet call is out** writes the fallback first, so it has a piece to follow.
    - **Re-check fix targets:** a whole-piece target (`outerwear`, `*`, `top.color_family`) is a changed piece; a field target (`bottom.hem`: cuff them) is the same piece worn differently.
    - **No retry after a 12 s timeout** (it would make 26 s); catch-up is for full checks only (re-check and snap sightings keep their v3 fields).
    - **Files outside the listed set:** `features/correct/CorrectSheet.tsx` (its "Closet updated" pill now follows the link), `features/fitcheck/session.ts`, `demo/scenarios.ts` + `prepareDemoScreen.ts` (screens 37–38; waits for the closet call), `tap.test.ts` (the toast name), `scenarios.test.ts`.
    - **Copy to skim (new, in `en.ts` → `addedInBuild`):** `Checking the details…`, `1 question` / `{n} questions`, `{n} already in your closet`, `Already in your closet`, `Not the same`, `It's my {item}`, `This photo`, `In your closet`, `Saved as your {item}.`, `Kept as a new piece.`, `Change`, `A quick question`, and the VoiceOver detail words (`full zip`, `front pouch`, `drawstrings`…).

93. **Lessons from the closet build:**
    - **Share one id factory between services in tests.** Two counters both made `R-1`: the closet reading overwrote the scoring reading and a copy check passed with nothing to check. The app uses UUIDs; the tests now share one counter, and the copy check asserts it saw 30+ names.
    - **expo-sqlite's `INSERT OR REPLACE` moves a row to the end,** so a patched closet item jumps in `all()`. Sort grids explicitly (the Closet tab: newest first).
    - **On the Simulator the first tap after a demo launch only dismisses the dev menu's `Tools` label** (as noted in CLAUDE.md); tap twice when driving demo screens.

## Oct 8 · Closet tab shape (UI research session; numbered #84)

84. **Founder approved all four Closet-tab calls (Oct 8).** Research and wireframes: `mockups/closet-concepts.html` (3 agents: internal docs, 18 competitor apps, user psychology).
    1. **Combinations live in Closet, as a Pieces | Outfits segmented control.** Pieces is the default (screen 15 as drawn), and the rotation card opens Outfits. There's no 5th tab, and Lookbook stays history only (worn checks, real photos, real scores).
       - *Why not Lookbook:* mixing "what I wore" with "what I could wear" breaks the calendar and "Days dressed with intent".
       - *Why not a 5th tab:* it would be empty in week one, it splits the closet in two, and it's a full extra screen before beta.
    2. **No avatar or AI try-on; use a "standing outfit".** His garment tiles are stacked in body order (top, bottoms, shoes, with a layer at the side) on one 3:4 tile. Outfits he has worn show his own kept photo instead.
       - *Why:* JMR 2023 (6 studies, 8,000+ shoppers) found virtual fitting rooms lower self-esteem for higher-BMI shoppers, which clashes with "never rate bodies".
       - Avatars are widely reported as inaccurate (Doppl, Alta, Glance).
       - Try-on APIs cost about $0.05–0.075 per image, about 15× a scoring call.
       - Try-on needs his unblurred body sent to another vendor, which breaks #54–55.
       - This re-confirms the earlier cut in brainstorm/01.
    3. **The rotation count is top + bottoms + shoes combos the rules rate "Put together" (7.0) or better at Everyday; a layer never multiplies it.** This settles the 04-logic open question: the mockup's 37 can't be reproduced, and counting layers gives about 180 for 16 pieces.
    4. **Unworn outfits show no score or score word.** This extends the morning card's "no predicted score" rule (02). Worn outfits show their real score. Scores rank the list but are never displayed for unworn outfits.
    - **Also approved with it:**
      - Each piece's screen (C2) gets "Goes with", the 3 best outfits built around that piece (`pickOutfit` with it pinned), plus worn count, last worn and outfit count, then "Style this", which opens Stylist.
      - Outfits view: occasion chips; "Try next" (not yet worn, best first) and "Your go-tos" (worn).
      - Tapping an outfit opens "Wear this", which goes to Stylist.
      - A thin-closet state that names the next check or buy and never says the closet is lacking.
    - **Engine:** a top-N variant of `pickOutfit` (keep a sorted list, not `best`), and `rotation()` returning item ids. No AI and free on every plan. About 3.5 days on top of the tab.
    - **Deferred to v1.1:** the swipe shuffle ("pin one, shuffle the rest"); no retention evidence was found.
    - **Owner:** the style-council session owns the Closet tab (CLAUDE.md). This entry is its UI brief.
    - **Research gap:** Reddit wasn't reachable by Claude's tools. A Codex prompt was given to the founder; its findings may refine copy and details, not these four calls.

## Oct 8 · One quick tap + Reads as

85. **Built: the one quick tap ("Jeans or sweatpants?"), the "Reads as …" label, and the style-key rename (founder approved the features and the wording Oct 8; a picked style never changes the score).** Spec: `handoff/02-screens.md` "S5q" and "S6s".
    - **The founder's Oct 7 outfit (demo photo `ph1`: his reading on s12's stock photo, never his photo):** at Everyday the card asks `Jeans or sweatpants?` → Jeans **6.5**, "Reads as streetwear", the one fix "Cuff them once" → 7.0, Black jeans added to Closet; Sweatpants 5.0; Skip 6.5 + `Scored as jeans. Not right? Tap.` (opens "Not quite right?" with Sweatpants picked, 6.5 → 5.0). Date / Going out 5.5 vs 3.5, Interview / Event 4.0 vs 3.0; Gym / Home don't ask. Demo screens **34** asking · **35** answered · **36** skipped.
    - **How it works (code):** after the read, `askQuestion` with the same options the card is scored with (one builder, tested) → the check is `asking` and the question is stored on it (survives the app closing). Nothing is counted or added until he answers. The answer is a new reading (`tapAnswer`, only the type changes, never the certainty: tested on every asking parity reading × 8 occasions × 2 styles, the scores always match the question's). It counts on the check's own day (an answer after 4 am still counts for its day); the re-check window starts at the answer.
    - **Decided in the build (Claude):**
      - **A re-check can't undo the answer.** `recheckMerge` keeps the first reading's piece only when the type matches, so the jeans read as sweatpants again would have dropped 6.5 → 5.0. Core `carryTapAnswer` copies his current answer onto the re-check's reading first, except when a fix replaces that whole piece. Re-checks and shoe snaps never ask.
      - **Same photo again** reuses his answer (no question). It's free only if that photo was already scored once: a check set aside while asking never counted, so sending that photo again counts (otherwise asking, then sending a different photo, then the first again, would be a free check).
      - **A newer photo while a question is open** sets the open card aside: `Not scored. You sent a newer photo, so nothing was counted.` (no Try again). Only after the new photo passes the face check.
      - **Answers are queued;** a second tap (or a double tap) does nothing. The occasion can't change while asking (plain text on the question card).
      - **An answer (not a Skip) marks the closet piece's type as confirmed.** "Not quite right? → Wrong item" now lists sweatpants and joggers, plus the piece's current type and its tap look-alikes (it lacked sweatpants, so the skip line had nowhere to go).
      - **The question card uses existing parts only:** the tagline with the occasion, a static score-sized block (no shimmer: nothing is loading), the question, `I couldn't tell from the photo. It changes your score.`, two equal buttons in the question's word order (no scores, the as-read answer isn't marked, so it doesn't lead him), Skip with `I'll score it as jeans.` VoiceOver announces the question, then the score after an answer, and moves focus to the result.
    - **"Reads as …":** `Reads as streetwear` (tag icon + footnote) under the summary for every detected look, smart casual included (#82.1). Stored on the check on every re-score. The "Scored for" line, its strings and its Why section are deleted (core `styleEffects` stays, tested). The Why sheet gets a ±0 row `Reads as streetwear · the look it's going for`. The card's tagline no longer shows the style.
    - **Style keys (migration V5):** preppy and old money → `classic_prep` ("Classic prep"), gorpcore → `outdoor` ("Outdoor"); vintage sport is "Vintage sport" (#82.3, was "Sporty"). V5 rewrites checks, ideas, the remembered card style (`idea.style.day/evening`), the style inside re-scored checks' details, and his picks (order and weights kept); tested on a real SQLite. Every read also maps old keys (`normStyleKey`). `seed.json` keeps the old keys on purpose, so that mapping is always exercised. The closet columns take V6.
    - **Morning-card edits** (pre-cleared with the UI session): `service.ts` normStyle, `MorningCard.tsx` myStyles, `copy.ts` styleLabel, each plus its import. Screens 25 and 30 checked on the Simulator: the pill reads "Smart casual"; the menu lists Your styles (Smart casual, Workwear), More styles (Streetwear, Minimal, Classic prep, Vintage sport, Outdoor).
    - **Tests:** `npm run check` green: typecheck, lint, 148 core tests, 187 app tests (new: core `tap.test.ts`, app `tap.test.ts` with 19 cases, `styleKeys.test.ts`, V5 on SQLite, copy checks for every tap string = core's text, Reads as on the demo set).

86. **Found in the build: the label follows his pick when two looks both fit, so screen 7 reads "smart casual", not "minimal."** p01b fits both minimal and smart casual; rubric v4 lets his picks break that tie, and the first-run profile's first pick is smart casual. So screen 7 shows `Reads as smart casual` while its next-level line ("Mix textures or shades, like a knit or suede.") is the minimal one, because the fix engine uses the detected looks, never his picks (#81). Logged as differs from mockup (02 §6 #23–25). For the founder (taste): keep the tie-break (the label agrees with what he said he likes), or always show the first detected look (the label always agrees with the next-level line). Recommended: keep it; it's what rubric v4 decided and it's 0 points either way.
    - Also seen at the largest text size: "Re-check after fixing" is clipped to "Re-check after fixi" (an existing card button, not from this build; for the UI session).

87. **Closet details refined by Codex's Reddit research (Oct 8; `inputs/closet-research-codex.md`).** 39 Reddit threads; 0 TikToks reachable. It confirms #84's four calls. Claude settled these details (UI, not taste; founder may veto):
    - **Opening segment:** Closet opens on **Outfits once he can make 3+ outfits**, otherwise on Pieces. After he taps a segment, the tab remembers his choice. Revises #84.1's "Pieces is the default". *Why:* the repeated male pain is "I rewear the same couple outfits"; the payoff is combinations, not inventory.
    - **"Goes with" roles:** it shows 3 options labelled **Best match · Dressier · More relaxed**, picked from the rubric's dressiness levels. If no dressier or more relaxed option exists, the next best fills the slot. *Why:* "what pants go with this?" threads want one answer plus a couple of alternatives.
    - **Reasons:** every outfit card carries a plain reason line from templates (like the morning card's why lines), never blank. *Why:* visible rules are what make suggested combos feel trustworthy, not random.
    - **Next best buy:** it names a kind and spec ("White leather sneakers · plain, low"), never a brand. Tapping it shows the outfits it would unlock, with the new piece dashed. There are no shop links in v1. *Why:* "buy this" reads as an ad unless it's grounded in his closet.
    - **Wear counts:** neutral only ("Worn 2×", "Last worn Sep 28") on a piece's screen. No "neglected" filter or "not worn in 30 days" nudges. *Why:* wear tracking turns into guilt.
    - **Google Photos Wardrobe:** confirmed on Google's pages (Sep 24, 2026; US/India/Brazil). It needs an AI Pro/Ultra plan or 1,000+ photos of yourself plus Face Groups. Early users report few items and no way to correct them. Kitted's edge is correction and judgement.
    - **Noted, not acted on:** men talk about "what to fix" more than about scores, so fixes should feel at least as valuable as the number (input for P7 polish). Codex flags "old money" as cringe, which supports renaming it "Classic prep" (#82).
    - **Copy to skim:** "Best match", "Dressier", "More relaxed", and the reason templates.

88. **Closet: a clear "no outfits yet" state, scanning clothes in as an option, and an online "Should I buy this?" for later (founder, Oct 8).**
    - **No outfits yet (settled):** Outfits needs a top, bottoms and shoes. Until then, the Outfits view and the rotation card say so plainly. The title is `No outfits yet`, followed by a 3-row checklist (Top ✓ · Bottoms ✓ · Shoes, missing) and the ways to fill the gap: `Scan a piece` (when built) · `Tap basics you own` (C5) · `Describe it` (C4d) · `Check my fit`. The checklist names the missing slot ("Add shoes and you'd have 4 outfits") and never says the closet is lacking. This replaces 02's one-line `closet.rotation.locked`. The 1–2 outfit state keeps #84's "{n} outfits so far" card with the next unlock.
    - **Scanning clothes (founder wants it; brought back from v1.1 into v1 pending 2 calls):** this is C4's "Take photo" / "Choose photos", which #56 pushed to v1.1 because no item-reading prompt existed. It's optional and never asked for up front.
      - A photo can hold several pieces laid flat (tip: `Lay a few pieces flat, not overlapping.`), read with boxes like the closet call (#82/#90). The review sheet lists them (S11 rows) before saving.
      - Fit and length can't be read off a bed. They stay unknown until he wears the piece in a fit check, and the closet matching then fills them in.
      - Photos with no person need no face blur. If a face shows up, the usual blur and nothing-sent rule applies.
      - **Needs:** an item-reading prompt plus a small test, about 3.5 days of build, and a free-tier rule. Recommended: scans are free up to 30 pieces a day, since a fuller closet is what makes Outfits work. It's an AI call at about 0.4¢ a photo.
      - **Order:** after the server and P4. If beta time runs short, Describe it and Starter essentials (no AI) cover the gap and scanning slips to v1.1.
      - **Bonus:** it builds the same item reading that "Should I buy this?" needs.
      - **Founder approved (Oct 8):** about $1 of Gemini for the item-photo test, and free scans up to 30 pieces a day. Owner: the style-council session (closet call and schema). It reuses `schema_closet_v1` fields where it can, plus slot and garment type. Photos: the founder shoots ~20 of his own clothes (shot list in CLAUDE.md) into `inputs/item-photos/`.
    - **"Should I buy this?" gets an online version (future, after #40's in-store version in v1.1).** While browsing a shop app or website, he shares the product page or a screenshot to Kitted from the iOS share sheet. He gets the same answer as in the store: what it goes with in his closet, "+N outfits", a duplicate warning, and Buy it / Skip it. Needs an iOS Share Extension (a separate native target) and reading product photos (often on a model; nothing about the model is rated). Target: v1.2.
    - **Fitting-room mode (founder idea, Oct 8; future, folded into "Should I buy this?" in v1.1).** He tries something on in a store and gets a straight verdict: keep it or put it back. Today a mirror fit check in a fitting room already works (score plus fixes, nothing new to build), which is enough for launch videos. The future mode adds:
      - which piece is new ("Trying on: the olive overshirt");
      - a verdict line built from the score's fit and proportion readings plus closet matching ("Fits well · goes with 6 things you own · +9 outfits", or "Runs long in the sleeves · skip unless it's the only size");
      - Buy it / Skip it.
      - Not now, because it needs the item reading from scanning and the closet matching to land first. The verdict must come from code (rubric readings plus rotation delta), never from the model's opinion.

## Oct 8 · Camera guidance (camera-guidance session; numbered from #100 so parallel sessions' entries don't collide)

100. **Camera guidance plan (Claude; the founder asked for it in all three ways a photo comes in).** Two layers everywhere: **live tips** in the pill before the shot, and a **quick check after the shot** (or a library pick) that opens the S3 panel inside the camera with `Retake` / `Use it anyway`. A refusal always says why in words; nothing is dropped silently.
    - **Native measures, TypeScript judges.** The camera and a new `assess(uri, kind)` only measure (brightness, highlight share, the camera's ISO and exposure, frame-to-frame motion, a sharpness score, people, faces, clothes labels, piece boxes). Every threshold and the tip priority live in `features/camera/guidance.ts`, pure and tested, so they can be tuned without a rebuild. Older native builds (no new fields) fall back to today's body-only guide.
    - **Fit-check live tips, most urgent first:** too dark (+ `Turn on flash`) · no one in frame · bright light behind you · too close · too far · move left/right · hold still (Mirror only; Propped is hands-free) · good. Problem tips are spoken in Propped (D5 already asks for "spoken once per change, throttled"; he's 2–3 m away and can't read the pill).
    - **Fit-check after the shot:** too dark (with `Retake with flash` when the flash is off), too blurry, bright light behind you: `Use it anyway` allowed. No person and can't-hide-the-face (ruling 10): no `Use it anyway`, as in 02 S3. Two people: unchanged (S23 after the cover closes). Shoes cut off: not a retake reason (partial check). Lens smudge (in S3's table): **not built**, because haze can't be told from blur on the phone.
    - **Scan clothes (C4 "Take photo", #88): its own route `/scan`, rear camera.** The preview is sized to the photo's own 4:3, so what he sees is exactly what's saved (the fit camera's aspect fill hides the sides). Each piece found gets a corner-bracket box. Live tips: too dark (+ torch) · a person in view ("Lay it flat or hang it up") · no clothes found · hold still · glare · a piece cut off at the edge · too far · good ("{n} pieces in view"). A standing hint: `Lay pieces flat, front side up, not overlapping.` After the shot every reason allows `Use it anyway` (nothing private is at stake; any face is still blurred).
    - **Vision requests:** body pose + faces at ~10 Hz (fit, unchanged); brightness from the luma plane and motion from a 64-px frame difference on every analyzed frame; in scan mode human rectangles + faces and `VNGenerateForegroundInstanceMaskRequest` (~3/s), `VNClassifyImageRequest` (~1/s; verified Oct 8 on the Mac: 1,303 labels incl. clothing, textile, hoodie, jacket, jeans, polo, suit, shoes, sneaker, boot, footwear, sock, hat, scarf, necktie, bed, bedding, pillow; no shirt/pants/sweater labels, so "clothes found" relies on the general ones plus the piece boxes).
    - **Can't be done on the phone, so tips only:** front side vs. back of a tee; overlapping pieces (they merge into one box). The item reading (style-council session) can flag "back side" later.
    - **Scans before the item reading exists:** a scan is prepped on the phone (any face blurred; a person whose head can't be found is not kept for sending, ruling 10) and saved to a scan inbox; the app says plainly `Reading clothes from photos is coming soon.` The style-council session owns the reading, the Closet tab and the C4 "+" menu; this session only provides `/scan` and the inbox.
101. **Built: camera guidance in all three ways a photo comes in (Oct 8; plan #100).** Verified on the Simulator (scripted states, demo screens 41–53) and on the Mac with the same Swift files; live behavior needs the founder's iPhone.
    - **Native (rebuild + pod install done):** `KittedPrep.swift` (photo prep moved out of the module and split into locate + write; on all 52 test photos the status, regions hidden, pose and upload bytes are identical to the old code) with a clothes-scan path (`prepareItems`: no person needed, every face hidden, written to `Documents/scans`); `KittedGuide.swift` (measures only: brightness, near-white share, motion, a sharpness ratio, people, faces, clothes labels, piece boxes from the foreground instance mask; `assess(uri, kind)` for the after-shot check). The camera view sends the measures with every guide event and takes `kind="scan"`.
    - **Thresholds (guidance.ts), calibrated on the Mac:** 52 real photos plus copies darkened 2 and 3.5 stops, brightened 2 stops, motion-blurred 10 and 25 px and soft-focused 6 px (at 2048 px). Result: **no false alarms on the 52 real photos**; "Too blurry" on 93 of 104 motion-blurred copies (8 passed, 3 refused for another reason) and 51 of 52 soft ones; darkness on about half of the 3.5-stop copies (his own brightness < 0.15; the darkest real person read 0.18). Scans use 0.10 on the whole frame (dark clothes on dark sheets). The live ISO/exposure and motion thresholds are guesses until the phone test; every value is logged.
    - **Scan checks on the Mac:** a person is found in 52 of 52 fit photos ("I see a person"); on cropped shoe bands the clothes label is weak (as low as 0.03) but the piece boxes find the shoes and flag them as cut off, so "no clothes" needs both a low label and no pieces. **Real flat-lay photos haven't been tested** (none exist yet); the founder's `inputs/item-photos/` shoot will tune the piece thresholds.
    - **What he sees:** the fit camera's pill says what to fix (too dark + `Turn on flash`, bright light behind you, come closer, hold still in Mirror), spoken in Propped; after the shot the S3 panel (`Retake` / `Retake with flash` / `Choose another` + `Use anyway`, or no `Use anyway` when it can't be sent). Library photos get the same check (on the Simulator, "Choose from your photos" hands the pick back to the camera). The scan camera (Closet "+" → Take photo / Choose photos) has a 3:4 preview, corner brackets per piece (copper when cut off), the standing hint, the same panel, a multi-photo review panel, and `Saved for your closet` + `Reading clothes from photos is coming soon.`
    - **Real on the Simulator:** the after-shot check on the bundled photo (fine; same numbers as the Mac) and on library photos (flowers → "I can't find clothes", a man in a tee → "I see a person"); scan prep and save (face pixelated in the send copy, kept copy as taken). **Mocked:** live camera states (scripted), the drawn flat lay.
102. **Settled in the build (Claude):**
    - **Mirror's tip no longer says "Volume button takes the photo"** (the volume shutter is deferred to v1.1); it reads `Phone at chest height, aimed at the mirror.` when no one's in frame, and the normal tips otherwise. The deck string `cam.tip.mirror` returns with the volume button.
    - **A scan with another sizable person whose face wasn't found is refused** (`There's a person here I can't blur.`), the scan version of ruling 10; the fit check already refuses two people.
    - **Retake uses the deck's `Use anyway`** (not "Use it anyway"); S3's body `This one doesn't count.` on fit checks, `Nothing's saved yet.` on scans.
    - **The S3 panel used to be a toast after the cover closed** (no person, can't frame); now it's said inside the camera while he can retake. Two people still go to S23.
    - **Scans wait in a key-value list (`scan.inbox`)**, not a table, so no migration collides with the closet session's V6+. Each entry: sha, kept/thumb/send paths (relative), time, source, pieces outlined.
    - **Closet "+" (C4) is a system action sheet with Take photo / Choose photos** (deck copy); `Describe it` joins when C4d is built (still a placeholder). The style-council session owns the Closet tab and may restyle it.
    - **The temporary photo of a retaken shot is deleted at once** (prep deletes only photos it uses).
    - **DEV log file:** the `[guide]` lines also go to `Documents/logs/guide.log` (≤ 200 KB, never in Release); `tools/phone/pull.sh` prints its tail and counts saved scans.
103. **Lessons from the guidance build:**
    - **`-demoScreen` was capped at the number of screens, not the highest number,** so screens numbered past a gap were silently ignored (the app just showed the screen below). Fixed: the cap is the highest `n`; the scenarios test now allows gaps but checks order and uniqueness.
    - **`VNClassifyImageRequest` on the Simulator's CPU returned the same labels for two different photos** ("night_sky…" for flowers and a man in a tee); the Mac gives distinct, sensible labels for the same code. Treat Simulator clothes scores as meaningless; the person and piece checks did work there.
    - **The dev build's floating "Tools" button sits over the top-right header button** and takes taps meant for it (drag it away when testing). Not in Release.
    - **JS logs from the Simulator go to whichever Metro the app is attached to** (another session's, in parallel work); writing a small dev log file in Documents and reading it from the Simulator container works for debugging.

94. **The Stylist chat was out of order on his phone; fixed, and the phone's app data was wiped at his request (Oct 8).**
    - **Cause:** boot always started the clock at the demo scenario's "Thu Oct 22, 7:38" (`SCENARIO_NOW`). Every relaunch went back to 7:38, so a check taken after a relaunch got an earlier timestamp than older ones and sorted into the middle of the thread. Every phone message was stamped Oct 22 07:38–08:53.
    - **Fix:** on a physical iPhone (`expo-device` `isDevice`) the clock is real time unless a `-demoNow` is passed. The Simulator and demo screens keep the demo clock.
    - **Scrolling (his request):** a new photo message now always brings the thread to the bottom and follows the reading and then the score card, even if he'd scrolled up (`StylistScreen.tsx`).
    - **Wipe:** done by uninstalling and reinstalling, which deletes the app's container (checks, kept photos, closet, chat). His Photos library is untouched. The Mac copies from `pull.sh` remain in `screenshots/phone/` (git-ignored).
    - **iOS lesson:** removing the last app from a developer also removes the trust in that developer, so he had to re-trust it in Settings → General → VPN & Device Management.
    - **Phone evidence from the check before the wipe (#83 confirmed):**
      - Mirror mode used the rear camera and captured 3024×4032 in 612 ms; prep took 134 ms (upload 768×1024, kept 1536×2048, 1 face region hidden).
      - His grey hoodie + black wide jeans read as **jeans**, scoring 7.0 under v4. The slides read as "other".

## Oct 8 · Closet identity: duplicates, pictures, review (workstream B; numbered from #110 so the measure session's #104+ don't collide)

110. **Diagnosis from the founder's Oct 8 phone pull (`screenshots/phone/20261008-165206`): 9 closet pieces for 5 real ones, all 4 duplicates from hard vetoes on shaky readings.** Real pieces: grey Nike hoodie, black wide jeans, grey boots, cream striped overshirt, tan gingham overshirt. Extra pieces: `Washed black jeans 2` (leg read straight vs wide: a 2-step veto), `Grey tee` (the hoodie under a buttoned shirt, a palm-sized patch read as a tee: type veto), `Grey sweatshirt` (the hoodie under an open shirt: a look-alike, but its points came out −1, so it was added silently), `Grey slides` (the boots read "other" then "slides": type veto). His 3 answers were all handled right; the failures are the questions it didn't ask. Also: every grey piece is drawn with the same family hex (#9CA0A6), so 5 tiles looked identical; Closet tiles can't be tapped and the piece page (C2) is a placeholder, so nothing can be fixed after the card.
    - **Replay harness** (`screenshots/closet-identity/replay/`, git-ignored: his readings + measured L*, today's matcher vs a scratch copy with the proposed rules, answering truthfully): today's rules reproduce the phone exactly (9 pieces, 3 questions). Proposed: **5 pieces, 4 questions, 0 wrong merges.** The 38 identity tests pass on the scratch copy except one, by design ("leg two steps apart" asks instead of `new`).
    - **Crop test** (cut-outs from the measure session, `screenshots/crops/oct8/`): clean for a piece worn alone (hoodie at 16:38 with the Nike mark, jeans, the buttoned gingham shirt); soft but usable for boots; unusable for an inner layer (a strip) and for an open overshirt (two side panels). Hands and phone show on every mirror-selfie top.
    - **Research** (git-ignored `inputs/closet-identity/`: `closet-apps-research.md`, `questions-research.md`, `cutouts-research.md`): every well-reviewed closet app shows the user's own photo; generated images (Google Photos Wardrobe) and stock pictures (Cladwell) draw complaints. No closet app asks "same item?" (Kitted's question is ahead of the field); the review-list pattern comes from Copilot Money and photo face groups. Questions: Horvitz 1999, Amershi et al. 2019 (HAX), Adamczyk & Bailey 2004 / Iqbal & Bailey 2008: ask rarely, at a pause, picture first, skippable, learn from answers. Every public clothing/human-parsing model is non-commercial or can't split a jacket from the top under it; Apple's subject lift treats body + phone as one object.

111. **Proposal to the founder (not built; `screenshots/closet-identity/index.html`, with mockups), ranked:**
    1. **Half-seen sightings never make duplicates** (~1 day): a sighting under an outer layer (measure `overlapOuter ≥ 0.5`) or small (`ownedPx < 4000`) can't be vetoed by subcategory, sleeve, shoe profile, missing logo or missing details; it costs points and asks. A half-seen sighting with a candidate scoring ≥ −4 asks instead of silently adding. Bottoms' leg veto only at 3+ steps (2 steps asks). Footwear `other` = type unknown. Measured L* within 6 → +2, 15+ apart → −2 (−1 half-seen); the ≥ 25 L* veto only for clean sightings. **Answers teach:** a Yes records the differing readings as that piece's field aliases (leg straight/wide stop asking), and once he confirmed a half-seen look-alike top (tee) for a piece, other half-seen top reads of it don't cap.
    2. **His photo on the tile, best sighting wins** (~2 days on top of the measure session's crops): the cleanest crop across sightings, never the latest; a piece never seen clearly gets its drawing filled with a patch of his real fabric; a plain drawing in the measured color last. Needs the founder's look.
    3. **The card's closet row shows his pieces** ("From this photo", 4 tiles marked New / ✓ / ?) with at most one question inline after the score; "Review" a real button (~1 day).
    4. **Closet: a "To confirm" card on top, and every tile opens a piece page** ("Seen in" his photos, Rename, Same as another piece, Not one of these photos, This isn't mine, Picture: your photo / drawing) (~2 days; the first slice of C2).
    5. **Names that tell pieces apart** (½ day): a brand from one clear sighting, strong patterns in the name ("Tan gingham overshirt", "Cream striped overshirt").
    - **Question policy (settled, Claude):** never before the score (except the quick tap); ≤1 on the card, ≤2 per check, ≤6 per rolling week, ≤3 waiting in Closet; none on a check with the quick tap or a score ≤5.5; only merge/split questions, a detail question only when that detail is the one difference between two of his pieces; picture first with a plain reason, "Your score stays the same either way." on the first few; skip keeps both pieces marked maybe-same (never merges); a skipped question returns at most once, with a new photo; 3 skips in a row pause card questions for 7 days.
    - **Not recommended now:** generic detail questions ("Is this hooded?"), generated piece pictures (3–7¢ each, invents hidden parts, new consent copy), parsing models (licenses), SAM 2.1 Tiny (80 MB download; only if plain cut-outs disappoint), Gemini outlines (support unclear, coarse). The Apple feature-print distance is a small bonus for bottoms/shoes only (the measure session: < 0.5 for the same jeans/boots; can't separate his two shirts).
    - **Waiting on the founder (taste):** the crop look (hands/phone on mirror-selfie tops), fabric-filled vs plain drawings, and the question copy. Build order on a go: #1 and #5, then #3 and #4, then #2 after the crop OK.

112. **Split with the measure session (Oct 8, by message).** It owns the native measure layer (sampled color, box ∩ person-mask crops from the face-hidden detail copy with head regions removed, feature prints, crop quality, later flat-lay cut-outs) and the call in `closet/service.ts` that fills `sampled_hex/sampled_l/crop_file/crop_quality`. Workstream B owns `closetIdentity` rules, the review/question UX, names and tiles. Asked of it: a crop quality comparable across checks (for "best sighting wins") and a single-shoe crop.
    - **Lesson:** a second Simulator ("Kitted Closet B", `25DAFF07-…`) loads a copy of the phone's data without touching the shared one: `simctl create` + `simctl install` the DerivedData build, copy `Documents/SQLite` + `Documents/photos` + `Library/Caches/detail` into the app container, and launch with `--initialUrl http://localhost:8081 -demoNow <ISO>` (the dev launcher's own flag); `defaults write com.trykitted.kitted EXDevMenuIsOnboardingFinished -bool true` skips the dev-menu intro. Driving it needs the founder's "Let Claude use it" grant.

104. **Reading accuracy plan: "the AI names and points, the phone measures" (founder-approved Oct 8; this session; split with the accuracy session (#110+, A) and the closet session (#110–112, B) by message).** Ranked: (1) measured color and lightness on the phone; (2) face-hidden close-ups sent with the photo in the same scoring call; (3) lengths/shape from the body outline; (4) image fingerprints for closet matching; later (5) flat-lay cut-outs for scans and (6) before/after comparison for fix detection, once real photos exist. Not doing: Gemini Pro, our own model, DeepFashion-style datasets (non-commercial licenses), more fields in the main prompt (v5 lesson), run-every-check-3-times voting. Gemini 3.x returns no logprobs, so a real confidence number isn't available; the self-reported `certainty` was "clear" on his misread jeans.

105. **Built (Oct 8): close-ups in the scoring call, adopted (`SEND_CLOSE_UPS = true`, prompt version `v3z`).**
    - **What changes:** photo prep (`KittedPrep.swift` → `zoomCrops`) cuts two face-hidden close-ups from the face-hidden 2048 image: his upper body and lower body split at the hips (person box ∪ body joints ∪ person mask, so the feet are included), or one close-up of the whole person when the pose has no hips. They go in the same Gemini call after the upload, labelled "Image 1:" …, with one line appended to the v3 prompt (`extraction-test/prompt_zoom.json`, synced to `assets/gemini/prompt-zoom.json`). The v3 schema and every other setting are unchanged; no close-ups = byte-for-byte the Oct 6 lock. Saved as `Caches/upload/<sha>-upper|lower|body.jpg` so "Try again" resends them (`reloadZoom`). The closet call doesn't get them (closet1 passed without).
    - **Why (`extraction-test/results/zoom1`, `zoom1-stock`; `zoom_test.py`):** see INDEX.md. His boots stopped reading as slides (9 of 21 → 0); tops 80% → 100%; consistency 60/66 → 64/66; mean score error on his photos 0.38 → 0.22. The boot definition line was tested and rejected.
    - **Costs:** 0.39 → 0.57¢ per check (+47%; 0.8 → 1.1¢ from Jan 2027), +0.5 s median. **The cost is the founder's call:** on in dev builds now (only dev builds call Gemini); `SEND_CLOSE_UPS = false` reverts.
    - **Known:** sleeve_end slightly worse (76 → 70%); hem and leg shape stay weak in every arm; scores drift +0.04 to +0.19 on average, mostly from fewer spurious "wrinkled" reads; on the stock before/after pairs that narrows the gains, so fix detection is still unproven and needs real fix photos.

106. **Built (Oct 8): measuring on the phone after the closet call (`KittedMeasure.swift`, `services/measure/garments.ts`).**
    - **What it does:** for each piece the closet call boxed, his pixels = person mask ∩ box, where boxes overlap the smallest box wins (so the hoodie strip inside an open overshirt is the hoodie's), every head region (prep's, grown 5%) removed. The color is measured on those pixels minus his hands and the phone (hand pose) and minus the outer layer's main colors (clusters ≥30% of the outer piece outside this box, within ΔE 12, at most 60% removed), as the largest of 3 Lab clusters. A display cut-out (transparent PNG from the face-hidden detail copy; hands kept; shoes: the clearer single shoe) and Apple's image feature print are written to `Documents/closet/<sha>-garment-<i>.png|.print`.
    - **Wired:** `closetService.matchPieces` measures once per check before the engine runs and stores `sampled_hex`, `sampled_l`, `crop_file`, `crop_quality` on the sighting. Nothing is sent, and no score changes. The engine's existing |ΔL*| ≥ 25 veto now has values (B keeps it for now). Tiles don't change (displayHex isn't fed the sample; tiles are B's). Demo photos, the web and older native builds measure nothing.
    - **On his 4 Oct 8 checks (Mac, the same Swift, `extraction-test/vision`):** the grey hoodie measured L* 28 / 33 / 30 in three different photos (alone, strip, under gingham): steady, while the closet call's words varied ("medium grey"/"heather grey"/"grey"). Indoor light reads everything darker than the swatches, so compare L* between sightings, never against FAMILY_HEX. Feature prints: < 0.5 is a fair "same" signal for bottoms and shoes; 0.5–0.9 overlaps both ways; they don't separate shirts and fail across contexts for layered tops. A small bonus at most.
    - **Leg shape from the outline (shadow only, not wired):** knee width ÷ hip span separates his joggers (0.63–0.67) from his wide jeans (0.75–0.86), but on 50 stock photos slim / straight / wide overlap (0.60–0.77 / 0.63–0.86 / 0.79–1.02). Not good enough to replace the AI; `Measure.legShape` stays for logging.
    - **Needs:** a reinstall on his phone (new native code), then the `[prep] … close-ups …` and `[measure] …` Metro lines. "Delete all my data" (P4/You) must also clear `Documents/closet` and `Caches/upload/<sha>-*.jpg`.

107. **Lessons from the measure build:**
    - **Person segmentation + Vision on the Mac** compile from the app's own files with `extraction-test/vision/build.sh` (`swiftc`, no Xcode project). The first call in a process takes 5–8 s (models load); later ones ~0.3 s per photo for everything.
    - **Removing the outer layer's colors needs its main colors only:** with 15% clusters, a cream shirt's grey shadows matched the grey hoodie and 79% of the hoodie was dropped.
    - **A color mask is not a picture:** holes where hands and shadows were removed look broken in a tile, so the display cut-out uses its own mask (ownership + heads only).
    - **A zoom prompt must match the number of images:** photos without hips get one close-up and the two-image line.

113. **Shoe kinds in the closet (Oct 8; asked by workstream A for its "Boots, sneakers or slides?" tap).** His answer names a kind, never a subtype, so the closet records `userConfirmed.shoeKind` on that sighting (`features/closet/service.ts` `tapConfirmed`, for `tap.kind === 'shoes'`, the kind read from the answered option's `group`) instead of confirming the subtype code picked. `closetIdentity`: footwear subtypes map to boots / sneakers / slides; a kind he named beats the read subtype (same kind → no question; a different kind → a different piece); two subtypes of one kind with nobody saying → ask (−1), not a veto; `other` footwear → type unknown, ask (his boots read "other" then "slides" became 2 pieces on Oct 8). Shoe profile (sleek vs chunky) still splits. 6 new core tests (56 pass); app closet + tap tests pass (41); typecheck clean. This lands the shoe part of proposal #111.1 early; the half-seen rules for tops and bottoms still wait for the founder's go.

## Oct 8 · Reading accuracy and uncertainty (workstream A; numbered from #120 so parallel sessions' entries don't collide)

120. **Measured on the founder's 11 truth-labeled Oct 8 phone checks: the footwear type is the one misread that moves his score.** Truth labels: `inputs/phone-oct8/truth.csv` (37 pieces; the founder confirmed the boots: work boots, faint dark green; Claude had read them as grey too). Readings + truth readings: `rubric/research/reading-accuracy/phone-checks-oct8.json`. Re-scored with rubric.py v4, truth vs as read:
    - **His boots** (shaft hidden under wide jeans) read `other` ×4, `slides` ×1, `low_profile_sneaker` ×1, `work_boot` ×1. Correcting only the boot raises 6 of 11 checks by +0.5 to +1.0. The kind of boot matters too: work boots make the outfit detect as workwear, which waives P1 for wide legs. Colour (grey vs olive) moves 0.
    - **"Swap to relaxed-leg pants" was not a misread:** his jeans read `wide` 6 of 8 times. P1_leg_vs_style fires when no look that allows wide legs is detected, and on his photos that mostly follows from the boot misread (read as work boots, the outfit is workwear and P1 is waived).
    - **"Cream vs tan overshirt" is two different shirts** (a cream pinstripe button-up and a brown gingham), both read right. The real colour flip is the gingham (orange once, tan twice): 0 points.
    - **Hoodie vs sweatshirt: 0 points.** Hoodie read as a tee under a buttoned shirt: +0.5 once. Leg width and hem misreads: 0 points.
    - **Today's tap wasted one question** (a downward "Jeans or sweatpants?" on jeans read right) and never asks about shoes read `other`.
    - **The measure session's zoom A/B on the same photos** (`extraction-test/results/zoom1/`, #105), mean |score − truth| at everyday over 30 reads: today's config 0.38, with close-ups 0.22. Close-ups never read his boots as slides, but they still split work_boot / suede_chukka / derby.
121. **Research (3 agents; reports in `inputs/reading-accuracy/`, git-ignored with all inputs).**
    - **Vision** (`vision-and-side-photo.md`): crops and resolution have strong evidence for small objects (his shoes are ~30 px in the upload). VLM self-reported certainty is poorly calibrated, so a question shouldn't rest on it alone. **A side photo has thin evidence** (body-measurement apps use front + side but measure bodies in tight clothes), and **it wouldn't show a boot shaft under wide jeans either**. No outfit app found asks about a garment or for a second angle.
    - **UX** (`asking-under-uncertainty.md`): ask only about visible facts, never fit (people rarely lie about plain facts but rate themselves favourably on fuzzy ones, Abeler 2019 / Dunning). Ask before the score, at most one question, and keep it rare (target under 15% of checks). Confident-and-wrong costs more trust than "I couldn't tell". Confirm-once-and-remember beats re-asking.
    - **Plan** (Plan agent): one shoe question, upward only; known pieces later.
122. **Decided (Claude, under the founder's Oct 8 "make the decisions" while he slept), ranked by accuracy, friction, speed and cost:**
    1. **Close-ups in the scoring call** (the measure session's #105, built; the +47% cost, 0.39 → 0.57¢ per check, waits on the founder): biggest single gain.
    2. **The shoe question, built (tap v2):** cheap, no extra AI call, and it catches the shoes close-ups still lose.
    3. **"Your green work boots?" for confirmed pieces:** next, after workstream B's `knownPieces` (B owns it). It fixes the work-boot / chukka / derby split that remains under close-ups.
    4. **An "I wasn't sure about your shoes" line on the card**, opening "Not quite right?": later, only if the question proves too rare.
    - **Not doing:** a required shoe photo or side photo (a second photo on every check; thin evidence; doesn't reveal the boot); fit questions ("slim, straight or baggy?"); asking on "likely" sneaker or boot reads (tried at a 1.0 swing: it fired mostly at dressy occasions, "were your sneakers boots?" for +1, inviting gaming and catching none of his misreads); letting the model judge its own confidence.
123. **Built: the shoe question "Boots, sneakers or slides?" (tap v2; rubric.py `ask_question(shoes=True)`, kitted-core `askQuestion`, the app's S5q card).**
    - **When:** footwear read `other`, or `unsure`, or slides that isn't `clear`; shoes visible and framing not upper-only; not at Gym or Home; and the best answer raises the shown score by ≥ 0.5. Upward only.
    - **Answers:** three equal buttons, Boots · Sneakers · Slides. Code picks the type inside a kind: his read type if it's already that kind; sneakers → low-profile; slides → slides; boots → by the rest of the outfit's average dressiness (≤ 2.75 work boots, ≤ 3.25 suede chukkas, else Chelsea boots; all his outfits → work boots). An answered `unsure` piece becomes `likely` (never `clear`), so it's scored but certain-only rules can't start firing.
    - **Skip:** keeps the kind that was read (slides stay slides: a skip never buys points the photo didn't show). Only a lost type (`other`) gets the most generous answer (ties in question order). The pair questions keep the approved generous skip.
    - **Choosing between questions (v2):** a question that could raise the score beats one that could only lower it; then the bigger swing; ties keep the earlier question (the pairs before the shoe question on the same piece).
    - **Re-checks:** `carryTapAnswer` spans `other` plus every type of the asked kinds, so his boots read as slides again stay work boots, unless a fix replaces the shoes.
    - **The closet:** a shoe answer confirms the kind on the sighting, not the type (B's #113).
    - **What he'd see on his Oct 8 photos:** asked on 5 of 11 (b0f0c8, 8bc29d, d6559a, f07193, e026de). Answering Boots lands each on its truth score: f07193 7.0 → 8.0; e026de 7.5 → 8.5, and "Swap the slides for chunky sneakers" goes away. Skip on e026de stays 7.5. d6559a now asks about the shoes instead of the wasted "Jeans or sweatpants?".
    - **Ask rate on the 156 saved stock + mirror readings:** Everyday 3.8% → 3.8%, Date 4.5% → 5.1%, Work 8.3% → 8.3%. With close-ups on his photos, the shoe question fires on 2 of 30 reads.
    - **Proof:** `shoes=False` is v4.0 exactly; check_port passes it, so the frozen prototype check stays 857,520 / 857,520 scorings and 285,840 / 285,840 taps. The parity set was regenerated by make_parity.py, adding 13 shoe edge cases and his 11 phone readings as read and as truth: TypeScript matches 7,233 / 7,233 cases, 847 asking. Before regenerating, every changed `ask` on the old 7,146 cases was reviewed: new shoe questions on lost shoes, plus a few upward-beats-downward swaps. Scores unchanged (no rubric version bump; rubric 4.0.0, engine 4).
    - **Demo screens 60–62:** the question, answered Boots (8.5), skipped (7.5). They use his Oct 8 reading (ph8) on s12's stand-in photo; his photo is never bundled.
    - **Also fixed:** derived readings (tap answers, corrections, merges) were stored under model `demo-bundled` even for real Gemini reads; they now keep the parent's model (commit 38e61d6).
124. **For the founder (taste):**
    - **The tap's hint line:** "I couldn't tell from the photo. It changes your score." The UX research suggests "I want to get this right." instead, because "It changes your score" tells him which answer pays. Unchanged until he says (he approved the current wording in #85).
    - **The pairs' generous Skip:** it gave his real joggers +1.5 twice, because "Jeans or sweatpants?" Skip → jeans. Keep it, or make the pairs' Skip keep what was read, like the shoe question?
    - **Wide jeans with no detected look still lose 0.45** ("Swap to relaxed-leg pants"). The rulebook defers the shared wide-trouser waiver to the gold set.

108. **Close-ups' cost approved (founder, Oct 8).** He took the recommendation: close-ups stay on (`SEND_CLOSE_UPS = true`, #105). The extra is about 0.18¢ per scoring check now (0.39 → 0.57¢), about 0.3¢ from Jan 2027: roughly $0.18 per 100 checks today. The server (Worker) must send the same request when it replaces the dev-only direct call.
125. **Founder, Oct 8: "go with" #122–124's recommendations.**
    - **Close-ups stay on** (already logged by the measure session, #108).
    - **Skip keeps what was read on every question** (tap v2). On "Jeans or sweatpants?" his real joggers read as sweatpants now skip to 5.0, not 6.5. rubric.py `ask_question(shoes=True)` and kitted-core: `default = as read`; `shoes=False` keeps v4.0's generous skip, so check_port is unaffected. Parity regenerated: 7,233 / 7,233, 847 ask. Demo screen 36 now shows 5.0 with `Scored as sweatpants. Not right? Tap.`

114. **Built: `knownPieces` for the reading's "Your green work boots?" question (Oct 8; A #122.3, founder go #125; B owns it, #113 follow-up).**
    - **App** (`features/closet/profile.ts` `knownPieces(items, obs, { excludeCheckIds })`, pure; service `closet.knownPieces(excludeCheckIds)`): owned pieces (not undone, not folded in) that he **confirmed** (a tap answer, a correction, "Yes, same one" / "No, a new one" / "Not the same", a name he set, or he added it himself) **or saw in 2+ checks**. Sightings and answers from the excluded checks (the one being asked about, and a re-check's first check) don't count, so a check can't make its own pieces known. Fields: `{ id, slot, colorFamily, subcategory, shoeKind, shoeKindSaid, name, confirmed, sightings }`, v3 only; shoeKind = his tap answer, else the subtype's kind.
    - **Core** `knownMatches(known, garment)` (exported with `KnownPiece` and `shoeKindOf`): same slot; **shoes by kind only** (unknown is compatible; color only breaks ties, because his green boots read grey); other slots by color family exact or neighbors (grey ↔ charcoal); ties narrow to the closest color, then the same subcategory. Ask only when exactly one comes back.
    - **On his Oct 8 closet as saved:** known = hoodie, jeans, the "jeans 2" duplicate (he answered the jeans tap on it), grey shoes, tan overshirt. Before 16:41: the "sweatshirt" → his grey hoodie, the "slides" → his grey shoes, the jeans → two candidates (no question). The duplicate shows why #111.1 still matters: duplicates make questions ambiguous.
    - Tests: 5 core (`knownMatches`), 4 app (`closetKnown.test.ts`); `npm run check` green (167 core, 282 app).
126. **Built: "Your grey work boots?", the one quick tap about a piece he owns (#122.3; founder approved Oct 8).** Core `askWithCloset(reading, opts, known)` (`kitted-core/src/known.ts`) on B's `knownPieces` + `knownMatches` (#114).
    - **When:** a garment matches exactly one known piece (confirmed, or seen in 2+ earlier checks; this check excluded). Shoes match by kind, other slots by colour family exact or neighbour. The piece's type must differ from the read, the read must be unsure (not `clear`, or `other`), and Yes must move the score by ≥ 0.5. This catches what close-ups still leave: his boots read as a "likely" chukka or derby, which the rubric alone never asks about.
    - **The question:** `Your {piece}?` (his closet's name, first word lower-case) with **Yes** (his piece's type) / **No** (as read), his piece's closet drawing in the score's slot, and the usual hint. **Skip** keeps the read, except a lost shoe (`other`), which skips to his piece: `I'll score them as your grey work boots.`
    - **Agreement drops the question:** when his piece agrees with the read, the rubric's question about that garment is not asked. That removes d918c0's wasted "Chelsea boots or work boots?".
    - **Choosing:** his-piece questions replace the rubric's question on the same garment. Across garments, upward first, then the bigger swing.
    - **Same photo, same score:** a photo scored before without a question is now scored the same way again (`start()`'s cached path). A closet that changed since can't make it ask or score differently. An answered photo already reused its answer.
    - **Rubric parity unaffected:** the closet only picks which question to ask. Every option's score is core `score()` on `applyTapAnswer`, tested against every option on his 11 phone readings.
    - **Closet side (B):** Yes should link the sighting to `knownId`; No should add it to `notItemIds` without confirming a type. Asked of B (its file).
    - **Tests:** core `known.test.ts` (10: 'other' → asks, Yes = truth 8.0; the close-ups' chukka/derby reads ask, a `clear` one doesn't; agreement drops d918c0's question; slides keep the shoe question; two matches → no question; gym/home/hidden shoes → none; scores as promised). App `tap.test.ts` (3: Boots on Oct 21 → "Your grey work boots?" on Oct 22; Yes 8.0; the same photo again isn't re-asked; with no confirmed piece, the shoe question).
    - **Demo screens 63** (asking) **and 64** (Yes): ph8 answered Boots on Oct 21, then ph9 (his f07193 reading, boots read `other`) on Oct 22.
127. **Lesson (Oct 8): 4af1522 committed core `index.ts` with workstream B's two uncommitted export names in it**, so HEAD alone didn't typecheck until B committed `closetIdentity.ts` (bcf1c1f). The phone was never affected (Metro serves the working tree). Claude's clean-worktree check missed it because the worktree's `node_modules` was symlinked to the main folder, so `@kitted/core` resolved to the working tree. From now on: stage only your own hunks of shared files (`git add -p`), and typecheck core directly in the worktree (`npx tsc --noEmit -p app/packages/kitted-core`). B is building the closet side of "Your {piece}?": Yes links the sighting to `knownId`, No adds it to `notItemIds`, Skip does nothing.

115. **Built: steps 1 and 2 of #111 (founder: "You can start", Oct 8). Half-seen pieces ask instead of splitting; a Yes teaches; names tell pieces apart.** Plan reviewed by a planning agent before the app wiring; its findings changed the half-seen rule (below).
    - **Half seen** (`profile.ts` `halfSeen` + `isHalfSeen`, used in `matchPieces` for full checks, re-checks, shoe snaps and corrections): **shoes** = the reading wasn't `clear` on them (every full-body shoe crop is small, so crop quality says nothing); **other pieces** = measured crop quality < 0.5 when the photo was measured (#104: his hoodie strips 0.28 and below, the hoodie alone 0.81), else a top in a photo that also has an outer layer. *Why not the outer-layer rule always:* it flags clearly visible tops (a dress shirt under a suit jacket, demo s09).
    - **Engine** (`closetIdentity`, committed bcf1c1f): a half-seen sighting can't be vetoed by type (−2, ask; a look-alike −1), sleeve (−1), shoe profile (−2), missing logo or details (0), or L* 25+ apart; it asks about its best candidate down to −4 (`PARTIAL_ASK_MIN`) instead of quietly becoming a new piece. All sightings: leg shape 2 steps apart asks (veto at 3+); measured L* within 6 → +1, 15+ apart → −2 (−1 half seen). Still vetoes when half seen: a far color, a different listed brand, his own "No".
    - **A Yes teaches** (core `lessonsOfYes`; `applyAnswer`): the shape reads that differed (leg, sleeve, shoe profile) become the piece's `profile.fieldAliases` and stop costing points; the type alias already existed. A changed answer removes exactly what that Yes added (`ClosetAsk.addedFieldAliases`). Once he confirms a half-seen look-alike top (his hoodie read as a tee), later half-seen top reads of that piece don't ask.
    - **"Your grey work boots?" (A #126) in the closet:** Yes → the sighting links straight to that piece (`answeredSame`, worn +1, no type claimed, the piece kept out of the engine for that check); No → that piece goes in the sighting's `notItemIds`, nothing claimed about the type; Skip → nothing.
    - **Names:** a listed brand seen once beats "no logo" reads (logos hide under layers, hands and the phone), so the hoodie becomes "Grey Nike hoodie"; strong patterns join the name ("Cream striped overshirt", "Tan plaid overshirt"; "plaid" chosen over "check"/"gingham" as the word US guys use, veto-able); `ClosetArt.pattern` (optional, under the drift guard).
    - **Verified:** his Oct 8 checks replayed end to end in core (`test/closetHalfSeen.test.ts`, fixture `test/data/phone-oct8-closet.json`, readings + measures only, no photos): **5 pieces for his 5, 0 wrong merges, questions 0 / 2 / 1 / 1.** App: 4 new service tests (the tee + straight-jeans photo asks twice instead of adding 2 pieces; after his Yes answers the next layered photo matches by itself; Change takes the leg lesson back; "Your … boots?" Yes / No). `npm run check` green (186 core, 289 app). Deliberate test changes: leg 2 steps → ask; `art.pattern`; "plaid" etc. in the fixed name words.
    - **Not done (logged for later):** the half-seen flag isn't stored on sightings (a migration; needed for "best sighting wins" tiles and to keep half-seen type reads out of a piece's type counts; the name uses the item's own type, so names don't drift meanwhile); a correction that moves an answered sighting doesn't take its lesson back; the ≤1 card / ≤2 per check question cap lands with the card row (#111.3), where `matchPieces` would rank asks and keep extra ones quiet.

## Oct 8, 2026 · Style-council session: rubric findings from the founder's phone checks (numbered from #150 to avoid collisions)

150. **Open rubric items from 6 real phone checks** (`screenshots/phone/20261008-220924`, judged against `inputs/phone-oct8/truth.csv` by the "Improving clothing code detection" session). Recorded, not built: session A is editing `extraction-test/rubric.py` (ask_question), so coordinate with it before touching that file. Every change goes rubric.py → parity fixtures → TS.
    1. **Bug, tip only (no score change):** the rule-of-thirds tip "A front tuck or a cropped layer can lengthen your legs." showed on 3 of 6 checks, all with a hoodie (mid_fly). A hoodie can't be front-tucked (ribbed hem band). Fix: show it only for tees, shirts and polos; for a hoodie or sweatshirt, nothing (or "a shorter hoodie"). Claude's call: do it, it's a plain bug.
    2. **Taste call for the founder (needs sources first):** black logo hoodie + black wide jeans + black slides with socks scored 7.0 twice, and the fix both times was "Add a layer → 8". A stylist would say swap the slides first, but v4 never charges slides with jeans (only `F4_hem_drags_over_slides`), so a shoe swap can't be offered. Options: a small slides-outside-Gym/Home charge, or a shoe-swap fix / next-level line with no charge. The style council rejected a standalone slides penalty (RULEBOOK ruling: "slides_drag covers the fit fact"; Highsnobiety calls socks with slides a 2025 go-to), so reopening it needs fresh sourcing. Recommended: a no-points next-level line ("Clean sneakers or your boots would sharpen this"), which keeps the score honest and offers the upgrade.
    3. **Fix variety:** "Add a layer" was the fix on 3 of 6 checks. Good advice he follows, but repetitive. Consider rotating equally good fixes, or preferring the one he hasn't been shown recently (code-only, deterministic per photo).
    4. **Already fixed upstream:** "Swap the slides for chunky sneakers → 8.5" and "Swap to relaxed-leg pants" both came from his boots being misread; close-ups (#105) and A's shoe question (#120–124) fix the read.
    5. "Steam out the wrinkles" on the cream overshirt: plausible, can't be judged from the photo.
128. **Founder, Oct 8: a to-do list for him in CLAUDE.md, and reminders.** New section "My to-do list" near the top: things only he can do (phone test, photos, accounts) and quick taste calls, each with what it unblocks. New rule 6 in "How we keep context": every chat adds what it needs from him to that list, checks items off when they're done, and now and then (not every message) ends a result with a one-line reminder of the 1–3 items that unblock the most. "Waiting on the founder" keeps the details.

## Oct 8 · Closet pictures under layers (research session; numbered from #140 so parallel sessions' entries don't collide)

140. **Research + prototypes: how closet tiles should look when pieces are layered (4 agents; nothing in `app/` changed).** Reports: `inputs/closet-pictures/generative-research.md`, `inputs/closet-pictures/apps-and-users-research.md` (with a Codex Reddit prompt, §7); prototypes on his Oct 8 photos: `screenshots/closet-pictures/index.html` (cut-out, best sighting, spotlight crop, fabric fill, hands removed) and `screenshots/closet-graphics/index.html` (new parametric drawings, `flats.js`).
    - **Founder's idea (Oct 8): ask whenever a sighting looks like a piece he owns.** Adopted as the core, as "ask until it learns": a Yes links the half-seen sighting to the piece, so the tile uses that piece's best photo (the strip under an open shirt shows his clear Nike hoodie from the day he wore it alone). Answers teach aliases so the rate falls (asking every turn reads as "imbalanced and annoying", Amershi et al. 2014; Cakmak et al. 2010). Workstream B already landed the matcher half (#115); the picture half is told to B.
    - **Ranked picture plan:** (1) his own photo, best sighting wins, linked by answers; (2) new parametric drawings as the fallback and for pieces never seen clearly (they replace today's 60×60 icons: one outline per top, family hex, no shading); about 3.5 days for his 5 pieces + 4 common types, 7–8 for every type, $0; (3) spotlight crop (piece bright, rest dimmed) for "Seen in" on the piece page, not the tile; (4) a rare "Snap it on its own?" only for a piece that blocks outfits.
    - **Bugs found:** mirror-selfie crops show logos backwards (flip crops from Mirror shots); the head-region removal blanks a rectangle that bites into collars and shoulders (frame top tiles below the head region instead).
    - **Not doing in v1:** AI-generated product pictures (Nano Banana 2 Lite now 3.4¢; consent is one line since the closet call already sends the same photo; but they invent hidden parts, exactly where layers need help, and competitors drew complaints: Google Photos Wardrobe and Layered added Redo buttons). Possible v1.1: opt-in, Pro-only, clear sightings only, labelled. Research try-off models (TryOffDiff etc.): non-commercial and server-only. Fabric patches inside drawings: fine for solids, worse than drawn patterns for prints. FUPstudio flats bundle ($24.95, license unchecked) and commissioning (~$750): not needed.
    - **Waiting on the founder (taste):** whether the new drawings look right. An optional $0.82 Gemini hand/phone clean-up test is written up in the generative report; not recommended now.

109. **Close-ups and measuring confirmed on his iPhone; mirror-selfie cut-outs flipped (Oct 8, night).**
    - **Phone run (pull `screenshots/phone/20261008-220924`):** two checks in a black Nike hoodie, black wide jeans and black slides (one mirror, one propped front-camera shot). Prep 237–254 ms with close-ups (upper 544×976, lower 544×932; 105–148 ms of it). Gemini 6.6 s, and 11.3 s once (hedged) during a flaky hour. Closet call about 4 s, then measuring 1.0–1.5 s (a Debug build). Read right: hoodie, slides "clear"; the bottoms asked "Jeans or sweatpants?" once (he answered jeans). Measured L*: hoodie 5.7 / 2.5, jeans 1.5 / 1.0, slides 3.9 / 2.2; crop quality 0.84–0.92 for hoodie and jeans. The propped front-camera hoodie cut-out is clean (no hand, no phone).
    - **First failure explained:** the 19:10 check failed because Gemini hung for a while (even a one-word request timed out from the Mac), and the dev server had been stopped. Prep had worked.
    - **Mirror flip (found by the closet session):** a mirror selfie's photo is the reflection, so logos read backwards on its cut-outs. When the scoring reading says `scene.mirror_selfie` (true on all 180 of his mirror reads, false on his propped one), the cut-out and its print are flipped back to true view at write time (`measureGarments(…, mirrored)`). The closet call's logo text was already read right ("Nike swoosh"), so it's unchanged.
    - **Fashion review of his checks** went to the style-council session (#150): the "front tuck" tip shows on hoodies (a bug), slides are never offered as the fix (a taste call), and "Add a layer" repeats.

116. **An unanswered question is never asked twice (Oct 8; from his 22:01 phone checks, pull 20261008-220924).** His two new checks added a black Nike hoodie and black Adidas slides correctly, but the jeans asked twice. The old "Washed black jeans 2" duplicate made every jeans sighting a two-way tie, and the second check's jeans then also counted the first check's still-unanswered copy as a candidate. The grid showed each open copy as a tile ("Washed black jeans 3", "Black jeans"): 4 black-jeans entries.
    - **Fix** (`profile.ts` `isAwaitingAnswer`): a piece created while unsure, whose candidate is still his, **isn't matched as a piece of its own**. A later sighting that would ask about the same candidate **joins that open question** (a sighting on the copy, no new piece, no new question; the card counts it as already in the closet). **One Yes moves every joined sighting** to his piece (worn +N, the other checks' cards follow, `ClosetAsk.riders`); a changed answer moves them back. Awaiting pieces **aren't tiles** (they show in their question card on top of Closet) and **aren't "known"** (`knownPieces`).
    - Still true: his existing duplicates (jeans 2, Grey tee, Grey sweatshirt, Grey slides from the old rules) keep jeans sightings asking until he answers or merges them. Merging needs the piece page (#111.4).
    - Test: `closetService.test.ts` "an unanswered question is never asked twice". `npm run check` green in a clean worktree with only these changes staged (186 core, 290 app). The measure session's mirror-flip hunks in the same two files were left out for it to commit (#109, cf200cf).

141. **Founder, Oct 8: code-drawn tiles, not his photos, wherever pieces are shown.** Seeing his pieces both ways (`screenshots/closet-graphics/new-drawings-row.png` vs `screenshots/closet-pictures/review/g-be.png`), he called the new drawings "pretty nice" and the cut-outs "ugly", and said outfits recommended from cut-outs would look bad. The drawings are made by code from the closet call's details (not AI-generated: free, the same every time, nothing invented, nothing sent).
    - **Where each goes:** the new parametric drawings (`screenshots/closet-graphics/flats.js`) on every Closet tile, outfit, standing outfit and morning-card tile. His photos only as evidence: the spotlight crop on "Same as your …?" questions and the piece page's "Seen in" (the full photo, not a cut-out). Cut-outs stay internal (measured color, fingerprints) and are never shown as tiles.
    - **This reverses #111 call 1 (photo on the tile) and the #140 default; #111 call 2 becomes the plain new drawing** (fabric patches looked worse on prints).
    - **Claude's honest caveat, accepted:** a drawing shows "a hoodie like his", and look-alikes (two grey hoodies) can come out near-identical.
