Mitigations, in the build: draw in the measured color (`sampled_hex`, kept inside the family band) rather than the family hex; draw the details that tell pieces apart (zip vs pullover, the brand as plain text, wash, pattern); the name under the tile carries the rest; the piece page shows his real photos.
    - **Build:** owned by workstream B (tiles). About 3.5 days for his 5 pieces + 4 common types, 7–8 days for every type the closet call returns; react-native-svg `SvgXml` takes the generator's output.

142. **Founder asked (Oct 8): should every check take a side photo too, to tell baggy fits apart and because the side view is part of an outfit? Claude: not by default; keeps A's #121 ruling, with a test before any change.**
    - **For it:** the side shows real style things the front can't: how the pants stack, jacket length over the seat, a tuck or a blouse at the waist, chunky vs sleek shoes. In 3D reconstruction, extra views cut shape error by about 65%, most for loose clothes (MExECON; `inputs/reading-accuracy/vision-and-side-photo.md` §3).
    - **Against a default side photo:** (1) the misread that moved his scores was the boots, which close-ups (#105) and the shoe question (#120) already fix, while his wide jeans read right from the front 6 of 8 times; (2) a side profile shows the stomach and body shape far more than the front, which pulls toward "never rate bodies" and body-image harm (the JMR 2023 try-on finding, #84); (3) it doubles the effort of every check, with no completion data (ZOZOSUIT: many never finished measuring); (4) the rubric has no side-view rules, and adding readings already failed the consistency bar (v5, #63–64); (5) body-measurement apps use a side view to measure bodies in tight clothes, not to read garments.
    - **Instead:** an optional quarter turn inside the same Propped 3-2-1 ("Turn to your side"), offered only when the pants' shape or stack would move the score and the reading is unsure, and never in Mirror. Build it only if a test shows it helps: 5–10 front + side pairs from the phone test, read with and without the side view through `extraction-test` (about $0.10).

143. **Built (Oct 8): code-drawn tiles everywhere pieces are shown (founder's go on #141: "if this is free, this is awesome… we should go this route"; he asked this chat to build it).**
    - **What he sees:** each piece drawn as a shaded "technical flat" instead of the 60 × 60 icon: Closet grid and its question card, the morning card's hero strip, outfit-idea and outfit tiles, "Which shoes?", the partial check's remembered-shoes tile, "Not quite right?" rows, the Added sheet and the scan camera's Simulator flat lay. Light and dark (black pieces get a rim light on night). Screenshots: `screenshots/drawn-tiles/` (git-ignored).
    - **Code:** `features/closet/flats.ts` (the generator: an SVG string per piece, 200 × 200, only elements SvgXml supports; ported from the prototype and extended to tees, polos, tanks, crews, quarter-zips, cardigans, hoodies incl. full zip, shirts, overshirts, denim jackets, bombers, zip jackets, puffers, blazers, coats; jeans, chinos, trousers, cargos, joggers, shorts; sneakers, runners, work boots, chukkas, Chelsea boots, loafers, derbies, slides). `artSpec.ts` maps the reading + closet art to it (volume → fit, leg shape, hem → break, denim wear, details, pattern, heather shade, chunky sole, the brand word). `GarmentArt` renders it with SvgXml, memoized by content; `PieceArt` draws any {garment, hex, art}. A v3-only piece draws its type's usual details. `ClosetTile`'s default drawing share is 0.8 (the flats carry their own margin); its unused `large` prop is gone.
    - **Unchanged:** scores, matching, names, the closet data. The tiny 24 pt icons in `CardParts` stay GarmentIcon.
    - **Not done yet:** the measured color (`sampled_hex`, clamped to the family band by core `displayHex`) isn't fed to the item's tile hex; that's a closet-service change for workstream B. Brand words are plain text; a logo shape is never drawn.
    - **Tests:** artSpec rewritten (every subcategory renders in both themes with no NaN/undefined, deterministic, text escaped; the founder's hoodie carries NIKE). `npm run check` green (292 app tests with the parallel session's work in the tree); the staged change typechecks alone in a clean worktree.

117. **Built: the closet question card, asked on the score card right after the rating and in Closet's "To confirm" (founder, Oct 8: "ask them both in the Closet tab and in the Stylist tab … light mode and dark mode … make sure everything is visible"). His ask to build it is taken as the go on #111's question wording (founder may still change any word).**
    - **The card** (`features/closet/DuplicateQuestion.tsx`, used in three places: the score card, Closet's "To confirm", and the S11 sheet behind Review): "Is this your grey Nike hoodie?" (pants and shoes: "Are these your khaki chinos?"); one plain reason line; "Your score stays the same either way." on his first 3 answered questions; **This photo** = his photo cropped to the piece's box, outlined in the accent with the rest dimmed (`SightingCrop` + pure `cropLayout`); **In your closet** = that piece's best earlier photo (cleanest measured crop, else biggest box), else its drawing; Yes, same one · No, a new one · It's my … (unchanged). Answered: one line + Change (unchanged).
    - **No face, ever:** the crop shows only the piece's region, scaled to cover the frame with nothing above the box's top edge (3% inset); tested on his 4 Oct 8 boxes × 3 frame sizes (`closetQuestion.test.ts`). It uses the kept photo he already sees in the chat; the dim overlay is the same in both themes (it sits on a photo).
    - **The reason** is chosen when the question is made (`service.ts` `askReason` → `ClosetAsk.reason/reasonWord`): half seen under a layer ("I could only see part of it under your overshirt."), small shoes, a hidden or different logo, a different fit, a look-alike type ("It looked like a sweatshirt in this photo."), a near color, else "They look a lot alike." Older questions without a reason show the last line.
    - **Score card:** the check's first question sits under "Added 2 to Closet · 1 question" once the closet call is done, with smaller pictures (120 pt) so the card stays short; more questions show "1 more in Closet". **Closet:** one "To confirm · N" card on top holding every open question (was one card each, tagged "A quick question").
    - **Verified on the Simulator, light and dark:** demo screens 38 (Closet), 70 (new: the score card with "Are these your khaki chinos?") and 37 (the sheet). Copy is in `en.ts` → `addedInBuild` ("closet.q.*", "closet.confirm.tag"). Differs from the mockup/02 S11: "Same as your …?" → "Is this your …?" / "Are these your …?" (founder's wording, #111). `npm run check` green (186 core, 306 app).
    - **Not yet:** the question cap (≤2 per check; the replays never exceed 2), tapping a tile for the piece page (merge / rename / "This isn't mine", #111.4), and the measured color in the tile drawings (follow-up from the tiles session, #143).

## Oct 9 · Layer-bias audit (founder: "it always tells me to put a layer on"; numbered from #160)

160. **Audit: clean outfits get "Add a layer" or nothing. Recorded, not built.** Data: his 11 phone checks (`screenshots/phone/20261009-114901`, pulled Oct 9) plus 191 unique saved readings (zoom1, zoom1-stock), run through `kitted_logic.suggest_fixes` with an empty closet. Script: session scratchpad (`audit.py`, `scen.py`); easy to recreate.
    - **The number:** of 90 clean outfits (no rule fired), the 45 without a layer credit got "Add a layer" as their **only** fix (45/45), and the 45 with one got **no fix at all** (45/45). Scores split on the same line: no layer 7.0–7.5 (41/45), layer 8.0–9.0 (41/45). On his phone, every outfit without a jacket got "Add a layer" (4 of 4 such photos: grey hoodie + boots, the black Nike + slides twice, Oct 9's green crewneck).
    - **Why (code):** (1) K2 elevated piece and K4 shoes have only `swapFromCloset` options in `fixes.json`, so with a thin or misread closet they never appear; K1 is the only credit with a generic "Free if you own one" option. (2) K1 is the biggest credit (0.75 vs 0.5), so it would win on gain ÷ effort anyway. (3) Rounding shows it as +1.0 (7.0 → 7.75 → 8.0; 7.5 → 8.25 → 8.5) while the others show +0.5.
    - **The copy is one-size:** the edit always adds a navy overshirt, and the line is "an overshirt or a knit" whatever is underneath. Over a hoodie it reads as "put a button-up on" (his words), and a knit over a hoodie or sweatshirt makes no sense. No weather or season check: a white tee + chino shorts + white sneakers gets "Add a layer → 8.5" in July.
    - **The score bias behind it (rubric.py, needs the gold set):** a knit sweater worn alone counts as "Layered" (+0.75) while the same crewneck in sweatshirt fabric gets nothing (green sweater + wide jeans + white leather 8.0 vs green sweatshirt 7.5). A clean hoodie + wide jeans + chunky sneakers tops out at 7.5 without a layer (8.0 with a standout color), while oxford + chinos + loafers starts at 8.0. The 0.75 value has no source in our research (style-council notes call the point values "my own judgment"). Smaller: a muted color other than burgundy/sage never counts as a point of interest (his green crewneck read `green` + `muted`, no K3).
    - **Recommended fix, in order (code-only, no score change, in `fixes.json` + `kitted_logic.py`, then TS `fixes.ts` and regenerated fixtures):** (a) generic "Free if you own one" options for K4 (shoes that earn it for this outfit: chunky sneakers or boots under wide jeans, leather sneakers or loafers with chinos) and K2 (swap the top for a knit or a collared shirt), so a clean card can show 2–3 different moves; (b) count "add a whole piece" as effort 3, so an equal-value swap leads (his slides outfit would lead with the shoes, as a stylist would); (c) a layer line that fits what's under it: over a hoodie or sweatshirt "Add a jacket: denim, bomber or chore coat" (modeled as that piece, never a knit or a button-up); over a tee or shirt "an overshirt or a knit"; (d) hide "Add a layer" when it's warm (feels-like ≥ 75°F from the weather band, else the season). Score changes (knit-alone as Layered, 0.75 → 0.5, muted accents) wait for the gold set.
    - **His fits, rated by Claude against the app:** 16:37 cream shirt over hoodie 7.5 (agree; "steam the wrinkles" plausible) · 16:38 grey hoodie + boots 7.0 (Claude 7.5; the boots were misread, so the shoe credit was lost; "add a layer" valid but not the most useful) · 16:40 gingham buttoned over hoodie 7.0 (Claude 6.5–7.0; "push sleeves up" valid, "relaxed-leg pants" invalid: misread) · 16:41 gingham open 7.5 (Claude 7.5–8.0; "swap the slides" invalid: boots misread) · 22:01 black Nike + slides with socks at Class 7.0 twice (Claude 6.5; "add a layer" is the wrong fix, the slides are the weak point, #150.2) · 22:52 olive fleece + gingham 7.5, no fix (Claude 7.5; "no fix" is this audit's second half) · Oct 9 green crewneck + wide jeans + white sneakers 7.5 (Claude 8.0; the green is a real accent; "add a layer" optional at best) · Oct 9 friend's navy chore coat + white tee + black jeans + low sneakers 8.5 twice (Claude 8.0–8.5; consistent across both shots).

118. **Built: removing a piece from Closet and a look from Lookbook, by long-press (founder, Oct 9: "the ability to remove clothes … do the same for the lookbook … maybe the person is shopping … and they want to remove a fit").**
    - **Closet:** long-press a tile → an iOS action sheet titled with the piece's name: **Remove from Closet** (red) · Cancel → the toast `Removed your grey Nike hoodie.` with **Undo**. The piece leaves the grid, the morning card's picks and matching (`isDeleted`, sightings kept so Undo restores it whole); his scores never change. If he wears it again it's added back like any new piece.
    - **Lookbook:** long-press a look → `Remove from Lookbook`, plus `Remove look and the new piece` / `… its {n} new pieces` when the look added pieces no other check has seen (a fitting-room try-on) → `Removed from Lookbook.` / `Removed the look and 1 piece.` with **Undo**. A removed look (and its re-checks) hides from the Lookbook (`details.removedFromLookbook`) and stops counting as a wear of its pieces; its chat card stays (the message says so), and it still used that day's free check. Undo puts back exactly what changed (`LookRemoval`: wears per piece, removed pieces).
    - **Settled (Claude, HIG):** an action sheet, not a confirm alert, with Undo after (destructive but reversible; one extra tap is enough); VoiceOver gets the same as a custom action ("Remove from Closet" / "Remove from Lookbook"), since long-press isn't discoverable with VoiceOver; no new dependency (ActionSheetIOS, already used by the Closet "+").
    - Tests: 3 service tests (remove + Undo, a look with and without its new pieces, wear counts). `npm run check` green (186 core, 309 app). The long-press itself isn't driven on the Simulator yet: the agent tool needs the founder's one-time "Let Claude use it" grant; the grid and Lookbook render unchanged.
161. **Rubric v5 goal: every point is earned, and every point has a reason (founder, Oct 9: "the rating is our selling point… this has to be meticulous"; "just because a person has a layer on, it doesn't have to be an 8.5").** Plan and prompts: `rubric/research/v5-brief.md`. Claude's calls: (1) credits move from presence to quality (a layer earns when it adds contrast or ties to another piece, fits the style, and sits right), decided on evidence, not taste; (2) accessories join through one "finished" credit inside the existing cap, from big visible pieces only, never a penalty for having none (extends the Oct 8 accessories plan); (3) **glasses, sunglasses, earrings and anything on the head are never scored**: the upload hides the whole head (#54), and glasses are mostly prescription and sit on the face; (4) people's ratings set the level: Reddit, TikTok and YouTube ratings feed rules and a "paper calibration" (readings written by hand from the descriptions), the gold set sets the numbers; popularity is never a signal; (5) every new reading passes a 3-run flip test on the real photos first (#63–64 lesson); (6) no score change without people's ratings: if the gold set slips, new rules ship as tips. Research runs by the founder: R1 Codex Reddit, R2 Codex TikTok, R3 Gemini YouTube (text only, no images saved, 2024+, men 18+, nothing that rates faces or bodies).

144. **Founder agreed (Oct 8) to #142: no side photo.** "We can go with the less complicated version." The optional quarter-turn test (about $0.10) isn't scheduled; revisit only if the gold set shows leg-shape misreads moving scores. The Codex Reddit prompt in `inputs/closet-pictures/apps-and-users-research.md` §7 is optional now that the tiles are decided (#141).

119. **Verified on the Simulator (founder granted access Oct 9): long-press removal, and two fixes it surfaced.**
    - **Seen working:** long-press a Closet tile → the action sheet (the piece's name, red "Remove from Closet", Cancel) → the piece leaves the grid → `Removed your olive overshirt.` with Undo → Undo puts it back where it was (checked in the Simulator's database too). Lookbook removal is covered by the service tests; its sheet wasn't driven on screen (another session relaunched the shared Simulator mid-test).
    - **Bug fixed: only the first toast of a session ever showed** (every Undo toast in the app: Closet removal, "Logged for today.", corrections). `ToastHost` unmounted its whole container when a toast ended; with the capsule's exiting animation that left every later capsule invisible on iOS. The container now stays mounted and only the capsule comes and goes. Seen: the second and third removal toasts in one session now show.
    - **Removal toasts stay 8 s (16 s with VoiceOver), others 5 s** (`showToast(…, { ms })`, `TOAST_MS_REMOVAL`): Undo is the only way back from a removal, and 5 s proved short in testing.
    - **Simulator lesson:** the agent's taps on an iOS 26 action sheet are often swallowed the first time (the sheet is still settling); tap again, and wait ~1.5 s after it appears.
162. **#160's code-only fix built (founder, Oct 9: "you can start"). Scores unchanged; only which fixes show and their order.** In `fixes.json` + `kitted_logic.py` (reference), ported to core `fixes.ts`, fixtures regenerated (`make_expected.py`, `make_rubric_json.py`, `gen:rubric`):
    - **Shoe swaps for the shoe credit without a closet item:** chunky sneakers, boots, low-profile sneakers, white leather sneakers, loafers ("Swap the {name} for …"). Never boots with shorts or on a warm day; never one everyday sneaker for another (low-profile / white leather / canvas / retro runners). On a tie the swap that moves the shoes' dressiness least wins (loafers → white leather sneakers, not boots).
    - **Adding a whole piece costs effort 3** (the generic layer and "Add your {item}"), so an equal-value swap leads; cards now list fixes in rank order (free first), not by rounded gain; the first check's fallback fix is the best-ranked one, not the first target's.
    - **The layer line fits what's underneath:** over a hoodie or sweatshirt "Add a jacket over it: denim, bomber or chore coat" (scored as a navy bomber); over anything else "Add a layer: an overshirt or a knit" (navy overshirt, as before).
    - **Warm days:** the app passes `warm` (the check's card weather band "none", else May–September by the month) and gets no layer and no boots fix; stored as `details.warm` so "Not quite right?" uses the same. The score never changes.
    - **Not done, on purpose:** no generic swap for the "elevated piece" credit. A knit worn alone counts as "Layered" today, so "swap the hoodie for a sweater" would be the same layer bonus by another door; it waits for the v5 ledger (#161). The closet's "Add your {item}" still offers an overshirt over a hoodie when that's what he owns (he wears that combo himself).
    - **On his phone readings (class, October):** black Nike hoodie + slides → "Swap the slides for chunky sneakers → 7.5", then "Add a jacket over it → 8.0"; grey hoodie + boots and the green crewneck → "Add a jacket over it → 8.0"; fleece + gingham → "Swap the chukkas for boots" (his boots misread); the friend's chore coat → nothing. In July the hoodie and crewneck cards show no fix. Tests: core `fixBias.test.ts` (60 cases from his readings + made-up outfits, matched to the Python), app `fitCheckService` "warm days". `npm run check` green (249 core, 311 app).
    - **Rubric flaws this surfaced (for the v5 ledger):** the shoe credit needs dressiness 2.5+, so canvas, retro runners and chunky sneakers never earn it except chunky under loose denim, and white leather sneakers count as too dressy for a tee and shorts.
163. **R1–R3 results checked (Oct 9; founder: "take these with a grain of salt… Codex could have made a mistake"). Report: `rubric/research/notes/v5-r1-r2-check.md`.** Codex Reddit: 16 of 120 rows (Reddit blocked), its accessory and headwear columns are keyword-match errors ("hat" on all 16 rows), mostly tailoring; themes only. Codex TikTok: 20 real ratings from 1 small creator (2 posts checked on TikTok's embed endpoint). **Gemini fabricated its file** (72 rows recombined from 12 descriptions and 12 reasons by a script, fake 10-digit IDs, saved outside Kitted); rejected, and its YouTube task (R3) never ran. **Paper test of v4 on the 20 real ratings: rank correlation −0.02; the creator spread 2–9, v4 put all 20 at 6.5–7.5.** The rubric can't see clutter, "basic", intentional stacked hems or coordinated bold color. Claude's calls: add a **spread** pass bar to the gold set (our scores' standard deviation at least 70% of the friends'), reorder the v5 ledger (layer contrast, color tie-in, clutter, stacked hems, finished credit; "basic" level waits for the gold set), and re-run research with honesty rules (R3b Gemini YouTube, R2b Codex more TikTok creators; prompts in `v5-brief.md`). No score change yet.
164. **Fit, piece by piece, joins v5 (founder, Oct 9: tell fitted, oversized and tight apart, judge it, and log it in the closet). Plan: `rubric/research/v5-brief.md` → "Fit, piece by piece".** Measured first: today's `volume` hugs the middle (his photos: 91 of 117 top reads = 3, never 5), and one number mixes the cut with the size, so v4 can't tell "oversized on purpose" from "a size too big". Claude's calls: (1) replace `volume` with `cut` (fitted/regular/relaxed/oversized) + `size_fit` (too_tight/right/too_big/unsure) + one garment-only `fit_cue`, replacing rather than adding fields (#63–64); (2) the phone measures **positions only** from pose joints (sleeve vs. wrist, top hem vs. hips, pant hem vs. ankle and shoe top); **never a garment width against the body**, because it scales with body size and would rate bodies; (3) size faults apply to every style, an oversized cut that fits is judged only by proportion; fix copy names the garment, never the body, with a banned-words copy test; (4) the closet stores cut and size_fit per piece (the clearest sighting wins) for matching, tiles, the piece page, fixes and the morning card; (5) truth from a size-ladder shoot (the same pieces in 2–3 sizes); bars: 90% same across runs, 80% accurate, else tip only. Fit research running (`notes/v5-fit.md`); optional R3c Gemini prompt for YouTube fit guides.
165. **Fit research in (`rubric/research/notes/v5-fit.md`, 22-row table; spot-checked RMRS and FashionBeans, two small corrections noted in the file); #164 revised.** Findings: the shoulder seam, sleeve end and hem carry every top's fit call (3–7 sources each); oversized on purpose = seam just past the shoulder with a wide but short (often ribbed) body, too big = seam halfway down the arm, long body, sleeves over the hands (5 sources on short body, 2 dissent); "one loose piece, one controlled piece" (4 sources), and both loose works when the top is short; skinny (not slim) under an oversized top reads off; extreme pooling is cooling (FashionBeans Sep 2026); no benchmark shows VLMs can judge fit. **Claude's change to #164:** don't ask the model for `cut`/`size_fit`; it reads cues (new `shoulder_seam` on_edge/just_past/down_arm, new `ribbed_hem`, `sleeve_end` gains mid_bicep/at_elbow, sleeves read on the hanging arm) and code derives cut and size. Phone positions as fractions of his own limb segments with a joint-confidence gate; `Measure.legShape` (a width probe, Mac harness only) stays out of the app. For the v5 ledger: v4's oversized-over-narrow-leg charge should key on skinny, not slim.
166. **R3b/R2b re-runs checked; online collection ends (Oct 9).** Gemini's YouTube file is real this time (4 videos verified by oEmbed, dates and timestamps consistent; quotes not word-checked): 14 tier verdicts from Brandon Balfour, 13 rules mostly from Fly With Johnny Thai. Codex couldn't load TikTok and added nothing. New ledger candidates: chain with tailoring is a miss (now 2 sources, so any accessory credit depends on the style), too-slim pants, branded high socks + chunky sneakers under a smart top as a formality clash v4 misses. Dissent logged: two creators say an outfit is incomplete without jewelry; Kitted keeps "no accessories never costs points" (6 editorial sources say restraint, and small jewelry isn't visible). Claude's call: stop chasing online ratings (bot blocks, few real rated fits); the gold set is the calibration. Don't widen the date range to 2021–2023 (skinny-jean era). Next: the v5 rules ledger.
167. **Instagram + Pinterest as a source (founder idea, Oct 9).** Accepted for three uses: a top-end check (curated good fits should mostly score 8+), base rates per style (layers, accessories, tie-ins, standout pieces, stacked hems), and checking "Reads as" against the account's own style. Not for the low end or the level. Claude's calls: Codex, not Gemini (Gemini fabricated once, #163); text descriptions in Kitted's garment words, no images saved (copyright; others' faces never sent to Google); Pinterest pins only when they trace to a real person's or brand's post (AI-generated outfit images are common there); slow, one tab, ~6 posts per account to protect his logged-in account; popularity never counts. Prompt: `rubric/research/v5-brief.md` → R5.

## Oct 9 · The fit ruler (measure session; numbered from #170 to avoid collisions)

170. **Fit ruler tested: no measurement replaces the AI's field (founder's brief, Oct 9; report `rubric/research/notes/fit-ruler.md`).** Built in the Mac harness only (`extraction-test/vision/FitRuler.swift`, runner `fit_ruler.py`, results `results/ruler1`): from Apple Vision's body pose and outline, walks the hanging arm, the front of the torso and each shin, and finds where the garment's own color stops (positions as fractions of his own limbs; never a width against the body; no skin color; lightness half-weighted; a joint-confidence gate; "unsure" otherwise). Tested on 72 photos against Claude's labels made before the run (`extraction-test/ruler_labels.csv`) and the AI's zoom1 arm B readings; tuned on stock + mirror only, his photos held out. Bar: beat the AI and unsure ≤ 20%.
    - **Sleeve end:** 79% when sure vs. the AI's 93% on the same photos; unsure 42% (60% on his photos: his hanging hand is usually in a pocket). Fails.
    - **Top length:** ties overall (63% vs. 64%); on his photos 71% vs. the AI's 43% (the AI reads his at-the-belt hoodies as mid fly), but unsure 30%. Fails the unsure bar; swapping it into his 37 saved readings changes **no score** and the front-tuck tip on 7 runs (13 → 12 net). The candidate to re-test on the gold set.
    - **Pant hem (gap / meets / covers):** 52% vs. 81%, unsure 54%. A break is a fold, not a position; dark pants on dark shoes have no step. Fails.
    - **Layers / shorts:** untestable here (every readable layered photo was a "no"; 3 shorts photos). Box-only variants (the closet call's boxes scaled to his joints) were below the AI too (top 65% vs. 69%, hem 63% vs. 77%).
    - **Sameness:** the same photo gives the same answer (100%); resized to 1600 px or cropped 3%, 86–98%. The AI over 3 runs: sleeve 97%, top 74%, hem 85%.
    - **Claude's calls:** (1) keep the AI's `sleeve_end`, `length`, `hem` for v1; the ruler stays in the harness as the baseline; (2) the v5 fit plan (#164–165) no longer assumes the phone replaces those fields; the AI-reads-cues, code-derives-fit path stays; (3) re-run `fit_ruler.py` on the gold set and the size ladder, with labels for a sleeve over the hand, an under-layer below a jacket, shorts at and below the knee, and a range of skin tones; (4) if top length then passes, a shadow week on his phone first (proposal in the report), then the rubric chat decides the swap; (5) `Measure.legShape` (knee width over hip span, never called by the app) moved out of `KittedMeasure.swift` into `extraction-test/vision/LegShapeProbe.swift`, so a body-width probe can't reach the app.
    - **Skin tones:** the ruler answered the sleeve on 75% of light, 50% of medium and 45% of deep-skinned photos (15 of the 29 deep are his own, with pockets and dark outfits). The sample can't separate the causes; the gold set must balance skin tones and show equal answer and accuracy rates before anything like this ships.
171. **Lessons from the fit ruler.**
    - **Vision's arm line often runs along the sleeve's edge,** so a band on it takes in the background (the black mirror frame on d6559a read as the sleeve ending at the bicep). Center each sample on the run of his outline it sits in (never report that run's width), and shrink the person mask 2 px.
    - **Shading moves lightness, not color:** weight lightness half (CIE94 textile kL = 2) on a sleeve walk, and require a step to be sharp (the bands just before and after it differ) and to stay gone (no return to the garment's color within 0.4 of a limb), so a print or a fold isn't an end. Keep full lightness for top vs. pants (grey hoodie over black jeans differ mostly in lightness).
    - **Don't find the shoe by matching a color:** most sneakers have white soles and two-tone uppers (s13's leopard sneakers read as a sock gap). Take where the shoe starts from the closet call's shoe box; use the shoe's color only for the "pants and shoes look the same" check.
    - **No case of a sleeve over the hand exists in our photos,** so that cut (2.25 forearm lengths) is an anatomical guess, not a calibration.
    - **The AI's sleeve number depends on the labels:** on the same 10 Oct 8 photos, arm B is 21/30 against `truth.csv` and 24/30 against the Oct 9 labels. The difference is e026de (the gingham sleeve stops above the wrist with the hoodie cuff below; `truth.csv` says at wrist). Its remaining misses are "covers hand" on his gingham shirt's open cuffs (8bc29d, f07193). Over all 50 labeled sleeves it's 90%: the stock photos are easier.
172. **Tests use a real profile: age and picked looks (founder, Oct 9: "let's collect age data from now on… test what age the user picks, what type of fashion they're going for… let's not just randomly test").** CLAUDE.md rule 7. Every test records per photo the person's age band (from their own birth year: 18–21, 22–25, 26–30, 31+), their up-to-3 picked looks in order, height/build/fit if given, and the occasion; it scores with that profile and reports results split by age band and look. Found while checking: the app's `profile` table on his phone is empty (onboarding isn't built), and `zoom_test.py`, `compare.py`, `rescore.py` and `fit_ruler.py`'s re-score all used an invented `minimalist:0.5,smart_casual:0.5`, so no test so far reflects his own picks. **Claude's pushback, taken into the rule:** (1) age is recorded and reported, not scored: an outfit getting different points by age is judging the person, the audience is already 18–30, and there's no evidence raters differ by age; the gold set can show otherwise, and then it's the founder's call; (2) age is never estimated from a photo, only self-reported; (3) stock photos stay "profile unknown" and run with one stated test profile rather than an invented age; (4) profiles live in git-ignored `inputs/`, with first names or codes and each friend's OK. Harness wiring (a per-photo profile file read by the test scripts instead of the fixed styles string) lands with the first real profile, his.
173. **The style picker shows each style as drawn outfits, not photos of people (founder, Oct 9: "people might not know the different types of styles… maybe adding an image… you can decide that part"; he also said the picked styles, not age, are the North Star).** 02 D2 update block. Claude's call, ranked: (1) **code-drawn outfits, two per style, with the name and one line** (chosen): free, already built (#141, #143), the same look as Closet, changes with a seed edit, and shows only clothes, so a pick isn't swayed by the model's age, body or skin; (2) the founder modeling: one body, one skin tone and his age would signal "this is for guys like him", and he doesn't own outdoor or classic-prep pieces; (3) a faceless avatar: the Closet research rejected avatars (#84), and a body shape still shows; (4) the current stock photos: one person per style, 6 photos for 7 styles, and the same bias as (2). Risk: drawings carry less vibe than photos. Check before P4 ships: show the 7 tiles to 5 people in his target age and ask which they'd wear and what they'd call each (a P2 to-do). Also: a `Something else?` tile logs his own words to find missing styles; fit stays its own question (founder's point: fitted jeans are a choice inside several styles, not an age thing). **Unchanged:** picks steer suggestions, "Reads as", tips and the morning card; they never change a score (#81), so someone in fitted jeans isn't marked down for not matching the founder's taste.

## Oct 9 · Stylist: chats by day, the "Ask your stylist…" bar in the tab bar (UI session; numbered from #180)

180. **Stylist keeps its chat by day, and the ask bar moves into the tab bar (founder, Oct 9: "the tab bar isn't dynamic", "the floating text bar is awkward", "scrolling to the bottom is buggy", "save chats by date").**
    - **Today only on Stylist.** The thread shows today's messages (the 4 am day key, so 2 am still belongs to yesterday). A clock button at the top left and an "Earlier chats ›" pill at the top of the thread open **Past chats**: one row per earlier day ("Yesterday", the weekday within a week, else "Sep 24"), with that day's best look photo and "Fit check · 8.0 Sharp" / "2 fit checks · best 8.0 Sharp" / "Outfit idea" / "N messages". A day opens as its own read-only page (no composer; its cards still open their sheets; tap answers never show there). Nothing is deleted or moved in the database; it's a view (`thread.ts` `chatDays`, `splitToday`).
    - **The "Ask your stylist…" bar is the tab bar's iOS 26 bottom accessory** (`StylistBar`, `NativeTabs.BottomAccessory`). The system draws its glass and moves it: above the tab bar normally, tucked in beside the shrunk tab bar ("📷 Ask…") once he scrolls down, like Music and Safari. Only on today's thread (not on Past chats or other tabs). The accessory can't follow the keyboard, so it's a launcher: tapping the field (or the mic) opens the real composer over it, which rides the keyboard and closes once it's empty with the keyboard down.
    - **The tab bar now shrinks on scroll on every tab** (#70's "never on Stylist" is replaced). It only works when the screen's first view is a plain ScrollView; Expo doesn't support it for FlatList (tested: FlatList and a View-wrapped list never shrank; a root ScrollView did). A day is a handful of rows, so the thread is now a plain ScrollView.
    - **Scrolling to the bottom:** the ScrollView scrolls to its real measured end (FlatList's cached row heights stopped short as cards grew in stages). "Scroll to this card" uses each row's measured top; a card on an earlier day opens that day's page.
    - **Not changed:** the AI note (first message) now lives in Past chats after day one; the consent screen still says it plainly. Web preview keeps the old always-docked composer (no accessory on web).
181. **Lessons.**
    - **iOS 26 tab-bar minimize needs the screen's first view to be the ScrollView** (a fragment with an absolutely positioned sibling after it is fine; a wrapping View or a FlatList is not).
    - **`NativeTabs.BottomAccessory` can be rendered conditionally** (by route segments) and `usePlacement()` reports `regular` / `inline`.
    - **The Simulator tool's instant taps miss iOS 26 glass header buttons and tab items;** a tap with `duration: 0.15` works. Not an app bug.
    - **With the Simulator's hardware keyboard on, a focused field shows no keyboard;** scrolling now puts such a field away (`Keyboard.isVisible()` check) so the composer can close.
174. **O2 Pick looks built (founder, Oct 9: "This looks pretty good. We can implement this.").** The first real onboarding screen (the rest of P4 is still placeholders). `features/onboarding/looks.ts` (the 7 styles' two drawn outfits each, picks in tap order, max 3, ordered weights 0.5 then the rest split, one pick alone 1), `LookTile.tsx` (drawings from `closet/flats.ts`, name, one line; a 2.5 pt ink border and check when picked; shakes on a 4th tap), `OnboardingNav.tsx` (Back + the 6-step bar, reused by O3–O5 later), `PickLooksScreen.tsx` (route `/onboarding/looks`, demo screen 2). "Up to 3." shows above Continue, not under the grid (the grid runs past the screen). `Something else?` opens a text field; his words (trimmed, 80 max) and the picks wait in kv `onboarding.looks` / `onboarding.otherStyle` until O5 saves the profile; nothing is guessed from the words. Continue works with 1–3 picks or with words; Not sure saves none. Copy in 02 §5 (`onb.looks.line.*`, `onb.looks.else.*`, new prompt). Tests: `onboardingLooks.test.ts` (4); `npm run check` green (315 app tests). Checked on the web preview (light, dark, picks, the 4th tap, the text field) and on the iPhone 17 Pro Simulator (light and dark). One change after seeing it on iOS: the streetwear tile's graphic tee became a black sweatshirt, because the closet's print mark reads as a missing picture at tile size.

## Oct 9 · Onboarding made walkable (founder: "the onboarding process isn't [set up] properly"; numbered #182+ after the Stylist session's #180–181)

182. **What was wrong:** every onboarding step except O2 was still a P0 placeholder with no buttons, so a new user (no `onboarding.done`) landed on the O1 placeholder and could go nowhere; O2's Continue led to the O3 placeholder. Separately, **the scoring call didn't check consent**: the closet call refused without it (#91), but `routedSource` sent any real photo to Gemini in a dev build. Both fixed:
    - **Built** (02 D1, D3, D4, §3): **O1 Welcome** (s15 hero, neck-down, faded; the 8.5 Sharp mini card; wordmark; headline; Get started), **O3 Basics** (his looks echoed as names, "Streetwear · Minimal", since the tiles are drawings now; the stylist's template reply; height blank until the first tap seeds 5′10″, range 4′10″–7′0″; Build and Fit segments; birth year blank; the tape-measure silhouette ported from the mockup's `sil()`; "Looks right" off until a year is picked), **O3w birth year** (a plain list of years in a sheet, newest first, nothing preselected; no wheel package), **O4 age stop** (under 18 by year sets the Keychain flag; the gate sends him to O4, terminal), **O5 Consent** (the §3 rows with `Google's Gemini API`; "Agree & check my fit" and "Not now" the same size). Agree saves the profile with `consentVersion` 1 and the date, ends onboarding, lands in Stylist and opens the camera; Not now saves the profile with no consent and lands in Stylist. Drafts wait in kv (`onboarding.looks`, `onboarding.basics`; `onboarding.otherStyle` is kept) until O5.
    - **Consent enforced twice:** the camera route opens the consent sheet (`/sheets/consent`, same content with an X) instead of the camera when he's onboarded without consent, and `routedSource` takes a consent check so a real photo can't reach Gemini without it (test: `onboardingFlow.test.ts`).
    - **Not built yet, hidden rather than dead ends:** "See an example check" (O1e) on O1 and "See an example first" / "Privacy policy" on O5 (no example screen or policy page yet); O2n "Pick by name"; S26 "Turn on fit checks" card in Stylist (the camera's consent sheet covers it for now); S14 first 3 moves, the paywall and the notification ask after the first check (P6); resume-on-relaunch at the exact step (relaunch starts at O1; the looks and basics he gave are kept).
    - **Flag for the founder:** O5's footer "You can turn this off anytime in You → Privacy." is in the approved deck, but the You tab isn't built, so there's no switch yet. It must exist before outside testers (P5).
    - Checked on the web preview: O1 → O2 → O3 → O3w → O5 → Agree → Stylist + camera; Not now → Stylist → camera → consent sheet → Agree → camera; 2010 → O4. The age flag doesn't survive a web reload (the web stand-in keeps it in memory); on iOS it's the Keychain. `npm run check` green (323 app tests).
183. **Weights audit after his Oct 9 6.5 (founder: "black oversized tee, black baggy jeans, a 6.5 feels off"), plus three card/closet bugs from his phone.**
    - **The phone had the update** (Metro was serving it; rubric 4.0.0, prompt v3z) and **the reading was right** (black tee volume 4, black wide jeans full break, white leather sneakers). The code was wrong: streetwear's shoe list had canvas, retro runners, low-profile sneakers and even work boots and trail shoes as "home" shoes, but not `white_leather_sneaker`. So tee + wide jeans + Club C-type sneakers scored 3 of the 4 points streetwear needs, read as no style, and the general standard charged the one baggy look three times (oversized top −0.225, wide leg −0.45, everything oversized −0.3): raw 6.525 → 6.5. **Fix (Claude's call, a bug, not taste):** `white_leather_sneaker` joins `ST_SHOE_H` in `rubric.py`; fixtures regenerated (`make_*.py`, `gen:rubric`). His photo now reads as streetwear and scores **7.5 Put together** (the style council's own anchor for "black tee + black jeans + white leather"), with "Add a layer: an overshirt or a knit → 8.5". **0 of 7,233 parity scores changed** (6 cases gained a streetwear label); no version bump (still 4.0.0, engine 4). `seed.json` also picked up #162's effort 2 → 3 on the layer fix (it hadn't been regenerated then).
    - **Audit of the 78 saved readings (his 12 phone readings, 27 zoom1 founder photos, 39 stock, live arm B):** 40 of 78 read as no style; 9 lost points to the "vs your style" proportion rules. Only his two tee + wide-jeans photos were a one-piece list hole. The rest is the open **wide-leg default** (#124 on his list): the general band stops at relaxed, so wide jeans lose 0.45 whenever the outfit isn't streetwear/workwear (his tan overshirt + grey tee + wide black jeans: 7.0, would be 7.5). Widening the default band to wide raises 321 parity cases by +0.5 (some 8.5 → 9.0: denim jacket + oxford + wide trousers + loafers; hoodie + wide sweatpants + slides 5.0 → 5.5), so it stays his call.
    - **His friend's blazer check (8.5; founder: "proportions weren't good, I'd say a 7"):** navy blazer (read as `suit_jacket`) + black tee + light-wash relaxed jeans stacking on suede chukkas. v4 has no rule for a tailored jacket over loose or light-wash jeans (the parity set has only 7 blazer-with-jeans cases at Everyday/Class), and the hem was read `slight_break` though it stacks. Proposed, not built: a tailored-jacket-over-casual-denim rule (−0.5, fix "Swap to dark straight jeans"), waiting on his OK and which check was his friend's.
    - **Closet question showed the floor (his screenshot):** the stored boots' box was right, but `cropLayout` always covered the frame, so a wide two-shoe box zoomed into its middle: the floor between the feet. Now a region wider than the frame is fitted to the frame's width and anything above the box is painted solid (`hideAbove`), so the never-above-the-piece rule (no face) holds. Tested on his Oct 8 box (both boots show).
    - **"No quick fixes for this one." twice:** below 9 the summary already says it; the fix area now skips that row when a Next level or Weather tip fills the section (the first tip drops its top line).
    - **Not fixed, explained:** "Are these your grey shoes?" / "It's my grey slides" come from Oct 8 pieces saved from his boots' misreads (`other`, then `slides_sandals`). Yes merges the sighting; the slides piece can be removed by long-press in Closet.
184. **Silhouette and proportion join v5 (founder, Oct 9: "proportions should be awarded… a person should not get awarded for not wearing good proportions"; "silhouettes are an important part of a fit"; "include suit rules"; "step by step").** Claude's calls: not a research sweep of every style × combination (the 7 styles are covered by the style council; most rules are editorial opinion; earlier research rounds had fabrication), but four steps: (1) two research agents, casual silhouette/proportion and tailoring, writing `rubric/research/notes/v5-silhouette.md` and `v5-tailoring.md`, each rule tagged reward / penalty / blocks-credits with the reading it needs, sources verified by Claude; (2) a reading test of those details on his saved photos, since the AI's leg shape (≈50%) and hem (26–43%) on his Oct 8 photos are the real bottleneck: unreliable readings ship as tips; (3) paper calibration on the saved fits; (4) the gold set sets point values. Design direction: layer/elevated credits only count when the silhouette holds, and a deliberate silhouette can earn a credit inside the existing cap.
185. **Silhouette and tailoring research in (step 1 of #184): `rubric/research/notes/v5-silhouette.md`, `v5-tailoring.md`.** Claude spot-checked 6 sources: GQ Apr 2025 and Sep 2026, Mr Porter, RMRS, NYT (Friedman), FashionBeans 2024. All of them matched, with two corrections noted in the tailoring file: the NYT volume-matching advice is the author's, not Derek Guy's, and FashionBeans pairs oversized blazers with slimmer trousers. Findings that change the plan:
    - **Silhouette:** the sources agree on short over full or a middle top over a middle leg, never long and loose everywhere. The best rule to test is a waist-break reward (a tuck, a cropped or at-belt top, or a hip-length layer over a straight-to-wide leg). A long loose layer over a loose leg should block the layer credit. Wide legs under a fitted, cropped or tucked top should stop costing 0.45.
    - **His friend's chore coat over slim jeans:** straight or fuller is preferred (6 sources), but no 2023+ source calls slim a mistake. So it gets a reduced layer credit, not a penalty, and it waits for a better leg reading: slim vs straight agrees with truth only 40–53% of the time.
    - **"A fitted jacket over baggy pants is off" is contradicted** (GQ, Mr Porter, H&M).
    - **Tailoring:** under a blazer, the hem should be no break to one clean break; a stack or pool is a fault (6 sources, 0 dissent), so the planned "stacked jeans = 0" rule needs a tailored-jacket exception. Relaxed tailoring is real; a too-big jacket shows in sleeves over the hands and shoulders that don't hold. Suits are v4's worst area (a suit jacket alone over jeans scores 9.0; a suit with white sneakers at an interview scores 8.5).
    - **Conflicts with RULEBOOK, for the council and the gold set:** the 9.0 anchor "overcoat + knit + wide trousers + loafers" is the shape GQ calls stumpifying; the chunky-sneaker credit under wide legs stays streetwear-only (Mr Porter and H&M disagree with it).
    - **Next (step 2):** a flip and accuracy test of the cues these rules need, on his saved photos (about $2): top and layer length, tuck, hem with the new `stacked`, `shoe_profile`, a 3-way `shows_below_outer`, and the hem-on-shoe position. Code-only rules that need no new reading (wide legs under a close top; the tailored-hem fault on the current `pooling`/`full_break`; no sneakers at formal weddings and interviews) can be paper-tested on the saved fits now.
186. **Rubric v4.1 (engine 5): the code-only rules from the paper test ship (founder, Oct 9: "we can go with your recommendations"). Report: `rubric/research/notes/v5-paper-test-a.md`; prototype `extraction-test/rubrics/v5a_proto.py`, switches A1–A5, all off = v4.0 exactly (0 diffs on 7,233 parity cases + 79 real).**
    - **Shipped:** (1) refined A1: no `P1_leg_vs_style` on a relaxed/wide leg under a close or short top half: no layer → top looseness ≤ 2, cropped/at_belt, or fully tucked; with a layer → the layer's looseness ≤ 2 or cropped/at_belt; never for loungewear bottoms. The literal A1 (the inner tuck counts under a loose overshirt) was dropped: it sent 45 cases to 9.0. (2) A1b, his "wide jeans" to-do item: relaxed/wide jeans and cargos are never charged by the default band (43 parity cases vs 321 for widening the whole band). (3) A3f: any sneaker under a tailored jacket at `interview` (and `wedding_formal` in the rubric's table; the app has no such occasion) costs S2 at least 1.0 unit (−1.2). S2's white-leather exemption had been dead code (the dressiness gap never passes 2). Event is held: it covers casual parties too.
    - **Proof:** rubric.py with these rules equals the prototype with A1r + A1x + A1b + A3f on all 7,233 parity cases. 122 cases move (none of the leg rules lands a new 9+). TypeScript matches all 7,233 again (parity fixtures regenerated). Version 4.1.0, engine 5, new rule parameters in rubric.json (`P1_leg_vs_style.freeLoose/closeTopMaxVolume/shortLengths`, `S2.tailoredFloor*`). Checks now store the live rubric version (`service.ts` had '4.0.0' hard-coded).
    - **His real fits:** his tan overshirt + grey tee + wide black jeans (FC-aa2f3d0b) goes 7.0 → 7.5, and its "Swap to relaxed-leg pants" fix is gone. Two of his boot questions stop asking because the answer no longer moves his score: boots vs chukkas on f07193 (both 8.0) and Chelsea vs work boots on d918c0 (7.5 vs 8.0, under the 1.0 bar). `known.test.ts` was updated and `test/data/phone-fixes-oct9.json` regenerated from `kitted_logic.suggest_fixes`.
    - **Not shipped, and why:** A2, a heavy hem under a blazer, is right and has 6 sources, but the AI read his friend's stacked jeans as a slight break, so it fires on nothing. It ships with the hem/`stacked` reading test. A4, the suit jacket worn alone, would take the friend's fit to 8.0, but for the wrong reason: the jacket looks like a navy blazer that the AI called a suit jacket. A5, the too-big blazer, charges one sleeve reading twice; it waits for the gold set.
    - **Honest result:** none of the shipped rules moves his friends' 8.5s. Both chore-coat checks stay 8.5 even with the jeans read as slim, and the blazer check needs the hem read. Fixing them needs better readings (step 2b and the leg probe), not more code-only rules.
187. **Measure instead of guess (founder, Oct 9: "run multiple agents… figure out scripts for the things the AI is messing up on… so they can take over"; "it can be later, but plan it in the common file").** Plan: `rubric/research/v5-brief.md` → "Measure instead of guess". Claude's calls: one probe at a time, not many agents at once (each needs labeled truth, a takeover bar and a review; parallel probes would compete for the same thin label set). The takeover bar is ≥10 points better than the AI on truth, ≤30% unsure, repeatable, and it holds on mirror, propped and dark-on-dark photos. A probe starts as a tie-breaker and becomes the reading only after the gold set agrees. Measurements are garment against garment, or positions along his own limbs, never garment against body (#164). Order: M1 leg shape (running), M2 hem stack, M3 shoe type/profile, M4 tee below the layer, M5 top boxiness, M6 measured colour. Expectation set honestly: two earlier probes (leg width vs hips, the fit ruler) didn't beat the AI, so some of these will end as shadow logs.
188. **Privacy fix: the face-hidden copy could miss his face (found Oct 9 by the leg-probe agent on stock s25; confirmed by Claude).** A mirror selfie with a camera in front of the face: the face detector found nothing, the body pose landed on T-shirts hanging on the wall, so prep hid the shirts' "head area", returned `ok`, and the upload copy showed his face. The same `Prep.locate` runs in the app, so a real check could have sent a face to Google; the stock copy was sent in the Oct 8 zoom1-stock test. **Fix (`KittedPrep.swift`):** for fit checks, the top of his own person outline (Vision person segmentation, balanced quality, 256 px wide) is always hidden too, grown to the whole head and chin (1.5× the head's width, 12–30% of his outline's height). A detected face, head joints or shoulders count as his only when they sit on that outline. If there's no outline and no face, head joints or shoulders on it, and his top is inside the frame, nothing is sent (ruling 10 unchanged). An outline that touches the top edge (chest-down shot) adds nothing. **Checked on the Mac with the app's own Swift (`kmeasure locate`, new) on 60 photos (stock, mirror, Oct 7, every phone check):** no status changes, s25 now hidden, and on his and his friends' photos the new region overlaps the old face box. Over-hiding is at most part of the collar on 5 stock photos, the safe direction. **Still not hidden:** a face in a framed picture on the wall in s25 (a print, not a person; the face detector doesn't see it). The iOS build compiles and installs on the Simulator. **Reaches his phone only after `tools/phone/install.sh` (native change): P1 on his list.**
189. **M1 leg probe: drop as a replacement (`rubric/research/notes/leg-probe.md`; `extraction-test/leg_probe.py`, `vision/LegTaper.swift`, `kmeasure legs`; labels `leg_labels.csv`, written blind before it ran).** Five shapes: 54% when it answers, 36% unsure, against the AI's 64% on the same photos. Straight vs wide fails: same taper, and the opening-vs-shoe width depends on the shoe. His joggers' elastic cuffs read as straight. One promising signal: slim vs straight by hem ÷ knee width got 11/12 and 9/10, but only 4 readable straight photos, an unstable cut (0.76–0.91) and 33–50% unsure. Repeatable (100% same answer). Not used as a question trigger (#121–124 rule out fit questions). **Re-test slim vs straight on the gold set and the size ladder (needs ≥15 slim and ≥15 straight).** So the chore-coat friend's case still waits for truth photos. Next probe: M2 (the hem stack).
190. **M2 hem probe: doesn't beat the AI; nothing changes in the app (`rubric/research/notes/hem-probe.md`; `extraction-test/hem_probe.py`, `vision/HemProbe.swift`, `kmeasure hems`; labels `hem_labels.csv`, blind, all 11 of his Oct 8 hems agree with truth.csv).** Heavy (full break/stacked/pooling) vs light: probe 65% when it answers, 32% unsure, against the AI's 75% on the same photos. Fold counting fails: his black jeans show no fold shading, and jogger cuffs or rolled hems look like stacks. Stacked vs pooling can't be tested (1 clear stack, 0 clear pools). The one promising signal is where the pant colour ends along the shin from the ankle joint: 20/22 vs the AI's 67%, but 56% unsure and fit on these same photos; as a tie-breaker it would add only +7 points (bar: +10). On the friend's blazer photo it also sees a slight break, so A2 still wouldn't fire. Kept as a shadow measure in the harness; re-test on the gold set (needs 15+ heavy, 15+ light, light-wash stacks, pooled trousers, joggers). It must never run on shorts (it would treat the shin as the pants).
    - **Claude's call after M1 + M2:** pause M3–M6. Both probes failed for the same reasons: too few labeled photos, and his dark-on-dark outfits, where geometry from a phone photo has little to measure. More probes before the gold set would repeat that. Proposed instead (his call, it costs money): a **focused second look by the AI** at the existing lower close-up with one narrow question (hem: does fabric fold or rest on the shoe? shoe: boot shaft, sole thickness), tested with the usual 3-run flip test on his saved photos (about $1). The close-ups already cut his boots→slides misreads from 9/21 to 0 (#104), so a narrow crop plus a narrow question is the most likely win.
191. **The gold set becomes mostly Instagram screenshots (founder, Oct 10: friends are students with no time to shoot, and he doesn't own 25 pairs of jeans).** This revises #167, which said no saved Instagram images (that was Codex collecting automatically) and the 80-photo staged plan in `speed-and-detail-plan.md`. New split:
    - **~85 Instagram fit-pic screenshots, good and bad, that he rates** 1–10, blind to the app, shuffled, with one line of why. Optional: friends rate about 20 of them (10 minutes, no shooting).
    - **~15 photos of him alone (~30 minutes) where one outfit is changed on purpose**, so the facts are known: the same jeans worn normally, cuffed, rolled and hitched; tuck on/off; jacket on/off; boots vs sneakers. Each as a mirror selfie and a propped shot. These give the before/after pairs and known-truth hems Instagram can't.
    - **Optional: ~15 slim and ~15 straight shop product photos** where the brand states the fit (e.g. Levi's 511 slim, 501 straight): true answers for M1's slim vs straight.
    - **Rules for other people's images:** they stay in `inputs/gold-set/` (git-ignored); usernames and captions are cropped out and nobody is recorded; internal testing only, never posted, shared or used in videos (ask a lawyer before any other use); faces are hidden by the app's prep before anything goes to Google. Rule 7: Instagram and shop photos are "profile unknown", scored with one stated test profile. His own photos carry his profile.
    - **Limits, said plainly:** for screenshots the facts are his reading of the same pixels the AI sees, so they calibrate *scores*, not the measuring probes. One rater means the calibration fits his taste until friends rate a sample. Curated Instagram photos are better lit than the app's real input, which is why his ~15 phone photos stay.
192. **The judges are a panel of Claude subagents, not people (founder, Oct 10: no audience for a survey, no money for a panel, no stylist; "it's probably sub-agents").** Claude's design, built to guard against an AI judge's known weaknesses:
    - **Five lenses:** a streetwear stylist, a tailoring editor, a minimal/Japanese eye, a workwear/vintage eye, and a well-dressed 22-year-old peer.
    - **Blind and independent:** each judge sees face-hidden copies under shuffled IDs only, never the app's score, the rubric's point values or the other judges. The shared brief is `inputs/judge-pilot/BRIEF.md`, with a fixed meaning for every number: 7 = put together, nothing special; 8 = sharp and deliberate; 9 = editorial, rare. Most everyday fits from this audience are not an 8.
    - **The target number** is the middle score of the five. A spread over 2 points gets flagged for the founder's look.
    - **"Trained" means grounded in the checked research notes.** No model can be retrained.
    - **Trusted only after validation:** the same photo twice must agree within 0.5; it must prefer the better version in his one-outfit variant pairs (cuffed/uncuffed, tucked/untucked); it must put his friends' blazer and chore-coat fits near his ~7; and its scores must spread, not crowd into 6–8.
    - **Pilot started Oct 10** on the 59 usable saved photos (his and his friends' phone checks, web mirror selfies, stock); the face-hidden copies were made with the #188 fix. This replaces the human rating plans in `speed-and-detail-plan.md` and #191 (his own ratings). A free friend sample (20 photos, 10 minutes) stays welcome as a cross-check.
193. **Judges score by parts, not by gut (founder, Oct 10: "set parameters by which they should judge… good colors with awful proportions should average out").** Brief v2 (`inputs/judge-pilot/BRIEF.md`): six parts, each 1–10 with written anchors (9–10 / 7 / 5 / 2–3): **fit, proportions and silhouette, color, shoes, cohesion and style, condition and finishing**. Each part is judged on its own, and a gut score is recorded last as a check only. **The outfit's score is computed by Claude from the parts, not by the judges:** a weighted average, first weights fit 25%, proportions 25%, color 15%, shoes 15%, cohesion 15%, condition 5%. A part that can't be seen is NA and the weights renormalize. Rounded to the half. The weights are a starting point. The pilot shows how the parts relate to the gut score, so a part that's over- or under-weighted becomes visible. The parts also map onto the app's own subscores (fit, color, proportion), so each part of the rubric can be tuned on its own. The holistic v1 run was stopped before any judge finished.
194. **Judge pilot results (`rubric/research/notes/judge-pilot.md`).** Five Claude judges scored 59 saved photos by parts.
    - **The app is too generous:** about 0.45 above the panel overall and 0.79 above it on his and his friends' photos.
    - **His friends' fits:** blazer 5.5 (app 8.5), chore coat 7.5 (app 8.5).
    - **The parts' weights roughly match the judges' gut** (never more than 1 apart).
    - **Honest limits:** the five judges never differ by more than 0.5 (one model, five times); scores are compressed (SD 0.62, 83% between 6 and 7.5); before/after pairs barely separate.
    - **Claude's calls:** use the panel for direction and for faults the reading misses (condition, hem bunching, long sleeves, a long shirt over wide jeans), not yet as fine truth. Next: different models per judge, ranking and A-vs-B pairs instead of absolute scores, anchor photos, a repeat run, and 10 founder looks at the biggest disagreements. Starting at 6.5 instead of 7.0 lines the app up with the panel (median 7.0, 8+ ≈ 8%); still his call (it needs a standout credit so 9–9.5 stay reachable).
195. **Scoring by parts (founder, Oct 10: split the score into parts, have scripts or the AI measure each, average the parts, show them).** The idea: replace "7.0 + credits − mistakes" with a weighted average of part scores, each 1–10. The parts: fit, proportions and silhouette, color, shoes, cohesion and style, condition, and occasion (as a part or as a cap after the average; to be decided). The card shows the parts.
    - **Claude's view:** a better design than v4. Points come from the parts, not from owning a layer; a beginner sees what to work on ("Color 9 · Proportions 5"); each part can be improved and calibrated on its own; a part that isn't visible drops out of the average.
    - **One change to the founder's version:** Gemini doesn't give part scores. It reports facts, and code (plus phone scripts, most likely for color, from the closet's measured color and lightness) computes each part, so the same photo still gets the same score (#63–64).
    - **Research started:** five agents, one per part (color; proportions; fit; shoes + occasion; cohesion + condition), working from `rubric/research/parts/BRIEF.md`. Each writes `rubric/research/parts/<part>.md` with its checks, evidence, the tool for each check and its measured reliability, anchors, worked examples against the judge pilot, the v4 rules it absorbs, the card line and a test plan.
    - **Next:** Claude merges them into one spec (weights, NA, the occasion choice, how 9–9.5 stay reachable) for his approval. Nothing in the app changes before then. This becomes the core of rubric v5 (`v5-brief.md`).
196. **Parts scoring: merged spec (`rubric/research/parts/SPEC.md`, from the six part reports in that folder). Waiting on the founder's OK.**
    - **Averaged parts:** Proportions 30%, Fit 25% (worst piece counts), Color 15%, Shoes 15%, Cohesion 15%. Each starts at 7 (the balanced, fine middle). Faults subtract; rewards apply only when there's no fault. An unseen part is NA and the weights renormalize.
    - **After the average:** Condition subtracts faults only (stain −1.2, wrinkled −0.4, cap −1.6). Occasion only holds the score down (a ceiling plus up to −0.5, never averaged in, because averaging would add about +0.45 to most fits). Round to the half.
    - **Each fact has one owner** (listed in the spec).
    - **Today's fields cap Fit at 7 and Proportions at about 8.5,** so 9 is hard and 9.5 waits for new fit cues.
    - **What the research found:**
        - The judge pilot's part scores are halo (they check direction, not each part).
        - The AI almost never reports condition faults (2 of 19).
        - Skin pixels leak into the phone's measured garment colors (5 of 83 neutral pieces), so no measured color feeds a score until it's skin-free. Closet matching has the same leak, which belongs to the closet session.
        - Until reading batch 1 passes, Proportions and Fit mostly say 7.
    - **Order:**
        1. Reading batches 1 and 2 plus a condition prompt, each flip-tested. About $4 in all; needs his OK.
        2. A Python prototype of the parts engine, paper-tested on his saved fits and the pilot.
        3. Port it, build a new fix engine aimed at parts, and the card's parts row (mockup first).
        4. The gold set tunes the weights.
    - **Founder calls:** the design, the ~$4, the Event chip's band or name, splitting Class/Work later, and the card mockup.
197. **The founder approved the parts plan and ~$4 of reading tests (Oct 10: "You have my okay").** The Event chip takes the default when it's built: the softer band. Reading batch 1 (hem_at, under_shows, scene.pose, lightness, shoulder_seam, new sleeve_end values) is being flip-tested on the labeled photos plus his newest pull (`screenshots/phone/20261010-185554`; budget $2, report `extraction-test/results/b1/`). The newest pull shows the phone ran rubric 4.1.0 today (his 16:07 check: a black quilted jacket over black jeans and white sneakers, 8.0, one quick tap answered), so it's getting JS updates through Metro. The face-hiding fix (#188) is native and still needs the reinstall.
198. **Reading batch 1 failed (`extraction-test/results/b1/report.md`; $1.83 spent of the $4 approved).** Six new fields in the scoring call made the score less steady: 96% → 86% of photos within 0.5 across 3 runs, mostly the shoe credit flipping on his grey suede boots. Footwear type fell 4.4 points, old-field accuracy fell 4–8 points, and latency rose 0.88 s. **lightness** (95% same, 94–98% right) and **scene.pose** (100%) pass on their own. **hem_at, under_shows, shoulder_seam and the new sleeve values fail** exactly on the lines the rules need. Lesson, again (#63–64): **adding fields to the scoring call shakes the fields that feed the score.**
    - **Claude's call:** the details the parts engine needs come from a **separate narrow second look** at the existing face-hidden close-up, with one or two questions and its own small call that runs in parallel, so it never touches the score's readings. Test that next (about $0.30–0.60). Then test lightness + pose alone (about $1.60) with what's left of the $4.
    - **For the parts engine:** no hard formality line in the shoe part. K4's 0.75 cliff is the main score wobble in both arms.
199. **Second look (look1, `extraction-test/results/look1/report.md`; $0.56 spent, about $2.39 of the approved $4 in all).** Separate one-close-up calls that can't shake the score.
    - **They pass:** whether there's a top under the outer piece (100% the same), the peek below it as yes/no (100% the same, 86% right when it fires; all 5 definite peeks caught, against 3 of 10 for batch 1), and whether the pants rest on the shoe as yes/no (94% the same, 97% right).
    - **They fail:** where a top or jacket ends, the pant break and stack (the friend's blazer stack is still read as a slight break), and the shoulder seam, which also made the other answers worse.
    - **Cost:** +0.24¢ per check for both calls, and 1 in 8 finishes after the scoring call.
    - **Claude's calls:** the peek and "rests on the shoe" go into the parts engine as a second look (the peek as a tip, or −0.5 at most, until the gold set). The shoulder seam is dropped. Jacket length and the hem stack stay unreadable for now. Next tries: a tight high-resolution hem-and-shoe crop with one "folds above the shoe?" question (cheap), and the gold set.
    - **Meanwhile:** build the parts-engine prototype on the fields that are reliable today (free), so he can see the before/after on his saved fits.
200. **Parts-engine prototype and paper test (`rubric/research/parts/prototype-results.md`; `extraction-test/rubrics/parts_proto.py`, `parts_test.py`, `results/parts1/`).** It uses only readings that passed steadiness tests; everything else is a tip.
    - **Steadier than v4.1:** 49/49 photos have all 3 runs within 0.5 (v4.1: 47/49), and 86% agree exactly (v4.1: 63%).
    - **Much closer to the judge panel:** mean difference −0.03 (v4.1 +0.54); within 0.5 on 50 of 59 (v4.1: 32); on his and his friends' photos the gap drops from +0.93 to +0.40.
    - **His and his friends' checks:**
        - friend's blazer: 8.5 → 7.0 ("Style 6: the tailored jacket and the loose washed jeans come from different worlds"; panel 5.5)
        - chore coat ×3: 8.5 → 7.0 (his own estimate was ~7)
        - his tee + wide jeans: 7.5 → 7.0
        - gingham: 7.5 → 6.5 (sleeves past the wrists)
        - the quilted jacket (ebe23f): 8.0 → 7.0
    - **The problem:** too compressed. Only 5.5, 6.5, 7.0 and 7.5 occur, and **nothing reaches 8** (the panel puts 7% at 8+). Fit can't go above 7 without a shoulder-seam reading, Proportions rarely earns its rewards, and a 15% part moves the total only 0.075 per half point.
    - **The prototype's calls, to confirm before the port:**
        - shoe wear is a flat −1.0 (−0.5 for work boots and suede; the worn read flips, and suede nap reads as wear)
        - only pooling blocks the proportion rewards
        - shoes out of the photo count as real shoes in the loungewear ladder
        - a lost shoe type makes Shoes NA
        - overdressed ramps from 1.5 levels above
        - parts are averaged unrounded
    - **Claude's call:** adopt it as the core. Before the port, tune the reward sizes so deliberate fits reach 8: anchor on the panel's top photos (P010, P021, P001, P043/P044) with the readable rewards (waist break, a short layer over a full leg, cohesion's signature pieces and standout, color's tie-in and standout, shoes clearly chosen). Don't stretch the whole scale (×1.5 gets 8+ to only 5% while seeing nothing new). New readings for 8–9 (shoulder seam, a stack cue) wait for the gold set.
    - **The fix engine has to be rebuilt around parts:** today's "Swap to … → 8.5" lines come from v4 credits.
    - **Taste calls for the founder:** slides at 4.5; Shoes NA on a lost type; no credit just for wearing a chore coat; what the card leads with when every part is 7.
201. **Tuning approved (founder, Oct 10: "go ahead with the tuning").** His four taste calls took Claude's defaults: slides 4.5 on Shoes; Shoes is NA when the shoe type is lost; no credit just for wearing a jacket or chore coat; and when every part is 7 the card says "Put together. Every part is solid; here's what would lift it" instead of naming a weakest part.
    - **The tuning runs on the prototype (report `rubric/research/parts/tuning-results.md`).** Only readable rewards change size, possibly with an "earned" bonus when two or more parts are strong and none is weak. The whole scale isn't stretched.
    - **Guarding against noise:** a held-out half of the judge-pilot photos (his outfits never split across halves).
    - **Targets:** median about 7; 8+ at 5–12%; steadiness no worse than v1; the panel gap within ±0.2. The friend's blazer stays at 7 or below. The obvious fixes and the stock before/after pairs have to lift the score.
202. **Parts engine tuned, v2 (`rubric/research/parts/tuning-results.md`; `extraction-test/parts2_test.py`, `results/parts2/`; test corpus now saved at `results/parts1/corpus.json`).**
    - **Results:**
        - 8+ on 5 of 78 photos (6%): the tucked shirt over straight trousers, the olive suit, three web photos. None at 9; median 7.0. The 11 that moved all went up 0.5.
        - Steadiness unchanged (49/49).
        - Panel gap +0.01, within 0.5 on 51 of 59. On the held-out half: 26/29 within 0.5 (v1 25), correlation 0.56 (v1 0.43).
    - **His fits:**
        - friend's blazer and the chore coat: 7.0
        - his gingham overshirt: 6.5
        - his gingham shirt worn alone: still 7.0 (panel 6.0); the shirt hanging long has no reliable reading
    - **Fixes lift and never lower:** tucking his black tee 7.0 → 7.5; slides to sneakers +0.5 to +1.0 on all 9 slide photos; better sleeves +0.5. The stock before/after pairs never drop, but none of their changes is something the engine rewards.
    - **What changed:**
        - the waist-break and short-layer rewards: +1.0 → +1.5 (+0.5 for an untucked short top in a mirror selfie, where the raised arms lift the hem)
        - a full tailored outfit: +1.0, with the proportions cap at +2.5
        - one style top to toe, or a suit worn right: +1.0; a standout piece: +0.75
        - color credits stack up to +2.0
        - dress shoes, loafers and Chelsea boots under a suit count as the outfit's own shoe
        - a +0.25 earned bonus when a second part reaches 8 and nothing is under 7 (+0.5 broke steadiness)
        - the weights are unchanged
    - **Honest limits:**
        - the tuning half couldn't separate the settings, so they were chosen on steadiness, the share of 8s and the named photos; the held-out half isn't fully clean
        - **the lift went mostly to tailoring:** streetwear has almost no reward the readings can see (volume, stacking on purpose, texture), a bias against the core audience
        - good fit (Fit ≤ 7), lengths, hems against the shoe, texture, fading and accessories stay invisible
    - **Claude's call:** before the port, a short streetwear pass that uses readable facts (a boxy top over a wide leg with a street shoe; the look's own shoe; the style read) so 8s aren't tailoring-only. Then the port, the parts-based fix engine and the card.
203. **Streetwear pass, v3 (`rubric/research/parts/streetwear-pass.md`; `extraction-test/parts3_test.py`, `results/parts3/`; v3 is now the prototype's default).**
    - **One new reward, "the streetwear shape":** +1.0 on Proportions for a boxy top (volume ≥4, untucked, ending at the fly or higher) over relaxed or wide jeans or cargos, with no layer, reading as streetwear, in chunky, basketball, canvas, retro-runner or low-profile sneakers. It replaces the waist-break reward when it's larger. White leather sneakers are excluded: counting them sent his green sweatshirt fit to 8.0 against the panel's 7.0.
    - **Real photos: 0 of 78 move.** No saved reading shows that shape (his hoodies read volume 3; his shoes are slides, white leather or boots; web m01's jeans read straight).
    - **Council outfits:** the graphic tee + wide jeans + Dunks goes 7.5 → 8.0, and vintage sport gets a path to 8. Two outfits their advocates rated 7.5 now score 8.0, for the gold set to settle. Workwear and outdoor still have no path to 8.
    - **Unchanged from v2:** steadiness, the panel numbers and every keep target.
    - **New fix candidates** (the copy is the founder's call): white leather → chunky sneakers; slides → chunky sneakers.
    - **Rejected:** a graphic as the standout (the read flips), a shoe-carries-the-leg credit at +1.0, straight legs, reading hoodies as boxy, a tonal streetwear credit (needs lightness).
    - **What still can't be seen, and the gold set must include:** boxiness on hoodies, a deliberate stack, leg width, the exact sneaker type, graphics, and accessories. Its pairs: white leather vs chunky; boxy vs regular tee; stacked vs cropped hem.
204. **The parts card mockup (`mockups/parts-card.html`; screenshots in `screenshots/parts-card/`, git-ignored). Waiting on the founder's pick.** Three options, each keeping today's card (score and word, summary, fixes, next level):
    - **(1)** a five-part strip under the score, about +95 pt, that turns into a list at accessibility sizes
    - **(2)** a meter row per part, +205 pt; the clearest, but tall and stat-sheet-like
    - **(3)** only the weakest and the strongest part, +125 pt
    - **Claude's recommendation: (1).**
    - **Rules found while mocking:**
        - parts show whole and half points only; the score keeps one decimal
        - the lead line names the lowest part only when it's under 7, otherwise the strongest, or "Every part is solid" (Fit can't go above 7 today, so "lowest part" alone would make every good fit say "Fit 7")
        - the Condition line shows only when it costs points
        - the Occasion line shows only when it holds the score down, with what the parts alone would make
        - the separate "Reads as" line moves into Style's reasons (default: drop it from the card)
    - **A fix row must never show a gain that doesn't happen.** On the friend's blazer, "dark straight jeans" lifts Style to 7.5 but the score stays 7.0, so the fix becomes "Swap the jeans for navy chinos → 8.0", with the jeans as a "Smaller step" tip.
205. **The parts live in a sheet, not on the card (founder, Oct 11: "displayed when they press the number… some UI clue… something that pops up like an overlay… instead of making that card so detailed").** The card stays as today (score and word, summary, fixes, next level). Tapping the score opens the Why sheet, which leads with the five-part strip and holds the reasons per part, Condition, Occasion and "Reads as" (moved off the card). The score gets a visible tap cue (a small chevron or "Why" affordance by "/10"; to be checked against the HIG when built). The mockup's options 1–3 become the sheet's layout question; the strip (option 1) is the default.
    - **His worry, and Claude's answer (Oct 11: "rating fits is hard… people have different styles and expectations… we're going men's, top level"):**
        - disagreement concentrates at the top (8 vs 9); beginner faults are broadly agreed, so score those with confidence
        - the niche is skill level (beginners), not a style
        - judge against the look the outfit is going for (the style read)
        - taste-level advice goes in tips, not points
        - show the reasons and keep "Not quite right?"
        - calibrate on many opinions (the gold set)
    - **His reaction to mockup B** ("too fitted, pants too low, not a 7") was to a drawn figure, not a scored outfit; drawings can't show fit.
    - **Next:** port the v3 parts engine into `@kitted/core` with parity tests against `parts_proto.py`, then the parts-aware fix engine, then the sheet.
206. **The v3 parts engine is ported to `@kitted/core` with parity (Oct 11; not wired into the app).** `src/parts.ts` (`scoreParts(reading, {occasion, styles, boldness, secondLook})`, `createParts(data, rubric)`, `partsCardRow`), exported from `index.ts`. It returns what `parts_proto.score_parts()` returns: the five parts with score, half-point display, NA reason, reasons and tips in order; NA parts, weights used, condition, occasion, the earned bonus, the average and the final, plus the unrounded average and final.
    - **Data, versioned on its own:** `handoff/fixtures/parts.json` (version `1.0.0`, from `make_parts_json.py`, which reads TUNE, the sets and the module constants from `parts_proto.py` and transcribes the in-code fault and reward sizes) → `src/partsData.ts` (the same `gen-rubric-data.mjs`). Weights, reward and fault sizes, caps, ceilings, the O1 curve, the bonus and the TUNE block are all data; a tuning change is a data change (the tests run v1, v2 and the v3 what-if knobs from data alone). rubric.json stays 4.1.0 / engine 5; v4.1's code path is unchanged. The parts engine reuses v4.1's helpers (prepare/normalize, Outfit, the style role tables, half accent, denim) through a new read-only `RubricEngine.helpers`; `score()` doesn't use it.
    - **Parity:** `handoff/fixtures/make_parts_parity.py` → `parts_parity.json` (8.8 MB; repeated part / condition / occasion objects are stored once in a pool). **10,934 cases:** all 7,233 rubric_parity cases; the 78-reading corpus and the newest phone pull (16 checks) at every occasion with the paper tests' second look (1,410); batch 1 arms A and B1, A with B1's lightness, and zoom1 arm B with per-run or majority second looks (648); second-look variants on the edge readings (460); 139 parts edge cases that force every check, tip, NA reason, ceiling and early return (the generator asserts it); 1,044 tune-variant cases. **Result: 100%, and every unrounded average and final is bit-for-bit equal** (the test allows 1e-9). v4.1's own parity (7,233 cases, plus the quick tap) stays 100%. `npm run check` green.
    - **What had to be matched by hand:** Python feeds rounded values back into the arithmetic (each part's `round(x, 3)`, condition and occasion deductions and the ceiling at 3 places, the bonus at 4), so the port calls `pyRound` at exactly those points; the unrounded average isn't in Python's output, so the generator recomputes it with the public `parts_proto.combine` and asserts it matches the rounded fields; `str(float)` in "held to 4.0" vs `:g` in "Fit 5.5"; "already credited" is object identity on the engine's own copies.
    - **A prototype bug, reported, not fixed:** `parts_proto.py` raises `AttributeError` when a garment with no subcategory reaches a reason line (Color's quiet-accent / standout line, Style's standout line; 6 fuzz cases, never a real reading; the schema always sends a subcategory). The fixture stores the error and the TypeScript throws the same message, so the port changes nothing silently. Fixing it (fall back to "piece", as Condition already does) is a one-line Python change plus a regenerate, for whoever owns the prototype.
