    - **Not done on purpose:** nothing in the app calls it; the fix engine, the live scoring path and the Gemini prompt are untouched. Next: the parts-aware fix engine, then the Why sheet (#205).
207. **Parts engine: the missing-subcategory crash is fixed (reported by the port, #206).** The Color standout / quiet-accent lines and the Style standout line now fall back to "piece" (and "colour" / "patterned") in `parts_proto.py` and `parts.ts`, as Condition already did. Fixtures were regenerated (`make_parts_json.py`, `make_parts_parity.py`, `gen:rubric`): 0 error cases remain, parity is still exact and `npm run check` is green. No real reading was affected, because the schema always sends a subcategory.
208. **A fix engine for the parts engine (Oct 11; Python reference + TypeScript port with exact parity; not wired into the app). Report: `rubric/research/parts/fixes-results.md`.** Reference `handoff/fixtures/parts_fixes.py` (`suggest_parts_fixes`), port `@kitted/core` `src/partsFixes.ts` (`suggestPartsFixes`), catalog `handoff/fixtures/fixes.json` → `"parts"` (version 1.0.0, 28 candidates + 2 Next level lines; bundled as `src/partsFixesData.ts` by `gen-rubric-data.mjs`; `make_rubric_json.py` leaves the block out, so rubric.json is byte-identical).
    - **How:** every candidate is an edit to a copy of the scored garments: free actions (tuck, push sleeves, button cuffs, cuff the pants, steam, de-pill, clean the shoes, wear the jacket in your hand), closet swaps and a closet layer, generic swaps (5 shoes, 4 pants plus straight leg, a better fit, a neutral or solid color), a short jacket (bomber or denim, scored at the worse of the two) and one tailor hem. Each is re-scored by parts v3 with the same occasion and second look (plus a `look` override: a tuck hides a peek, a cuff lifts the hem off the shoe). **A fix only when the rounded score rises**, with the parts it raises ("Raises Shoes to 8", "Raises Proportions and Style", else "Clears the condition point" / "Fits the occasion better"). **A Smaller step** (one line, no number) when a part's half point rises, the score doesn't, and nothing drops; a smaller version of a shown fix's move first, then the largest raw lift. **Next level** (no number) only with no fix and no step, and only when its edit really lifts the named part. No fix: "No quick fixes for this one." ("Dialed in. Nothing to fix." at 9+, unreachable today).
    - **Order:** free, closet, "free if you own one", tailor; larger gain first in a class; one fix per group (a later class only with a strictly larger gain); max 3, max 1 tailor; first check: free only, else one. #162 kept: no added layer and no boots on warm days, no boots with shorts, never one everyday sneaker for another; gym/home: care and sleeve fixes only. New guard: no swaps on a lost shoe type or of closet pieces whose type was never read (`other`), which would switch on a part the photo couldn't judge.
    - **Dropped while building:** a front tuck (always beaten by the full tuck); "an overshirt or a knit" as the layer line (the parts engine gives nothing for just wearing a layer, so the line could never be true); plural-unsafe copy ("a neutral one" → "a neutral color").
    - **Results (empty closet, own occasion, test profile; founder profile pending, friends profile unknown, 703a8b and ebe23f unknown):** the mockup's check passes: d1967f "Swap the jeans for navy chinos → 8.0", Smaller step "Dark straight jeans lift Style to 7.5, but the score stays 7.0." The layer leads 1 of 26 cards (v4.1: 6) but appears on 12, because PR2 rewards a short jacket over full legs (+1.5); free tucks and steams lead where they apply. 8 of 26 checks have no fix (v4.1: 5); 7 of them show a Smaller step, 1 a Next level line.
    - **Parity:** `make_parts_fixes.py` → `parts_fixes.json` (4.4 MB, fix objects pooled): the corpus + newest pull (82 readings), 254 edge readings and 33 fix edges, at the 8 app occasions, warm on/off, empty and seed closet, plus first checks: **12,005 cases, all equal** (`test/partsFixes.parity.test.ts`; the generator asserts every candidate and both Next level lines appear). Copy: every line is in `app/src/i18n/en.ts` `pfix.*` under `addedInBuild`, held word for word to the catalog by `app/src/__tests__/partsFixCopy.test.ts` (with a body-words check). `npm run check` green.
    - **Left for later:** wording of the odd-but-true Smaller steps ("A cuff lifts Shoes to 8": the cuff makes the tee fit also read minimal; "Navy chinos lift Shoes to 8"); wiring into the card and the Why sheet (#205); re-check targeting for these edits (each fix carries its catalog `edits`). The founder's copy skim (the `pfix.*` lines).
210. **Style fairness pass: parts engine v4, opt-in (Oct 11; founder: "very good, regardless of the style, and also based on the style that the user picks"). Report: `rubric/research/parts/style-fairness.md`; test `extraction-test/parts4_test.py` → `results/parts4/`.**
    - **Audit (v3, the council's 57 rated outfits, made-up readings):** every style lands plain fits at about 7. Workwear (best 7.5), outdoor (7.0) and minimal (7.0) had no path to 8; vintage sport only through streetwear's boxy shape. The cause: a layer earns points only by shape (PR2), and PR2's short-jacket list is streetwear's (bomber, trucker, leather), while the other styles' deliberate fits are a chore coat, a shell or fleece, a track top. Every style over-scores its mistakes by about a point for the same reason (the long-top reading failed, `hem_at` 74%; the 5.0 floor), so that's not a style bias and isn't fixed here.
    - **v4 = v3 + three rewards, `engine="v4"`; v3 stays the default** (the TS parity and the #208 fix engine are on v3; v1-v3 checked identical on 15,705 results). PR5 "the workwear shape" (+1.0 Proportions): a chore coat over straight-or-fuller jeans or cargos, with boots (the whole boots kind, since boot type flips), in an outfit that reads as workwear (v5-silhouette §5, medium-strong). PR6 "the outdoor shape" (+0.5): a fleece or shell over a full leg with trail shoes (weak evidence; `trail_shoe` isn't a live value yet). The track jacket joins PR2's short layers (not a live value yet either).
    - **Results:** chore coat + jeans + boots 7.0 → 8.0; workwear deliberate 8+ 0/3 → 2/3; vintage sport 1/5 → 2/5; outdoor best 7.0 → 7.5 (still no 8); all anchors mean error −0.24 → −0.18. No real photo moves (0/78; the new checks fire on 0 saved readings in any run). Keep-targets met: blazer 7.0, the friend's chore coat 7.0 ×3 (8.0 with boots: the shape needs boots, per his ~7 call on sneakers), gingham unchanged, black tee 7.0, batch 1 49/49, panel +0.01, held-out unchanged, 8+ 6%.
    - **Not adopted:** PR5 at +0.5 (no path) or +1.5; field jacket, overshirt, chinos or sneakers in PR5 (sneakers lift the friend's chore coat to 7.5 and worsen the held-out gap); PR6 +1.0 (founder call); "the look's own shoe" detected with the shoe (`sc_pair`: lifts streetwear done badly 6.5 → 7.0).
    - **Picks (proposal only):** no score effect (no evidence raters judge by the wearer's stated style; the council ruled the same, #59-61; testable in the gold set like age). Fixes: "reads as his pick" breaks ties right after the shown gain (measured with the #208 engine: the lead fix changes on 4 of 329 reading×pick cards, never against a bigger gain). The Next level line follows his first pick's path to 8. Wording drafts need the founder's OK.
    - **Founder calls:** approve v4 as the next engine (then port + regenerate fixtures in one step); outdoor +0.5 vs +1.0; the picks proposal.
209. **The app scores new checks with the parts engine; the Why sheet is built around the parts; the score opens it (Oct 11, #205; commits 44d7a18, 6ded70a, 2414fa3).**
    - **Service:** `app/src/features/fitcheck/scoring.ts` has one switch, `DEFAULT_SCORING_ENGINE = 'parts'` (`setScoringEngine('v4')` rolls back). A new check is scored with `scoreParts` and gets its fixes from `suggestPartsFixes`; the row stores `rubricVersion: "parts-1.0.0"` and `details.parts` (the five parts with reasons, weights, condition, occasion, bonus, average, and `gainPart`, the part the card's move raises). The card's fix rows say which parts they raise ("Raises Shoes and Style · free if you own one"); the tip line is the Smaller step or the Next level line (`details.tipLead`).
    - **History is never re-scored:** `engineOf(row)` reads `rubricVersion`, so editing an old v4.1 check (a correction, the occasion, its re-check) keeps v4.1. A re-check uses its first check's engine, so the two numbers compare.
    - **On the parts engine:** corrections, the occasion re-score and its preview, partial picks and shoe snaps, re-checks (`partsRecheckOutcome`), "What changed?" (`applyPartsFix`), "Fix doesn't apply". Core adapter `src/partsCheck.ts` (outside the parity port).
    - **Still on v4.1 (written down):** the one quick tap picks its question with v4.1's swing (`askQuestion`/`askWithCloset`, parity-tested against rubric.py); its two answers' scores are recomputed with the parts engine, so a question can now ask with little or no parts swing. The re-check merge's "new problem" test still asks v4.1's rules. The seeded demo history is v4.1 rows.
    - **Tests:** the v4 story suites (fitCheckService, closetService, tap) and cardContent's expected_scores blocks assert v4.1 numbers, so they run on the v4 path via the switch; new `partsService.test.ts` covers the parts flows (p01b 8.0, ph8 6.5 → 7.5, corrections, occasion, partial, re-check, tap scores, an old check staying v4.1, rows adding up). No fixtures edited.
    - **Why sheet** (`features/why/PartsBreakdown.tsx`, `partsRows.ts`): the five-part strip (copper = most to gain; a list at accessibility sizes), one group per part (weight, number, reasons, a half-point rounding row when needed; Fit's "stops at 7" note), then Average of the parts (one decimal) · Strong in more than one part · Condition (fault or "looks clean") · Occasion (only when it holds the score down, "the parts alone make 6.6") · the floor · Reads as (unless Style already says it) · Rounding → Your score. Rows add up by construction.
    - **Card:** the score block is a button with a small chevron after "/10" (HIG: a chevron marks a disclosure/navigation target), VoiceOver "Score 8.0, Sharp." + hint "Shows the breakdown."; the "Reads as" line is gone from the card; "Why 8.0?" stays.
    - **Demo stories:** screen 7 (p01b at Date) is still 8.0 Sharp but has no fix (the parts engine gives nothing for wearing the held overshirt), so its card says "No quick fixes" with the Next level color line. **Screen 10's re-check story broke:** wearing the overshirt (p01a) scores 7.5, so it reads 8.0 → 7.5 with "Fixed and clean" (the overshirt hides the waist the tuck earned). Screen 11 (s24 partial) is 7.0 (was 6.5) with a Smaller step. Screen 35 (tap answered) is 6.5 with "Swap the slides for chunky sneakers → 7.0". The re-check copy "Fixed and clean" on a drop needs a parts-aware line; the screen-10 story needs a new before/after pair.
    - **Screenshots** (`screenshots/parts-app/`, git-ignored): s7-light, s7-dark, s8-light, s8-dark, s8-dark-bottom (the average → score rows), s10-light, s11-light, s35-light, s35-light-scrolled.
211. **Parts engine v4 is the default, and his picks shape the fixes, never the score (Oct 11; the main session approved v4 with outdoor at +0.5, #210).**
    - **v4 adopted:** `parts_proto.py` defaults to `engine="v4"` (v1-v3 stay reachable by name); parts data 1.1.0 (`make_parts_json.py`, engine v4); `parts.ts` ports PR5 "the workwear shape", PR6 "the outdoor shape", the track jacket in PR2's short layers and the `sc_pair` what-if knob, all numbers from `parts.json`'s TUNE block.
    - **A picks leak found and closed on the way:** H2 ("clearly X top to toe") read the "Reads as" label, which his picks choose on a tie, so a pick could add +0.5 to Style (5 of 25,048 reading × pick runs; e.g. a minimal + smart casual tie picked as smart casual, 7.3 → 7.7). v4 judges H2 on the label the outfit gets with no picks (`h2_pick_free`, off in v1-v3). Both generators now assert the score is identical under every picks they run, and `parts.parity.test.ts` checks it again on every parity reading (no picks, each single style, three picks, his own).
    - **What v4 changed against v3** (9,890 default cases that existed before): 30 cases, 22 with a new overall, all on hand-written readings; **0 of the real readings** (corpus, phone pull, batch 1, zoom1). PR5 on 9 (7.0/7.5 → 8.0, one 7.0 → 7.5 at a wedding), PR6 on 11 (7.0 → 7.5 on 3), the track jacket on 3 (7.5 → 8.0), the pick-free H2 on 7 (each had picks that won H2: 8.0 → 7.5 or 7.5 → 7.0; the same readings without picks are unchanged).
    - **Picks in the fix engine** (`parts_fixes.py` and `partsFixes.ts`): (a) the sort key is class, rounded gain, then the earliest pick the fixed outfit reads as, then raw gain; (b) Next level entries with a `style` are his first pick's path to 8, tried first, shown when the card has no fix and the edit really lifts the part and the edited outfit reads as that style, unless the Smaller step shown is the same move (`group`). Across the 4,390 picks cards (real readings and edges, each with none, every single style and two 3-pick sets) the lead fix changes on 59, never to a smaller gain (the test checks that class and gain at every position are the same as with no picks), e.g. oct7-ph1 with Streetwear: chunky sneakers before navy chinos, both +1.0; stock s05/s16 with Workwear: boots.
    - **New copy** (`fixes.json` "parts" 1.1.0 nextLevel; `en.ts` `pfix.next_*`): streetwear "A short jacket, like a bomber, gives it the streetwear shape and lifts Proportions. The score may not move yet."; workwear "A chore coat and boots give it the workwear shape and lift Proportions. …"; minimal "Tucking in the {name} gives it the minimal shape and lifts Proportions. …"; classic prep "A tucked shirt under a navy blazer gives it the classic prep line and lifts Proportions. …"; smart casual "Tucked into chinos, it takes the smart casual shape and lifts Proportions. …". Outdoor and vintage sport have no line (no readable path yet). The drafted "swap to boots for the full workwear shape" line was dropped: under v4 the boots swap is a real fix with a number (+1.0 on a chore coat over sneakers). The "Toward your workwear" tag wasn't built (wording waits on the founder).
    - **Parity:** `parts_parity.json` 12,133 cases (adds 19 v4 edge readings, the v3 engine as a tune variant, a v4 what-if variant, 756 picks cases); `parts_fixes.json` 17,300 cases (adds 9 pick fix edges and 4,390 picks cases). TypeScript equal on all of both. `npm run check` green; no app test changed (no demo reading moves under v4).
212. **The one quick tap asks by the parts score for parts checks (Oct 11; closes #209's "Still on v4.1").**
    - **What:** a parts-scored check picks its question with the parts engine's rounded score: `askQuestionParts` and `askWithClosetParts` (core `partsTap.ts`). Same candidates and rules as v4 (the 9 look-alike pairs and their doubt test, the shoe question only when the type is lost, never at Gym/Home or with shoes out of view, a question that could raise beats one that could lower, then the bigger swing; a known piece replaces the rubric's question on the same garment; his piece agreeing with the read drops it) and v4's bars, measured on the parts score: a pair asks at ≥ +1.0 with a doubt or ≤ −1.0 when not "clear"; the shoe question at ≥ +0.5; "Your green work boots?" at ≥ 0.5. Skip keeps what was read (a lost type: the best kind; a lost known shoe: his piece). Every option's score is the parts score of that answer, so the card shows what the button said.
    - **v4 untouched:** `askQuestion` (parity path) is unchanged; `askWithCloset` is now `askWithClosetBy(…, askQuestion, score)`, the same code with the engine passed in. Two read-only helpers were added to `RubricHelpers` (`tapDoubt`, `kindPick`) so the wrapper reuses v4's doubt test and boot pick. v4 checks keep v4's questions (`service.ts` picks by `engineOf(row)`); the old "v4 question, parts option scores" patch (and its wrong 3-option swing) is gone.

Jeans-or-sweatpants a765e8 and 6b14db still ask (swing +1.5 → +1.0: sweatpants 5.5, jeans 6.5); the lost-boots shoe question 8bc29d and f07193 still ask (+0.5, boots 7.0 vs 6.5), e026de's slides still ask (+1.0 → +0.5); b0f0c8 and d6559a stop asking (boots, sneakers or slides all give the same parts score). With his confirmed boots: "Your green work boots?" asks on f07193 and 8bc29d (+0.5, Yes 7.0 / No 6.5), no longer on b0f0c8 and d6559a. Tests: `kitted-core/test/partsTap.test.ts`, plus `partsService.test.ts` (the stored question is the parts one; Skip on a parts check scores the kept read and stores the answer).
213. **Style-crossing faults were missing, and "Going for" (founder, Oct 11).**
    - **The gap he spotted:** "oversized shirts and graphics are good in streetwear, but in minimal or classy they wouldn't stand." The parts engine scored an oversized graphic tee + pleated trousers + loafers, an oversized hoodie + slim chinos + loafers, and an oversized plain tee + slim chinos + loafers all at 7.0, against about 5.5–6 deserved. v4's style bands and vetoes didn't carry over into the parts. The fix (parts v5, a style-clash pass in Cohesion built from the council's vetoes) is running in its own session.
    - **His idea:** list style options after the upload so the app knows what he's going for. Claude's recommendation: show a pre-filled "Going for: X ⌄" chip on the result card, filled from the morning card or his first pick, so it costs no extra tap and he can change it like the occasion. It **steers the advice** ("For a minimal look, the graphic tee pulls the other way. Swap to a plain tee.") and the tie-break when an outfit reads as two styles. **It doesn't change the score**: renaming can't game it, and nobody is punished for telling the app more.
    - **History:** a per-check style menu existed (#61, #68) and gave way to "Reads as" (#85), because beginners can't name their style and a label-driven score invites gaming.
    - **Founder call:** should "Going for" ever lower the score when the outfit clearly misses that style? Default: advice only.
214. **Parts engine v5: a piece from another look (Oct 11; closes #213's style-crossing gap; report `rubric/research/parts/style-clash.md`, test `extraction-test/parts5_test.py` → `results/parts5/`).**
    - **The rule, SX1 on Style (−3.0):** a top that is a graphic tee (by subcategory, never the flip-prone `pattern`) or any top at volume ≥4, with at least two dressy pieces in the rest of the outfit (streetwear's vetoes plus chinos: trousers or chinos, loafers / derbies / dress shoes, a collared prep top, tailoring), where the rest reads as minimal, smart casual or classic prep and the top breaks every one of them. A graphic tee breaks all three; a roomy top breaks a look when the outfit no longer reads as it or its volume is above that look's band (smart casual and prep 3, minimal 4). The top must not be the look's own piece. SX1 replaces "reads as" (H1/H2) and takes the larger of itself and H3. Every condition comes from the council's tables (RULEBOOK Part 4), so it isn't new taste.
    - **Exempt:** a top under a layer that belongs to the quiet look (a blazer, topcoat, chore coat or leather jacket: an anchor or home piece in its table). A street layer (bomber, denim jacket, puffer) doesn't bridge. Every outer layer is exempt; shoes are never the odd piece (loafers with wide jeans are endorsed); a regular hoodie or sweatshirt isn't street-coded (Ivy sweatshirt + chinos + loafers; the two flip); an oversized top over straight or wide trousers reads minimal (city boy). A graphic tee with only one dressy piece in a minimal rest is a tip (`SX1_tip`), no points.
    - **Size:** at Style's 15% the existing −1.5 moves the total 0.225 and leaves all three founder cases at 7.0; −2.5 is the first size that moves them (6.70, just under the rounding line); −3.0 lands them at 6.5 with margin. 6.0 would need Style at its floor. **Founder call:** keep 6.5, or add a Proportions charge for a roomy top over slim pants (thin evidence: sources name skinny) to bring the two oversized-over-slim cases to 6.0.
    - **Results:** the oversized graphic tee + pleated trousers + loafers, the oversized hoodie + slim chinos + loafers and the oversized plain tee + slim chinos + loafers go 7.0 → 6.5 (Style 4). 29 crossing outfits behave as intended (10 faults, 2 tips, 17 unchanged, incl. the tee under an unstructured blazer, the hoodie under a topcoat, the jersey under a blazer, the city-boy boxy tee). **No real photo moves (0/78), no council outfit moves (0/57 rated, 0/39 others), steadiness 49/49, panel +0.01, held-out 26/26 within 0.5, 8+ 6%, picks never move a score.**
    - **Fixes:** two new catalog lines, `top_plain_tee` "Swap the {name} for a plain tee" and `top_regular_fit` "Swap the {name} for a regular fit" (fixes.json "parts" 1.2.0; `en.ts` `pfix.*`). The shoe side ("Swap the loafers for white leather sneakers") is the existing line, which now fires because one dressy piece no longer triggers SX1. Each founder case shows three +0.5 fixes. Wording oddity left for the copy skim: on the slim-chino cases "Swap the chinos for navy chinos" leads, because that swap also sets a straight leg ("Swap to straight-leg chinos" gives the same gain but loses the raw-gain tie). 0 real fix cards change.
    - **Shipped:** v5 is the default (`parts_proto.py`; v4 by `engine="v4"`); parts data 1.2.0; `parts.ts` `styleClash`; `parts_parity.json` 13,027 cases (17 SX1 edges, v4 and a v5 what-if as tune variants, `v5_vs_v4`: 39 hand-written cases changed, 0 real); `parts_fixes.json` 18,131 cases. TypeScript equal on every case of both; `npm run check` green. New checks store `parts-1.2.0`; an older parts check edited later is re-scored by v5, which changes no saved reading.
    - **A council ruling partly reversed, on the founder's word:** RULEBOOK Part 3 ("mixing styles is never a mistake") and ruling 2 ("detection never adds a penalty") now have one narrow exception, this one. Every other mix still costs nothing.
    - **Founder calls:** the size (above); an oversized graphic tee under a blazer (kept as no fault; FashionBeans 2025 dissents); the one-dressy-piece graphic tee (tip only, or a fault?).
215. **"Going for" is asked during the wait (founder, Oct 11: "I like the during-the-wait question").** His notes on v1:
    - it doesn't look like it pops up during a wait
    - two picks (the look and the occasion) in about 7 s is tight

    **v2 is being mocked** (the same file, `mockups/going-for.html`). The question sits inside the reading card under the progress and slides in with its own "While I read it" treatment. The look is the main question, and the occasion is one line ("Headed: Everyday ⌄"). Both are pre-picked. The score never waits: if it lands first, the question collapses into "Going for minimal · Everyday · Change" at the top of the result. A change of look updates the advice instantly; a change of occasion uses the existing re-score confirm.

    **His third point, "the app should follow the rules that are listed, it shouldn't just guess":** after the style-clash fix lands, Claude writes a plain-words rule list per style (`rubric/v5-styles.md`), plus a conformance test per written rule (an outfit that must pass or fail), so a broken rule fails the tests. The AI only names garments; code applies the written rules.
216. **The style rules the app follows, written down and tested (Oct 11; founder: "it should follow the rules that are listed, it shouldn't just guess"; closes #215's third point).** `rubric/v5-styles.md` is the plain-words rulebook of the parts engine as it runs today (v5, parts data 1.2.0), written from `parts_proto.py` and its data, not from the research. **121 rules, each with an ID:** shared 64 (how a score is made 6, Proportions 10, Fit 6, Color 7, Shoes 12, Style 8, Condition 4, Occasion 5, Loungewear 6), streetwear 14, workwear 9, vintage sport 7, outdoor 6, minimal 8, classic prep 5, smart casual 5, no clear style 3. Each style section says how it's recognized, what it rewards (with the points and what they're worth on the score), what it allows, what it marks down (incl. the #214 crossing fault) and what it can't see yet.
    - **Tests:** `extraction-test/rules_conformance.py` holds 256 made-up outfits (live reading values only, except 2 marked "future": the track jacket and trail shoes; only the app's 8 occasions; no person, no picks unless the case says so, boldness "a little", rule 7) with assertions per rule: the check that must or mustn't fire and by how much, part scores, the band, the label, ceilings, and paired outfits that must move a part by an exact amount. It writes `handoff/fixtures/rules_conformance.json`; `kitted-core/test/rulesConformance.test.ts` runs the same cases through `scoreParts` with the same assertion rules, plus equality with Python's numbers. Test names start with the rule ID. The script also fails if the doc defines a rule with no case or a case cites a rule not in the doc. All 256 pass in both; `npm run check` green. **The engine wasn't changed.**
    - **No case showed the engine breaking a written rule.** Writing the rules from the code did surface 16 differences from the research, listed in the doc's "Known differences". The ones that matter most: (KD-3) the council's per-style bands and excuses aren't in the parts engine at all, so nothing is ever charged as too baggy and a roomy top over *slim* legs costs nothing; (KD-4) "clearly X top to toe" (+1.0 Style) is reachable only for streetwear, smart casual and a suit, because the other styles' tables have no signature bottoms or shoes the reading can name; (KD-5) adding a prep marker (a cardigan, a colored oxford) renames an outfit classic prep and can cost that +1.0 (Style 8.5 → 7.5 on the same oxford, chinos and loafers); (KD-16) the Event occasion still uses the interview band, though a softer band was approved (#197); (KD-15) the app doesn't send the second look, so "a shirt showing below the jacket" never fires on a real check. The rest are reading limits already known (boxy hoodies, stacks, lightness, trail shoes, accessories, tips-only faults, Fit capped at 7) and the open founder calls from #214.
    - **Claude's call:** fix candidates only, not built: KD-5 (judge top to toe on any style the outfit reads as, not only the named one) and KD-16 (Event's band), each through the paper tests and the gold set first, since they move scores. From now on, a rule change edits `v5-styles.md` and its cases in the same commit.
217. **Parts engine v6: three known differences from `rubric/v5-styles.md` fixed (Oct 11; founder: "go ahead"; report `rubric/research/parts/v6.md`, test `extraction-test/parts6_test.py` → `results/parts6/`).** v6 is the default (`parts_proto.py`, parts data 1.3.0, `parts.ts`); v5 stays reachable with `engine="v5"`.
    - **KD-5 (a bug):** "clearly X top to toe" (+1.0 Style) is judged on every style the outfit reads as (the detected set, which picks never change), not only the label (`h2_any`). The cardigan over an oxford with chinos and loafers went 7.0 → 7.5, the plain oxford outfit's score (Style 8.5 both); a pink oxford with chinos and loafers 7.5 → 8.0 (it also has the standout color). The line names the pick-free label. Only smart-casual-plus-another-look outfits can gain (KD-4 unchanged for workwear, vintage sport, outdoor).
    - **KD-16:** Event expects 3–4 (the casual wedding's band), not the interview's 3.5–4.5 (`occ_target`; the default #197 approved). Only the too-casual deduction and ceiling move, by half a level: no deduction from a mean of 2.75 (was 3.25); the ceiling starts below 2.25 at 7.0, 6.0 at 2.0, 5.0 at 1.5, 4.0 at 1.0, 3.5 at 0.5 (was 6.0 at 2.5 … 3.5 at 1.0). Slides and loungewear at an event stay held to 4.0, the untucked dress shirt −0.6, no floor. A hoodie, dark jeans and white sneakers at an event: 6.0 → 7.0. On the real photos scored at Event: 42 of 78 rise, none drop.
    - **KD-3:** the council's per-style bands come back as one Proportions fault, **PB1 "roomier than the look", −1.5** (the council's own step price: 0.45 on the score), read only on steady groupings (volume ≥4 vs ≤3; narrow vs straight-or-fuller legs). The bands become (top, leg) boxes built from `STYLE_BANDS`, default always included (widen only): a roomy top worn alone is held over full legs by streetwear, minimal, workwear, vintage sport and outdoor, over slim legs only by outdoor. Exempt: tucked or ending at the belt over a full leg, full-leg pressed trousers (city boy), under a layer, loungewear, skinny legs (PF5), sleeves over the hands (FF5), and whenever SX1 fires (one fact, one charge: the founder's three crossing outfits stay 6.5). The leg side (wide for smart casual, prep, no style) sits on relaxed vs wide, not steady: **PB2, a tip only**. Not carried (KD-17): 4 vs 5 and 1 vs 2 volume, streetwear's −0.2 hoodie cuffs, a roomy jacket against the leg, a half tuck.
    - **Results:** 0 of 78 real photos move at their own occasion (PB1 fires on none; one batch-1 run of web m03 takes it, steadiness 49/49); council: 1 of 72 moves (la-overshirt-chinos-wls 7.0 → 7.5, advocate 8.75), every per-style row unchanged; blazer 7.0, chore coat 7.0 ×3, gingham unchanged, tee + wide jeans 7.0; panel +0.01, held-out 26/29, 8+ 6%; picks move nothing (226 readings × 9 pick sets). The band outfits: an oversized tee over slim jeans or over straight jeans with no look 7.0 → 6.5; an oversized oxford over slim chinos 7.5 → 7.0; over wide jeans (streetwear), wide trousers (city boy) or tucked: unchanged. Sizes −0.75/−1.0 never show on a plain 7.0; −3.0 breaks steadiness.
    - **Shipped:** `parts_parity.json` 14,273 cases (40 new v6 edges, v5 and a v6 what-if as tune variants, `v6_vs_v5`: 350 changed, all hand-written or real readings at Event, plus m03's one run); `parts_fixes.json` 19,811 cases (0 of 82 real fix cards change at their own occasion); TypeScript equal on all. `rubric/v5-styles.md`: new [PRO-11], [PRO-12], [OCC-6], [WRK-10], [VSP-8], [OUT-7], [SMC-6], rewritten [STY-2], [STR-11], [MIN-5], [MIN-6], [PRP-4], [GEN-2]; KD-3, KD-5, KD-16 removed, KD-17 added; `rules_conformance.py` 290 outfits, 128 rules, all pass in Python and TS. `rubric.ts` exposes `legExcused` read-only (v4.1 untouched). No new fix copy (existing lines cover it). `npm run check` green. New checks store `parts-1.3.0`.
218. **"Going for" v2 built (Oct 11; founder: "go ahead" on `mockups/going-for.html` v2, #215). Screenshots `screenshots/going-for-app/` (git-ignored); demo screens 71–78.**
    - **The wait:** once the first piece is found (or 1.2 s in), the reading card grows an inset under the stages ("While I read it", copper dot; it rises 22 pt on a 450 ms no-bounce spring and fades in; a 200 ms fade under Reduce Motion). "What are you going for?" with his up-to-3 setup picks as compact drawn tiles (the onboarding drawings, `LookTile`'s `Outfit`) plus Not sure; More (MorphPicker) holds the other looks and Something else (an iOS text prompt; his words are kept, the advice runs as Not sure); "Headed: Everyday ⌄" replaces the old occasion chips and grows into the same morph menu (`MorphPicker` got a `prefix` mode). The skeleton numeral folds away to give the room; at AX sizes the stages shrink to the current one ("Checking fit · 2 of 4") and tiles become 68 pt rows. A tap adds a check in place ("Got it: minimal. I'll aim the tips there."), a selection haptic, nothing moves. The score never waits. Gym and Home: no look question (only Headed). Re-checks and the same photo again: no question; a re-check keeps its first check's answer.
    - **The pre-pick (Claude's call where the brief and the mockup differed):** the morning card's look only when he opened the camera from the card (the mockup's rule), else his last confirmed look within 7 days (kv `goingFor.last`), else his first pick from setup, else Not sure. One line says where it came from in words ("Both from this morning's card." / "From your last check." / "Your first pick from setup."). So a plain Check my fit no longer takes today's card style (the #61 reason, "both camera paths score the same", is gone: picks never move a score); `fitCheckService.test.ts` was updated for that.
    - **The result:** the inset folds into one line at the top, "Going for minimal · Everyday · Change" (tag icon = picked for him, check = he chose); the occasion left the tagline. Change grows the inset back in place and scrolls the card's top into view. A new look rewrites only the advice (`changeGoingFor`: new fixes order, Next level / Smaller step, summary, `gainPart`; the score columns, parts, Reads as and Why rows are never written) with a copper wash and "Tips updated for smart casual." for VoiceOver; a new occasion keeps the #62 confirm ("Re-score for interview? Same photo. It would be 6.0 instead of 7.5."). VoiceOver: the result card stays one element with a new "Change what you're going for" action (an action sheet of looks); while Change is open the card ungroups so the tiles are reachable.
    - **Stored:** `details.goingFor` {style, confirmed, source, bothFromCard, other} with `row.style` mirroring it, and `details.goingForAdvice`. The fix engine gets the look as `styles` (the existing #211 tie-break and Next level).
    - **The advice (core `goingFor.ts`, new; `parts.ts` untouched, only exports read):** a match is "the detector finds the look" with no picks ("It reads that way too."). A mismatch says "Reads as {style}." ("It doesn't read as one look yet." for none) and names a piece only when a written rule turns it away: SX1's clashing top (for the quiet looks it breaks), a piece the look's table counts against it (role negative) when the rest reads that way without it, or a piece whose plain twin (no graphic/logo/pattern/designed wear, neutral color, regular cut) makes the whole outfit read that way. Types the reading couldn't name ("other") are never named. The move is a parts-catalog candidate (free, closet, own; never the tailor), applied with `applyPartsFix`, that makes the outfit read as the look and doesn't lower the score; the catalog's own line for the piece's type goes first. When the move is already a fix on the card it isn't drawn twice: "Fix 1 also takes it toward smart casual." No number on the move.
    - **Results (stock photos, profile unknown; test picks Minimal, Streetwear, Smart casual):** s14 (white sweater, denim jacket, drawstring trousers, chunky sneakers) reads minimal: going minimal matches; going smart casual: "Reads as minimal." + the white-leather-sneakers move (also fix 1). s15 going minimal: "Reads as smart casual. For a minimal look, the overshirt pulls the other way." (no move: going plain would cost the plaid's points). The #213 made-up outfit (oversized graphic tee, trousers, loafers) going minimal: "the graphic tee pulls the other way" + "Swap the graphic tee for a plain tee". On his 11 Oct 8 phone readings (test profile) most mismatches name no piece; two do (the logo hoodie and the orange oxford, going minimal).
    - **Tests:** core `goingFor.test.ts` (7), app `goingFor.test.ts` (12: pre-pick order, the wait's tap, every demo reading × every look × 4 occasions gives the same score/raw/parts, Change keeps the Why rows equal, the occasion confirm, the words). `npm run check` green (358 app tests).
    - **Not built / differs from the mockup:** the fold into the line is a cross-fade, not a shape morph; the card's own height change isn't on the spring (the stages and pills are); the Headed menu opens full width (the row is the hit area). Copy is in `en.ts` `addedInBuild` (`gf.*`, `card.a11y.changeLook`) for the founder's skim. Open founder call unchanged: should "Going for" ever lower the score? Default: advice only.
219. **His Oct 11 re-check of the identical photo (6.5, then 7.0): four fixes (Oct 11; the main session's diagnosis; paper test `extraction-test/parts7_test.py` → `results/parts7/`).** The check: photo af7e93eb at 13:26–13:27 (pull `screenshots/phone/20261011-133011`), a green zip hoodie over a cream striped oxford whose hem shows, light-wash wide jeans, black/white Dunk-style sneakers, at Class, going for streetwear. Founder profile still pending (rule 7): scored with the test profile and with his streetwear pick; picks never move a number, so both agree.
    - **(1) A re-check of the same photo never calls the AI** (`service.ts` `samePhotoAs` / `finalizeSamePhoto`). When the re-check's image hash equals its first check's (or any check's that day), the matched check's reading is reused, so the score is the same; nothing is counted (the re-check stays open, no quota, no closet sighting), and the card says "That's the same photo as before, so nothing changed. Make the fix, then take a new photo." with a **Take a new photo** button (`en.ts` `recheck.samePhoto*`, `card.a11y.recheckSame`, `addedInBuild`; stored as `details.recheck.samePhoto`). The re-check's raw read now keeps its model (it was logged "demo-bundled"). Demo screen 23 (S21, "change not seen") now re-checks with `p01b2`, a second shot of p01b's outfit with its own hash, since the identical photo no longer re-reads. Tests: `samePhotoRecheck.test.ts` (4).
    - **(2) Parts engine v7: a condition read counts only on a garment read "clear"** (`TUNE["v7"]["cond_clear_only"]`, parts data 1.4.0; v6 stays reachable by name). On a "likely" garment, wrinkles, wear or a stain move no points anywhere (the Condition deduction, ruling 46's lost credit, the shoes' "look worn", the floor's test) and become a Condition tip: "the oxford shirt might be wrinkled; not counted, it isn't clear enough in the photo" (the Why sheet's Condition row shows it at ±0). **Steadiness, batch 1 arm A (Everyday, test profile): 49/49 within 0.5 before and after; exact 40/49 → 39/49** (phone-5a9049's three runs go 7.0/7.0/7.0 → 7.0/7.0/7.5: its boots' wear is read "likely" on one run and nothing on another). zoom1 arm B 66/66, exact 57/66, unchanged. Honest finding: on batch 1 the condition flips sit mostly on garments read "clear" (4 of 7 flipping garments), so v7 only removes the "likely" kind, like his oxford. Real photos at their own occasion: 1 of 78 moves (stock s14, "likely" wrinkled chinos, 7.0 → 7.5, which costs the held-out MAE 0.43 → 0.45; within 0.5 of the panel stays 26/29); panel gap +0.02; 8+ 6%; council cases 0 of 110 move.
    - **v4.1's F7 left as is, on purpose:** v4.1 now scores only old checks, and matching it would move 5 of 78 real photos' saved v4.1 scores when edited (b0f0c8, 5a9049, 7f14a4, s14, s20). Rubric stays 4.1.0.
    - **(3) "Your White tee?" can't be asked about a striped oxford** (core `knownMatches`: a known piece is never a candidate when its pattern differs from the read (solid vs any pattern, or two different patterns; "other" differs only from solid; unknown never blocks; not on shoes, whose pattern reads are noise) or its collar family differs (collared shirts and polos vs tees and knits; hoodie vs tee stays askable, #115)). `knownPieces` now carries `pattern` (the tile's drift-guarded one, else the item's read). Both `askWithCloset` and `askWithClosetParts` go through it. Tests: core `oct11Recheck.test.ts` (his readings, `test/data/phone-oct11-af7e93.json`, no photo) and app `knownPattern.test.ts` (his White tee row).
    - **(4) A hoodie or sweatshirt worn as the outer layer is a streetwear signature piece** (`rubric.py` `ST_OUT_A` += hoodie, sweatshirt; it already was one as the top). A list hole like #183, so no rubric version bump. His fit now reads as streetwear: **7.5 on all four stored readings** (first read, tap answer, re-check raw and merged; raw 7.7 each, Style 8.5, Shoes 8), was 6.5 / 7.0. **v4.1 parity: 3 of 7,233 cases move, all one hand-written fuzz reading** (jeans as the top under a hoodie, at the interviews: 4.5 → 5.5, 4.0 → 4.5, 5.0 → 6.0); **0 parts parity cases move from this list alone**, and no saved real reading had a hoodie read as the outer layer before his.
    - **Every earlier target kept:** his friends' blazer 7.0, chore coat 7.0 ×3, his tee + wide jeans 7.0 ×2, gingham unchanged, council plain fits at 6.5–7.5 and deliberate 8s unchanged, steadiness 49/49, panel +0.02, held-out within 0.5 26/29 (MAE +0.02, s14 above), 8+ 6%, picks move nothing (193 readings × 9 pick sets).
    - **Fixtures regenerated** (`make_readings`, `make_seed`, `make_expected`, `make_rubric_json`, `make_parity`, `make_parts_json`, `make_parts_parity`, `make_parts_fixes`, `rules_conformance.py`, `gen:rubric`): `parts_parity.json` 14,827 cases (newest pull now Oct 11, 8 v7 condition edges, v6 as a tune variant; `v7_vs_v6`: 383 cases changed, 198 with a new overall: hand-written readings, s14 at every occasion, and batch-1 runs of 5a9049, 8bc29d and f07193); `parts_fixes.json` 20,191 cases (real cards at their own occasion: s14 loses "Steam" and leads with the low-profile sneakers, 5a9049's fixes reorder, b0f0c8 and s20 change only their tip line). `rubric/v5-styles.md` → v7: new [CON-5], [CON-1] and [STR-1] reworded, KD-12 noted; `rules_conformance.py` 298 outfits, 129 rules (a new `cond_tips` assertion, mirrored in `rulesConformance.test.ts`). TypeScript equal on every case. `npm run check` green (595 core, 366 app).
220. **Agreement test: trusting a proportion fact only when independent readers agree doesn't unlock any proportion rule (Oct 11; founder: "I think we have not put proportion into account"; report `rubric/research/parts/agreement-test.md`, script `extraction-test/agree_test.py` → `results/agree1/`). Paper test, no API, no engine or app change.** Three readers on batch 1's 50 photos: (a) the scoring call (arm A, 3 runs), (b) the second look (look1 U/L, 3 runs), (c) the phone (the fit ruler; the hem probe for a heavy hem; base / 1600 px / 3% crop as its three runs; re-run locally on the 7 newer phone photos). Rule 7: founder profile pending, friends profile unknown, 703a8b/ebe23f unknown, stock/web profile unknown; one test profile, so no age or look split.
    - **No fact passes under "2 of 3" or "all agree":** agreement lifts precision to 100% (heavy hem, long layer, rests on the shoe) but halves coverage and drops steadiness to 76–84% (bar 90%), because each reader flips on different runs. The readers' errors are misses, not false alarms: the scoring call's "full break" is right 30/30 but finds 36% of heavy hems; (a) and (c) never see a peek or a long top, so "agree" there means (b) alone; the phone answers on ~40%. The friend's blazer stack (d1967f) is "not heavy" to the second look (3/3) and the phone; the stored reading says slight break.
    - **Single readers that pass:** "rests on the shoe" from the second look alone (97%, 92% steady, 29 photos, 20 his/friends'; 77% against the Oct 9 "covers" labels). The hem probe's heavy hem passes on 38 photos (94%) but its cut is in-sample and it's 86% on all 50: not trusted.
    - **Rescoring the 79 real photos (scratch wrapper around v7):** under any agreement rule, 0 Proportions and 0 shown scores move; Proportions stays exactly 7 on 57/74; steadiness 49/49; panel +0.02, held-out within 0.5 26/29. Even Claude's labels as perfect facts move Proportions on 7 and the score on 3: today's rules are narrow (a heavy hem costs only under a blazer, ruling 13; no labelled long top exists; the long layers are tailored and exempt).
    - **The judges' gap on his photos is mostly the bunched hem on his wide jeans:** Proportions +0.72 above the judges' proportion part (all his at a flat 7); the label ceiling +0.55; a what-if "heavy hem on loose jeans −1.0" with label facts +0.10, but on the scoring call's own hem +0.53 with worse ranking and steadiness (Proportions exact 44/49). Not proposed.
    - **Claude's calls:** no new rule turned on; F1/F2/F3/F10/HB stay tips; the score spread is unchanged. Recommended keeping the second look off for scoring (+0.24¢ a check moves no shown score on 79 photos; only 1f1997's Shoes 4.5 → 4.0); the founder's call. New founder taste call: should a heavy or stacked hem on loose jeans (no blazer) ever cost points (overturns ruling 13)? Default no until the gold set's hem pairs. Candidate for the gold set: "rests on the shoe under a tailored jacket" as F1's stand-in (fires on d1967f, not on ph3/s07; n too small).
221. **Stacked hems on baggy jeans never cost points (founder, Oct 12: "it shouldn't cost a point for baggy jeans that stack up").** Ruling 13 stands: a deliberate stack or full break on loose jeans and cargos is fine in every style. It still costs points under a tailored jacket (rule F1), and pooling on narrow legs stays a Fit fault. That closes the #220 taste call.
    - **Next, in a new chat (founder, Oct 12):** research and build phone-side scripts that measure proportion directly (where the top and the outer layer end, where sleeves and cuffs end, how the pant hem meets the shoe), instead of relying on the AI's weakest fields. The brief is in CLAUDE.md "Next steps" → "Proportion scripts (new chat)".
222. **Proportion scripts, step 1: which facts are worth measuring, and with what (Oct 12; the proportion-scripts chat). Report `rubric/research/parts/proportion-headroom.md`, script `extraction-test/headroom_test.py` → `results/headroom1/`. Paper test, no API, no engine or app change.**
    - **Headroom (parts v7, 79 real photos, one test profile; founder profile pending, friends/stock profile unknown):** a perfect reader of every proportion fact (Claude's blind labels swapped in) moves only **4 of 79 shown scores** (one is the founder's: f07193, jacket sleeves read as covering the hands, 6.5 → 7.0). Where the AI is wrong (hem slight vs full break, his hoodies "mid fly" vs at the belt), the rules don't use the difference (#221; PRO-4 gives a hoodie the waist break only at roominess 4+). Where the rules are sensitive (pooling, covers-hand, tuck), the AI is mostly right. So **Proportions sits at 7.0 because of the rules, not the readings.**
    - **Worth a script, in order:** (1) leg shape at slim vs straight-or-fuller (20/77 photos move on a one-step flip; 8 of the AI's 14 label disagreements cross a line the rules use; the leg probe #189 looked promising there); (2) a "boxy short top" measure (top length together with roominess; the only path to the waist break for his hoodies; roominess has no labels yet); (3) cheap veto guards that can only remove a "pooling" or "sleeves cover the hands" fault. **Not worth a script under today's rules:** outer-layer length, rests on the shoe, outer roominess, tuck, the peek.
    - **Public labels (licenses checked from primary sources):** only **Fashionpedia** is commercial-safe (annotations CC BY 4.0; images keep their source licenses, internal use only). Val split: 1,158 photos, ~20% men, with garment/sleeve/shoe masks, length labels and straight / wide-leg / tight / baggy silhouettes; 236 MB images + 14.5 MB labels. No hem-break labels anywhere. DeepFashion(2), DeepFashion-MultiModal, ModaNet, Sapiens: research-only or non-commercial.
    - **Segmenters:** no commercial-safe ready-made clothing parser (SCHP/LIP research-only; SegFormer clothes models under NVIDIA's non-commercial license). Usable: Apple's Core ML **SAM 2.1 Tiny** (Apache 2.0, iOS 26) prompted with the closet call's boxes; Apple's own box-prompted `GenerateIterativeSegmentationRequest` is iOS 27+ and not in this Mac's Xcode 26.6 SDK. Deferred: it would serve top/layer/hem positions, which the headroom says don't move scores today.
    - **Next:** with the founder's OK, download Fashionpedia val and use its men's pants (straight / slim-tight / wide) as truth for the leg probe's slim-vs-straight test.
223. **The leg probe fails slim vs straight on public truth; outline-width leg shape is dropped for good (Oct 12; founder OK'd the Fashionpedia val download, 250 MB, `inputs/fashionpedia/`, git-ignored; report `rubric/research/notes/fp-leg-test.md`, script `extraction-test/fp_leg_test.py` → `results/fpleg1/`).** 95 full-length labelled pants (41 "tight (fit)", 49 "straight", 5 wide; leggings, sweatpants, peg, crop, flare and bootcut left out; Fashionpedia's own boxes), the #189 probe unchanged. Hem ÷ knee with the cut frozen on our photos (0.765): **56%** right (29/51), unsure 43%; leave-one-out on Fashionpedia 68%; standing poses only 58–63%; opening ÷ shoe 45%. Medians differ (0.69 vs 0.83) but the spreads overlap almost fully. #189's 92% on 12 photos was luck. Public photos, profile unknown (rule 7); no gender field, so all wearers.
    - **Claude's call:** leg shape stays the AI's reading; no phone measurement replaces or vetoes it, and it isn't re-tested on the gold set. With #222, no proportion script is worth building under today's rules: the remaining candidates are a boxy-short-top measure (needs roominess labels from the gold set) and two veto guards that would change about 2 scores. **Proportion scripts are paused until the gold set exists**; the real lever for Proportions sitting at 7.0 is the rules (what earns or costs shape points), set by the gold set.
224. **Gold set, first run: the app barely separates good fits from bad ones (Oct 12; founder supplied 226 screenshots in `inputs/ig`, `inputs/pinterest`, `inputs/reddit` and OK'd the Gemini spend; report `rubric/research/notes/gold-set-1.md`, scripts `extraction-test/gold_read.py`, `gold_compare.py` → `results/gold1/`).** 225 photos (one refused for two people), parts v7 with the live reading config, 2 runs ($2.69; 3 runs would have been ~$3.85, over the approved $2–3). Panel: judge brief v3 (`inputs/gold-judge/BRIEF.md`: the whole scale, never body/age/setting), 5 lenses on 3 models (Fable street, Opus tailoring + workwear, Sonnet minimal + peer), batches of 40 with 15 hidden repeats; the first single-batch attempt hit the image-view limit (a judge scored 74 photos unseen) and was discarded (`inputs/gold-judge/attempt1/`). Rule 7: all profile unknown, one test profile, Everyday.
    - **Panel:** repeats within 0.5 on 13–15/15 per judge; sd 1.02 (pilot 0.62); judges r 0.92–0.96 (agreeing models may share AI taste: a human check of 20 photos comes before tuning on it).
    - **App vs panel:** r 0.33; app sd 0.55; the panel's ≤5.5 fits get 6.65 from the app, its 8–8.5 fits 7.23; Pinterest bad fits +0.88 too generous, Instagram creators −0.51 too harsh. Same person, different outfits (13 series, 67 photos): r 0.38. Parts: shoes r 0.61, cohesion 0.21, proportions 0.18, fit 0.04, color −0.18.
    - **Claude's read:** the readings name the garments; the rules miss what makes them good or bad (size faults, loud patterns, pieces from different worlds, a mismatched near-suit) and over-punish slides and sweats under a deliberate outfit. Next is rules tuned against the panel (fit, color, cohesion first), not phone scripts.
    - **Privacy:** ig-098 (head off the top edge) was sent with mouth and chin unhidden; the app does the same today (fix offered as its own task). rd-021: the blur covers most of the shirt.

225. **Market research and positioning (Oct 12; founder: "figure out how I'm supposed to do market research… how this app should get positioned"). Report: `market/market-research.md`, with 4 detail files in `market/`.** Four research agents ran (direct raters, closet and AI-stylist apps, market size and free substitutes, benchmarks and App Store search). App Store counts come from Apple's pages and API. Claude also pulled 813 recent public reviews (Dressly, Umax, Pureple, GRWM) from Apple's review feed and spot-checked the key claims: Twelve70's subtitle, Looksee's paid First Fix, ChatGPT try-on on Oct 1, 2026, Whering's $7M round (Jul 7, 2026) and its planned photo scanner, and Fitted's photo-to-closet feature.
    - **Findings:**
      - No leader: GRWM, the biggest pure outfit rater, has 1,866 US ratings, and 64 of 107 name-matched raters launched in 2026, mostly with 0 ratings.
      - Free ChatGPT is the main substitute.
      - Young men already pay for face-rating looksmax apps (~157K ratings).
      - Competitors' bad reviews are mostly paywall and billing complaints (charges, paywalls or cancelling in 42% of Dressly's, 58% of Umax's and 67% of GRWM's reviews pulled).
      - "Score + fix + re-check" is already sold (DRPD, Looksee), and the closet from outfit photos is live at Fitted (Whering plans it).
      - $29.99/yr is below the North America median of $39.99; plan on ~2% of downloads paying and ~25% of yearly subscribers renewing (RevenueCat 2026).
    - **Claude's calls (marketing wording, not product):**
      - **Lead order:** score + one fix → same photo, same score → it rates the outfit, never you → the daily check is free → it learns your closet (retention, not headline).
      - Say "fit check" in videos.
      - No "pocket stylist" in public copy (Twelve70's subtitle).
      - No "accurate" / "expert" claims until the gold set backs the score (r 0.33, #224).
      - Don't compete on try-on.
      - Beachhead: college guys and new grads at a "new chapter" moment.
    - **Founder's calls (on the to-do list):** the App Store name and subtitle (default `Kitted: Outfit Rater for Men` / `Score your fit. Get the fix.`), and $5.99 vs $7.99 monthly (default keep).
    - **Founder's research tasks:** 10 interviews (Mom Test script in the report), a competitor teardown with the same photo twice in each app and in ChatGPT, a TikTok scan by hand, and a Codex Reddit prompt.
    - The handles and domain task moves from P2 to P1.

226. **Niche, problem statement and differentiation written down; Fitted reviewed (Oct 12; founder: "the good thing about our app is the fit check, and you don't have to upload all your clothes… it picks it up as you rate and then gives you suggestions").** Added to `market/market-research.md` §5.1–5.3 and §4.2, and to `market/competitors-adjacent.md` → Fitted.
    - **Niche:** a fit check for guys who want to dress better but don't know where to start (18–30; beachhead 18–25 at a "new chapter" moment).
    - **Problem (short):** guys can't tell whether their outfit works or what to change, and there's no quick, honest, judgment-free way to find out.
    - **Differentiation:** Claude's correction to the founder's framing: "no uploading" alone isn't unique (Fitted's Fit Pic Magic does it; Whering has announced it). The unique part is the joined loop: fit check → score + one fix → the closet fills as a side effect → suggestions from owned clothes → check that fit. One line: "the only fit check that learns your closet while it rates your fits". Lead with the fit check; the closet is the reason to stay.
    - **Fitted facts** (lookup API, 180 reviews, the Superwall post re-read):
      - 1,157 ratings, 4.5★; no outfit score
      - 600K+ downloads and 500M views via $20 creator test videos, with a creator as co-founder (self-reported)
      - 30% of recent reviews 1–2★ (billing, bugs, sign-in, bad cut-outs)
      - ~1 rating per 500 downloads (one data point; small competitors are bigger than their ratings suggest)
    - **Open, tested by the interviews:** how often the problem happens (daily vs only before events).
225. **The founder's blind check passes the panel; the panel becomes the scoring target (Oct 12; founder rated 20 gold-set photos blind, `inputs/gold-judge/founder/ratings.csv`, page `inputs/gold-judge/founder/index.html`; numbers `extraction-test/results/gold1/founder_check.txt`).** 20 photos: 7 weak, 4 so-so, 4 average, 5 strong by the panel, 4 of them the app's biggest disagreements; 10 Instagram, 7 Pinterest, 3 Reddit. Rule 7: the rater is the founder (profile pending); the photos are profile unknown.
    - **Founder vs panel r 0.69,** within 0.5 on 16/20 (each judge 0.61–0.75; the 22-year-old peer judge is closest at 0.75). **Founder vs app r −0.09;** the panel vs the app on these 20, 0.06.
    - **Where he differs from the panel:** harsher on skinny jeans (pin-002: 5.0 vs 7.0; skinny legs are named in 5 of his notes); kinder to summer fits with sandals or jorts (ig-065 7.0 vs 5.5, pin-021 6.5 vs 4.5, ig-097 7.0 vs 5.5).
    - **His notes, as rules to test:** skinny or too-fitted pants, a shirt too long untucked, "colors all over the place", a shirt the wrong size, wider pants under a bulky top. Some notes judge by how the clothes make the body look ("makes him look bigger"); the app keeps those garment against garment (#164).
    - **Claude's call:** the panel is the target for retuning the parts engine's rules on paper. The retune is its own chat (brief in CLAUDE.md "Next steps").
    - **Correction to #224:** the duplicate removal dropped ig-120 (a distinct photo) instead of a duplicate. ig-096 and ig-117 are two different shots of the same outfit, not one photo, and both stay. 224 distinct photos were read; nothing else changes.

227. **Parts engine v8 candidate: retuned on paper against the gold-set panel (Oct 12; the retune chat, brief in CLAUDE.md; report `rubric/research/parts/gold-retune.md`, scripts `extraction-test/gold_tune.py`, `gold_features.py` → `results/gold-tune/`). No API, no app change: the app still scores with v7, and v1–v7 are unchanged (298/298 conformance cases pass).**
    - **Method:** a held-out split (seed 20261012, `split.json`): 133 tune / 92 test photos, an Instagram creator's series kept on one side, the founder's 20 forced into test. Rules and sizes were chosen on the tune half only. The test half was opened once after freezing (r 0.53, founder 0.35); three later changes were not chosen from test numbers: no narrow-leg charge under a tailored jacket (a sport coat's trousers flipped straight/slim across runs), a big logo is loud only on a tee (the founder's own black graphic hoodie was charged with straight jeans, not with wide), and stacked baggy jeans and cargos cost nothing (#221, closing KD-2).
    - **Result:** held-out r **0.32 → 0.54**; the founder's 20 **−0.09 → 0.41** (within 0.5 on 12/20, was 9); test worst band 6.71 → 6.36, best 7.17 → 7.43. 3-run steadiness 49/49 and 66/66 unchanged (exact 39 → 35, 57 → 52). The friends' four fits stay 7.0; one founder check moves (0d39d6 6.5 → 7.0). 8+ on saved photos 6% → 4%, on gold 13% → 16% (panel 28%).
    - **v8, all on existing reading fields:** roomy long-sleeved pieces are the cut, not "a size big" (−0.5, was −2.0); pooling on loose jeans/cargos costs nothing and no longer blocks shape credits (−0.5 on loose trousers); a narrow leg −1.0 on Proportions (skinny −2.0, ripped −0.5 more; not under tailoring, not suit trousers); the waist break +1.5 over a loose leg (mirror selfies and knits included), +0.5 over a straight one; a jacket read ending at the belt over a loose leg +1.0; no "too dressy" for loafers/derbies with jeans and no "dainty" tip; runners 5.0; slides +0.5 when nothing else is off; no credit for one bright piece or one standout pattern; a loud print −1.5 on Color; top-to-toe and suit credits +0.5 (were +1.0); a tank top alone −2.0 on Style; the loungewear ceiling +0.5 when the outfit reads as a style; weights Proportions 30 · Fit 15 · Color 20 · Shoes 15 · Style 20; "problems add up" (half of each extra part's shortfall under 6.5, up to −1.0). The 7.0 start stays (fitted 6.94–6.96).
    - **Tried and not used:** a stretch away from 7 (no r gain, 8+ jumps to 27%); the mirror-selfie flag (a proxy for Instagram, not outfit quality); bigger PR1 or narrow-leg sizes (flat or worse on tune).
    - **Not built:** size faults (the reading can't tell a size big from roomy on purpose), the mismatched near-suit (its one example is in the test half; tip), a shirt too long (the length reading almost never fires; tip).
    - **Ceiling:** a ridge fit on every existing reading field reaches test r 0.63–0.66, so the readings can support somewhat more than v8's plain rules.
    - **Caveat:** inside one source the app still follows the panel only moderately (test: Instagram 0.51, Pinterest 0.26); much of the gain separates deliberate creator fits from dated Pinterest ones. That matches 2026 taste and the founder's notes, but the founder's own one-outfit variants are the real test.
    - **Founder's calls (to-do list):** adopt v8 and port it (default yes); the two new card lines, "the slim jeans read dated; a straight leg balances the outfit" and "the big, loud print on the tee takes over the outfit" (default as written). Rule 7: gold photos profile unknown, one test profile (Minimal + Smart casual, a little bold, Everyday); founder profile pending; friends unknown.

228. **Parts v8 adopted and ported to the app (Oct 12; founder: "yes, adopt v8 and port it to the app"). New fit checks score with v8.** The two new card lines ship as written ("the slim jeans read dated; a straight leg balances the outfit", "the big, loud print on the tee takes over the outfit"); they're on the founder's skim list.
    - **Engine:** `parts_proto.py` default `engine="v8"`. Two cleanups at the port, no score moved on the gold set (v7 and v8 reports identical): the unused "stretch" switch removed (at 1.0 it could still change the last bit of v7's floats), and the "lowest part" tie goes to the heavier part by v8's own weights. The result carries `piled_penalty` ("problems add up").
    - **Core:** `src/parts.ts` ports every v8 knob; parts data 1.5.0 (two new sets: LOUD_PATTERNS, LOUD_LOGO_TOPS); 16,231 parity cases exact, including v1–v7 replayed as tune variants and a v8 what-if. The checks v8 retired (CP1, H4, FF5_too_big, the SW3 tip) must still fire in the v7 cases.
    - **Fix engine:** catalog 1.3.0. New fix `top_tee` "Swap the {name} for a tee" (the tank top). Next level: `next_color` reworded to a muted accent ("One quiet color, like a rust or olive knit, lifts Color.") because v8 gives a bright piece nothing; `next_style` ("One standout piece, like a plaid overshirt") retired because v8 doesn't reward it. New fix edge `pick_oxford_half_wide` (the prep blazer line, hidden on the old edge by a same-group Smaller step). Every fix parity case exact.
    - **App:** the Why sheet's new row "Problems in more than one part" (`pwhy.piled`); `StoredParts.piledPenalty` (absent on older checks = 0); `loungeHolds` subtracts it. A new test proves the row is there and the rows add up.
    - **Rulebook:** `rubric/v5-styles.md` rewritten for v8 (a helper agent): 16 rules changed what they do, 7 new (SCO-7 problems add up, PRO-13 narrow leg, PRO-14 a jacket ending at the belt over a loose leg, COL-8 loud print, SHO-13 slides softened, STY-9 tank top alone, LNG-7 the loungewear lift); KD-2 resolved, KD-18–20 added (standout credits off, size big vs roomy unreadable, narrow legs charged in every look). 339 cases for 136 rule IDs, Python and TS.
    - **Behaviour that changed in tests (each checked, then the test updated):** the demo hero p01b 8.0 → 7.5 (a short tee over straight chinos: the waist break is +0.5 there), so screen 7 shows 7.5 "Put together" with the colour tip and the p01b → p01a re-check story starts at 7.5; ph8 (slides) leads with the free "Cuff your jeans once → 7.0" (the chunky-sneaker swap → 7.5 is still a fix, but a first check shows the lead fix); the founder's Oct 11 green hoodie (af7e93) 7.5 → 8.0 on every stored read (the zip hoodie read ending at the belt over wide jeans earns PRO-14; same photo, same score still holds); quick tap on the Oct 8 checks: the boots question returns on b0f0c8 (boots 8.0 vs slides 7.5), a765e8's jeans answer is 7.5 (swing 2.0), 6b14db no longer asks; "Going for minimal" on s15 now offers "Swap the patterned overshirt for a solid color"; the friend's blazer keeps "Swap the jeans for navy chinos → 8.0" with no Smaller step; the pick tie-break story moved to stock s16 (Workwear: boots before navy chinos).
    - **Old checks:** a check stored on parts 1.4.0 keeps its score; if it's edited (a correction, the occasion, a re-check) it re-scores on v8, as with every earlier parts update.
    - **Known weak spot (from the rulebook rewrite):** PRO-14 rewards a hoodie or overshirt read as ending at the belt even when a shirt hangs below it (the founder's Oct 11 oxford under the zip hoodie); the second look that sees the peek isn't run in the app (KD-15). Left as built; the gold set's own photos should say whether it over-rewards.

## Oct 12 · Theme and vibe review (theme session; numbered from #230 to avoid collisions)
230. **Archivo is in the app; a three-critic review of the theme (Oct 12; founder: "go for it" on Archivo, and "critique the app's theme").**
    - **Archivo:** the 3 static cuts (ArchivoCondensed-SemiBold / -Medium, ArchivoExpanded-Bold) and `OFL.txt` from Omnibus-Type/Archivo are in `app/assets/fonts/`, embedded by the expo-font plugin (`app.config.ts`) and loaded at runtime on web (`fontFiles.ts`, `useKittedFonts()` in the root layout). PostScript names match `typography.ts → archivo`. Seen on the Simulator: the score numeral, the re-check numeral, the Welcome preview card and the woven KITTED label (`screenshots/archivo/`). Still to do: show `OFL.txt` under You › About › Acknowledgements when You is built (the license asks for it to ship with the font).
    - **The verdict (3 agents: brand, screen-by-screen, code audit):** the look is calm and trustworthy but generic, "a banking app with a chat in it", and in light mode closest to ChatGPT, the main rival. The denim/rivet concept lives mostly in the docs. Dark mode is the stronger look. Best screens: the morning card (4.5/5), the drawn clothes, the Why sheet. Weakest: the Stylist header cut-off, camera live view, "Going for" box-in-box, placeholder routes.
    - **Settled by Claude (no taste involved; to build in a fix batch):** restore the camera's contrast fixes the code lost (`CameraScreen.tsx`: countdown backing 35% → `fixed.countdownBacking`, mode switch 40% → 60% with 90% labels, "Hold still" on a `fixed.photoTag` capsule); hide buttons that open placeholders (Share on the re-check and fit-check cards, the Added-sheet rows) and wire or hide "Swap one" (`OutfitIdeaCard.tsx:69`, no handler); native header buttons in ink, not copper (`app/_layout.tsx` nav theme `primary`); no green text without a sign (GoingFor "It reads that way too."), no red "Remove", no iOS-blue alert button; a scroll-edge fade under the Stylist header (Closet has it); hide "Re-check after fixing" when there's no fix; one page gutter (16) and one card inset across tabs; radius tokens for thumbnails (12); Welcome's fixed line heights removed (they overlap at large text); the dark-mode segmented control; the "Earlier chats" pill and inline camera button up to 44 pt; dark clothes on dark tiles get a faint outline; the camera's bright ring around the face goes (it spotlights the one thing we promise not to judge).
    - **Taste calls for the founder (to-do list):** the app icon (still Expo's default); copper only for score movement (links to ink); tracked capitals back on the card labels (`MONO_UPPERCASE_AT_NORMAL_SIZES`); a slightly bluer indigo; dark first in videos, App Store shots and the share card; pull the share card forward from P6; a score reveal (count-up + haptic).
    - **Seen on the Simulator (Oct 12, after the port):** screens 7, 8, 10, 11, 61, 77 (`screenshots/v8-app/`, git-ignored) show v8 as described. Found and fixed one card bug v8 made common: with no fix, no tip and no Going-for line, "No quick fixes for this one." printed twice (the #183 fix only covered the case with a tip). Now the fix section and its seam are left out when the summary already says it (`FitCheckCard.tsx`). Screen 10's re-check story reads 7.5 → 7.5 "I still see the same outfit" (it was already broken, 8.0 → 7.5, since #209): it still needs a new before/after demo pair.
231. **Theme fix batch built; the founder took every default (Oct 12; founder: "defaults, go ahead with the fix batch"). Screenshots `screenshots/theme-batch/`; icon options `screenshots/icon-options/index.html`.**
    - **Founder's calls (all defaults):** copper only when the score moves (links, header buttons, "Undo", "Change" go ink semibold; minus values ink2); spaced capitals on card labels (`MONO_UPPERCASE_AT_NORMAL_SIZES = true`; accessibility sizes still use sentence case); a bluer indigo (light `primary` #121628 → #1A2448, white on it 15.1:1; `me` #1B2340 → #22305C, 11.5:1; dark `me` #2A3150 → #2B3860, 10.0:1; `fixed.label` → #22305C; body ink unchanged; old values in comments in `tokens.ts`); dark first in videos, App Store shots and the share card (no code); the share card moves to right after onboarding; the app icon: Claude drew 3 options, the founder picks.
    - **Built (4 builders on separate files, then one check):** the camera's contrast fixes (countdown 60%, mode switch 60%/90%, "Hold still" on a capsule), the head drawn like the body (no bright ring), the guide stops above the controls, the "Checking the photo…" pill hides behind the result panel; Share and the Added-sheet rows hidden behind `SHARE_CARD_BUILT` / `EDIT_ITEM_BUILT` (both false); the morning outfit card's dead "Swap one" removed (the seeded card has no daily idea to swap; MorningCard's own Swap works); "Re-check after fixing" only when there's a fix; the chevron sits on "/10", and re-checks use a copper → arrow; card header tags all ink3; a 20 pt fade under the Stylist header; Going for: no box-in-box, ink sentences with a green check icon, equal look tiles (one-word names shrink rather than split, two-word names wrap); one 16 pt gutter on every tab, `space.rowX/rowY` row insets, radius tokens; Welcome's fixed line heights gone; the dark segmented control raised; 44 pt targets; onboarding lines 1 pt; text boxes follow Dynamic Type; a faint rim on dark clothes in dark mode; the Added sheet closes with X like the others; the empty crop slot on the quick question gone.
