229. **Four critics on how outfits are scored (v8) (Oct 12; founder: "use agents to critique the way outfits are getting scored right now… there is still a way to go"). Report `rubric/research/parts/v8-critique.md`; paper only, nothing changed in the engine.** Critics: data (the gold set's misses), stylist (90 made-up everyday outfits scored by eye first), the card's advice (the fix engine on 223 gold photos and 78 saved checks), and the model's design (12 alternative structures, tune half, test once).
    - **Verified by Claude:** the source label alone predicts the panel at test r 0.71 (v8 0.56); within Instagram a panel 8 outscores a panel 7 50% (tune) / 60% (test) of the time; **within-source test r is 0.37 for v8 vs 0.38 for v7**, so #227's "twice as well" came from telling the sources apart. Correction added to `gold-retune.md`. The founder's 20 also follow source (r 0.82; within source 0.42 with the panel).
    - **Claude's calls:** keep v8 (the stylist critic: within 0.5 on 79/90, r 0.80, and it backs v8's main rules); no more tuning against this gold set (re-weighting moves r by ≤0.015, refitting points overfits: tune 0.79 → test 0.35); the next scoring work is judged by within-source r and "does an 8 beat a 7"; no source, mirror or room signals; no more presence credits.
    - **Proposed, waiting on the founder (to-do list):** (a) card advice fixes with no score change (hem and cuff tips when the score can't move, dark straight jeans instead of navy chinos, no default short-jacket line, free moves first, no contradictory Smaller step); (b) four consensus rule fixes (an interview needs a collared top, knit or jacket; same-wash double denim −1.0; an oversized graphic tee under a blazer charged; a look's own loud print exempt from CL1). Then the founder's one-outfit variants plus a second human rater, the hem/length close-up reading test (#190; ~$0.30–2), and only then the additive-scorecard redesign.
233. **Whole-app review by 5 agents and the first fixes (Oct 12; founder: "make agents go through the app and look for improvements"). Fresh screenshots of every demo screen: `screenshots/review-oct12/` (78). Numbered #233 because the scoring session took #232.**
    - **Reviews:** copy and voice (40 items), accessibility (15 + Apple's Nutrition Labels), what breaks (no network, permissions, app killed, limits, storage), navigation and flow, and a first session as a 20-year-old from TikTok. The shared verdict: the pipeline works, but the first result often gives nothing to do ("No quick fixes", since fixed by the scoring session), the share card and notifications (the growth and return loops) aren't built, and You has no settings, privacy switch or delete-my-data that the consent screen promises.
    - **Fixed now (no taste involved):** a check cut off by the app closing no longer stays stuck (`fitcheck/interrupted.ts`, run at boot); errors during a check fail the card visibly; a boot error no longer leaves a blank screen; Try again keeps the card if the photo is gone; one check at a time; Try again and re-checks count against the limit (each is a paid read), with one extra "Try another photo" after a re-check that missed the change (02 "Change not seen": it doesn't use another re-check; once only); a damaged saved record is skipped instead of blanking a tab; consent's buttons can't lock; a malformed AI reply takes the "didn't use your check" path. Accessibility: VoiceOver reaches the closet question, the partial check's shoe choices and the re-check buttons; "Save this look" hidden until it saves (`SAVE_LOOK_BUILT`); "Take a new photo" on the unreadable / not-clothed cards (they were dead ends); Basics, chips, menus, Lookbook rows and camera tips reflow at large text; flash and camera-mode labels; Reduce Motion for the tile shake and the panel slide; a non-color marker on the part with most to gain; text on the composer's glass. App tests 379/379, tsc and lint clean (the core's own tests were mid-edit in the scoring session).
    - **Waiting on the founder (outward-facing copy):** the copy fixes (to-do list), the "still reading" message for a second photo (dropped silently today), and a limit card before the camera opens.

232. **Step 1 of the critique (#229): better card advice and four consensus scoring fixes, parts v9 (Oct 12; founder: "Proceed" on Claude's plain-words plan: "improve the advice on the card… fix a few obvious mistakes").** New fit checks score with v9 (parts data 1.6.0, fix catalog 1.4.0); Python and `@kitted/core` exact on 17,405 scoring and every fix-engine parity case. Numbered #232 because #230–231 are the theme session's.
    - **Scoring (v9 = v8 + four rules, `TUNE["v9"]`):**
      - **An interview needs a collared shirt, a knit or a jacket:** without one, the score is held to 6.0; a hoodie or sweatshirt with nothing over it, 5.0.
      - **Same-wash double denim** in a light or mid wash costs −1.0 on Color. Dark raw denim on dark stays free (gold ig-040 panel 8.0 and rd-009 7.5 praised it, so the first version was narrowed).
      - **An oversized graphic tee under a tailored jacket** costs −1.5 on Style (closes KD-9).
      - **The loud-print fault skips a look's own loud piece,** only in looks where loud prints are native (streetwear, workwear, vintage sport, outdoor) and never when the piece is bright. The first version also exempted quiet looks; gold pin-048 and pin-062 (argyle sweaters, panel 6.0) went up, so it was narrowed.
    - **Effect on scores:**
      - **Gold set:** test r 0.54 and within-source 0.36 (v8 0.54 / 0.37); no shown gold score moved after the two narrowings.
      - **The stylist critic's 90 outfits:** within 0.5 on 81 (was 79), r 0.84 (was 0.80). In-sample: these outfits suggested three of the rules.
      - **Saved checks:** none of the founder's or friends' scores moved.
    - **Advice (fix engine and copy, no score change):**
      - **New "Finishing touch" tips with no number**, shown when there's no fix, ahead of a Smaller step that means buying something:
        - cuff jeans or cargos that pool or break, except wide jeans in a streetwear outfit (#221);
        - hem trousers (about $15);
        - tuck in a layer that hangs below a shorter one.
      - **"Swap for navy chinos"** never replaces jeans and never goes under a navy jacket. The friend's d1967f now gets "Swap the jeans for dark straight jeans → 7.5".
      - **"Add a short jacket"** only when the outfit reads as streetwear, workwear or vintage sport; not on warm days, not over sweats, joggers or gym shorts, not with slides.
      - **No "Swap the dress shoes for loafers" under a suit.**
      - **Wording:**
        - The summary says "Good base. Swapping one piece gets you to 7.5." when the move means another piece; "One easy change" stays for free moves.
        - The Smaller step reads "… to 7.5. Not enough to move the score yet." (no second number).
        - "The score may not move yet." is gone from every Next level line.
        - "Clears the condition point" became "Looks crisper".
    - **Measured with the fixes critic's own scripts (v8 → v9):**
      - **Gold set:**
        - the lead fix matches the judges' advice 63/131 → 65/113 (48% → 58%);
        - fixes of a kind no judge mentions 45 → 28;
        - some advice matching the judges 94 → 100 of 223;
        - navy chinos lead 26 → 5, the short jacket lead 14 → 0, tips on 32 cards.
      - **Saved checks:**
        - navy chinos lead 11 → 4, the jacket lead 13 → 3;
        - the jacket shown on the founder's and friends' cards 11 → 6 of 25 (all streetwear reads).
      - **The cost:** "No quick fixes" cards rise (gold 92 → 110, saved 23 → 34). The wrong swaps are gone, and the hem tip can only fire when the reading notices the hem, which it misses on most photos (#229 B; the hem close-up test is next).
    - **Tests:** core test stories updated to the new advice (the friend's jeans, the pick tie-break now on stock s13). The rulebook `rubric/v5-styles.md` and its conformance cases were updated for v9 by a helper agent.

## Oct 13 · The founder's taste sources, read from their own videos (transcript session)
234. **How Tim Dessaint, Wanhee Kim and Jaye Alexander judge an outfit (`rubric/research/creators/creators-rules.md`).** The founder brought 45 auto-caption transcripts. Cleaned with `tools/youtube/clean_vtt.py`; channels looked up with yt-dlp metadata (no download). 32 kept and read in full by five reader agents, one batch per creator, under one brief (`rubric/research/creators/notes/`). 13 dropped as off-topic or about bodies.
    - **A fourth creator was in the folder:** 7 Frugal Aesthetic videos (2023–2024). Claude's call: count him as a fourth voice, not a taste source, and mark rules only he holds; his 2023 trend claims are treated as dated.
    - **Jaye changed his mind on skinny jeans** (banned Dec 2024–Feb 2025; "cut doesn't matter" Dec 2025; "nobody dissing skinny jeans" May 2026). Claude's call: use his latest view.
    - **What they share:** clean basics are "okay" (the app's 7.0 agrees); "great" is a deliberate silhouette plus one focal point on a quiet base. All four: fit and proportion first, nothing tight, no big top over narrow pants, the pant–shoe meeting makes or breaks it, a too-long top is a fault, one point not several, loud logos are try-hard, a neutral base, deliberate smart/casual mixing is good.
    - **Where the app differs:** no credit for one focal point since v8 (three of four reward one bright accent on a dark base; the panel didn't, #227); slim jeans cost 1.0 and are called "dated" (none of the three says slim is dated; skinny, yes); a shirt hanging below a jacket never counts (the second look isn't sent, KD-15); tone-in-tone earns nothing (KD-6); accessories never count (only Jaye wants them near-required).
    - **Ranked proposals, waiting for the founder (no engine change made):** (1) send the second look (+0.24¢/check); (2) a three-question hem close-up test (touches the shoe / drags behind the heel / ankle showing); (3) drop "dated" from the slim line now, test −0.5 vs −1.0 on his variants; (4) a narrow "one point" credit on a dark base, after an accent pair in his shoot; (5) tone-in-tone from the phone's measured lightness; (6) a fitted untucked top over wide legs, if roominess 1–2 vs 3 proves steady; (7) a "Finishing touch" tip for a chain or watch, no points; (8) test Shoes at 20%.
    - **Next:** screenshots of the on-screen outfits (Tim's five before/after pairs first) and the within-creator test: does each "after" beat its "before"?
235. **Scoring v10, "the creators' rules", and the stylist guide (founder, Oct 13: "use the data you collected as rules, and as tips… the AI's context"; Frugal Aesthetic confirmed as his fourth taste source).**
    - **v10 (default in Python and `@kitted/core`; parts data 1.7.0, fix catalog 1.5.0; 18,616 scoring and 23,049 fix parity cases exact; conformance 386 cases, 142 rules; report `rubric/research/parts/v10.md`):** slim legs −0.5 (was −1.0) with "would balance better with a straight leg", skinny −2.0 keeps "read dated"; [COL-11] one bright piece as the only color with a black, charcoal, navy or dark-denim piece beside it, +0.5 Color (Wanhee's "tone down"); [STY-11] a knit-over-knit tip; the cuff tip now leads with hemming.
    - **Claude's call to ship COL-11 although the panel disagrees:** gold test-half r 0.54 → 0.53 and the founder's 20 0.41 → 0.36 (one photo, a paisley shirt read as solid). The panel is confounded by photo source (#229), three of four creators back the rule, and it is narrow (11 of 225 gold photos, 0 of 78 saved checks). His one-accent photo pair is the real test; if it fails there, set COL-11 to 0.
    - **Unchanged:** none of the founder's 21 or his friends' 4 saved checks moved; 8+ share 4% on saved checks; steadiness 218 → 216 of 223 gold photos.
    - **Side effect:** check 6b14db asks "Jeans or sweatpants?" again (the jeans answer is now worth 1.0 more).
    - **`rubric/stylist-guide.md`:** everything the four creators teach (fit, pants and shoes, balance, color, restraint, layering, accessories, mixing, occasions, condition, per-look notes, shopping, where they disagree), paraphrased in the stylist's voice, with rules for the chat model (never rate bodies, never give a score in chat, never name the creators). It is the context the Stylist chat loads when real answers are built.
    - **Frames:** 26 outfits grabbed from 5 of the 7 videos (Tim's 5 before/after pairs and 10 outfits, Frugal's shorter-tee pair, 3 of Wanhee's toned-down examples, Jaye's favorite); the videos were downloaded at 720p only for this and deleted. Wanhee's and Jaye's accessory videos are mostly seated talking, so they had little to grab. `extraction-test/creator_read.py` scores them (prep done; the readings need the founder's key, ~$0.45).
236. **The second look ships in the app (founder, Oct 13: "go ahead with the second look"; +0.24¢ a check).** Two narrow Gemini calls, U (a top under the outer piece, and whether it peeks below) and L (do the pants rest on the shoe), run beside the scoring call on the face-hidden close-ups; same config as look1 (#199). Each arm that's back by the reading or within 1.5 s after counts; later ones are dropped (billed, unused). Stored in `fit_checks.details.secondLook` and reused on every re-score, so the same photo keeps the same score; a same-photo re-check copies them with no call. Old checks, demo photos and Release builds (dev key only) run without it. JS only, no rebuild.
    - **What fires:** [PRO-9] −0.5 and no jacket shape credit when U sees the top peek below the outer piece; [SHO-2]'s extra 0.5 for loose jeans dragging on slides follows L. KD-15 closed.
    - **Claude's calls (the helper's defaults, kept):** each arm counts on its own (a slow L doesn't drop U's peek); a re-check only uses looks its first check had, so before/after stay comparable; demo photos never get one; a look that lands after the reading is settled is ignored.
    - **Honest expectation:** under v7 the agreement test (#220) found the second look moved no shown score on 79 real photos. Its value is the creators' rule (A5) and the hem test (#237) that builds on L.
237. **The hem close-up test, ready to run (founder's OK on ~$0.50, Oct 13; no calls made: no key here).** `extraction-test/hem_test.py` with `prompt_hem3.txt` / `schema_hem3.json`: a crop from the ankles down (placed by the knee and ankle joints on 76 of 78 photos) and four yes/no questions in one call: the hem touches the shoe; fabric folds above the shoe; it drags at the heel; ankle or sock shows (the creators' A4/B1–B4; "folds" and "drags" are split because a soft stack on loose legs is free and dragging costs everywhere). 78 photos (22 of his phone checks, 7 web mirror selfies, 22 stock, 25 gold), labels in `hem3_labels.csv` (130 mapped from earlier labels, 182 by eye, blind to AI answers). Pass bar per question: steady ≥ 90%, right ≥ 85% when it answers, can't-see ≤ 30%. `--report` also gives a paper estimate of six hem rules on the v10 engine. About $0.28 for three runs, capped at $0.60. Caveats: one labeller; the "drags" yes set is thin (9); 14 of the 22 phone photos are his one pair of black wide jeans. Run with `tools/run_key_tests.sh` (also runs `creator_read.py`).
238. **The two key tests ran (founder, Oct 13: "use the API key from before… for these reasons"; the app's dev key from `app/.env.development.local`, never printed; `tools/run_key_tests.sh` now loads it). $0.72 in all.**
    - **Hem crop (78 photos × 3, `rubric/research/notes/hem3.md`):** "the hem touches the shoe" (96% steady, 94% right) and "ankle shows" (95%, 97%) pass; "drags at the heel" passes the bar but only says yes 6 times (finds 25% of drags); **"folds above the shoe" fails (69%)**, so the stack is still unreadable. The rules that passed move 0 scores on 78 photos (3 ankle tips). Claude's call: no third call in the app; re-run on the founder's hem variants.
    - **The creators' own outfits (26 frames × 3, `creators-rules.md` §8):** the app agrees on 5 of 6 before/afters and gets 1 backwards (corrected in #239: the first printout said 3 of 6). Causes: [STY-4] charges a tank under an open flannel with trousers and loafers −1.5 (the creators' favourite kind of mix); skinny joggers skip [PRO-13]; the befores' faults (multicolor scarf, knit on knit, "basic and predictable") aren't scored; Tim's favourite is held to 7.0 by a relaxed blazer's sleeves read as covering the hands (−2.0). Jaye's "perfect" outfit is 7.0 (flares, pendant, boots unreadable). COL-11 fires on Wanhee's examples but moves Color only (~0.1 on the score).
    - **Proposed, waiting for the founder:** (1) [STY-4] judges a covered base layer by the layer over it; (2) the skinny charge on skinny joggers out of the gym; (3) later, maybe: a roomy blazer's long sleeves −1.0 instead of −2.0.
239. **Scoring v11 (founder, Oct 13: "yes build both fixes as v11"; default in Python and `@kitted/core`, parts data 1.8.0; 19,837 scoring and 23,511 fix parity cases exact; conformance 398; report `rubric/research/parts/v11.md`).**
    - **[STY-4] a covered base top:** a tank or tee under another top layer (flannel, overshirt, camp, denim, oxford or dress shirt, cardigan, any outerwear) is left out of the "formal and very casual pieces fight" gap; the layer over it stands for the top half. The reading has no open/closed field, so it isn't required. Side effect: a tee under a suit isn't charged either (7.0 → 7.5). A tank alone, or under a hoodie, is still charged.
    - **[PRO-13] skinny joggers or sweats** out of the gym and home: −2.0 ("the skinny joggers read dated; a relaxed leg balances the outfit"). Only 3 of 73 jogger/sweat readings are "skinny" (all Tim's leather pair), so ordinary cuffed joggers aren't hit and the full charge was used.
    - **Effect:** gold set, the founder's 20, steadiness and all 78 saved checks unchanged (neither change fires on them). Creator pairs: Tim's tank-under-flannel pair goes from backwards to a tie (the roomy flannel's sleeves still cost 0.5, KD-19); his skinny-joggers pair widens to 6.5 → 7.5; 5 of 6 agree, none against.
    - **Correction to #238:** the run's printed summary said the app agreed on 3 of 6; its own saved per-run scores, and three recomputations (fresh processes, different hash seeds, with photo prep), give 5 of 6 under v10. The cause of the bad printout wasn't found. The notes now carry the reproducible numbers.
241. **The outfit card stops repeating itself (founder, Oct 14: "make sure it doesn't recommend the same outfit again and again with the same top").** Before: only items *worn* in the last 2 days were pushed down (−0.75), and shown-but-ignored cards weren't tracked, so a closet's best outfit won every day (a 14-day run with 5 tops showed the navy sweater all 14 days). Now `pickOutfit` takes a 14-day `history` of shown cards (day and evening), the wear log and fit checks, and ranks outfits that already pass weather, occasion and layer rules by: no weather-wrong piece, no same full outfit within 7 days, the top he's gone longest without, then score. The evening card never repeats that morning's top while another works. Bottoms and shoes keep a mild push. Same history → same pick; a locked day card stays; Swap one and Style this still win.
    - **14-day simulation (5 tops, 3 bottoms, 3 shoes):** different tops 1 → 5 ignoring every card (2 → 5 with Wear this daily); same top as yesterday 13 → 0; same full outfit within 7 days 12 → 0; evening repeating the morning's top 3/3 → 0.
    - **Claude's call (the helper's):** weather-wrong pieces now rank above rotation (before they were small deductions), so rotation can never put a sweater on a 75° day.

## Oct 14 · Theme round 2: You, Lookbook, Closet, share card, score reveal (theme session)

Archivo's OFL). The woven label is the plan badge (KITTED / copper KITTED PRO). No stats on You (an average score on a profile reads as a report card on him).
    - **Lookbook:** the founder's open call answered with the same recommendation: keep the tab as "how you're doing": each month a woven label and three numbers that only go up (looks, days checked, best), "Best of the month", a 2-column neck-down grid with the score under the photo, copper rivets only on looks that improved, a look page (card, fix, pieces, "Wear this again"). No average or chart (dips read as failing; old v4 and new parts scores aren't on one scale). Later: calendar toggle, filters, a monthly recap made for sharing. **Found:** today's Lookbook thumbnails can show the face (a mirror selfie), against 03 §8 neck-down; to fix in the Lookbook rebuild.
    - **Closet:** a counts line (pieces · outfits you can make), open questions as one stacked card, shelves in body order (Layers, Tops, Bottoms, Shoes) split by seams and sorted dark to light, white tiles with each kind drawn at its own scale (shoes on a floor shadow), every tile opening a piece page (hang-tag name plate, worn / outfits / last worn, Seen in, Style this, merge, remove). Later: the fit tape (describes the garment, never him) and the next-best-buy card (+N outfits in copper, a kind, never a brand).

Never: confetti or badges, motion that frames the face or body, copper off score movement.
    - **Build order proposed:** score reveal + re-check win (~1 day); You with Settings and Privacy & data (~2–3 days, needed before outside testers); share card (1–2 days, needs `react-native-view-shot`, founder's OK); Lookbook month view + look page (3–4 days); Closet shelves + piece page (3–4 days).

240. **Real weather on the morning card, from the US National Weather Service (founder, Oct 14: "go with the US weather service now… we will move over to Apple WeatherKit").** Before: dev builds always showed `SAMPLE_WEATHER.coldRain` and "Allow" never asked iOS. Now: a local Expo module `app/modules/kitted-location` (CoreLocation, reduced accuracy by default, when-in-use, one fix; no new npm package) and `app/src/services/weather/nws.ts` (coordinate rounded to 2 decimals → `/points` → hourly forecast → `DayWeather`; feels-like from NWS wind-chill / heat-index formulas; caches in memory only; 404 outside the US, offline or any error → the season). Live in dev and release builds (no key needed); demo screens keep sample weather. Card label "Weather: National Weather Service"; permission text "Kitted uses your approximate location to get today's weather for your outfit idea. It goes only to the US National Weather Service and is never stored."
    - **Claude's fix on top:** the morning service waited only 2 s for weather, too short for a location fix plus two requests; live weather now gets 7 s (the helper's wrapper in `morning/index.ts` was removed).
    - **Needs a phone reinstall** (new native module). Until then the phone's card has no weather and no Allow row (season only).
    - **Open:** whether the App Store privacy label counts this as Coarse Location collection (05 already plans "Coarse Location, App Functionality, not linked"); the WeatherKit move after enrollment (CLAUDE.md §4b).

Actions: **Rename** ("Rename piece", 40 characters, empty or the original restores it, "Reset to the original name", toast "Renamed to {name}.") and **Remove from Closet** (the menu replaces the old confirm sheet; Undo stays). A rename shows everywhere the name shows and never changes matching (tested by renaming every piece and re-running a check). VoiceOver: Rename and Remove as tile actions. JS only. 245. **(Committed as "#243"; renumbered.) Re-checks and new checks could lock up until the app restarted (founder, Oct 14: the card's re-check "isn't working" and new fit checks "aren't accepted"; reproduced in tests, not yet on his phone).** The "one check at a time" lock (#233) is released only when a check's job settles, and the job read the Gemini stream with a plain `for await`: the 14 s give-up only fired the abort, it never ended the wait. A stalled live stream that ignores the abort (a dead connection after a Wi-Fi change) left the job hanging, and every later photo and re-check returned 'busy', which shows no words: the camera closes and nothing happens.
    - **Fix:** `runRead` races each stream step against the abort, so a timeout or cancel always ends it; `exclusive()` has a 40 s cap (`TimerConfig.slotMs`) that stops the read, frees the slot, ends the reading card and marks a still-scoring row failed (timeout, Try again); a check given up by the cap can't touch the next one. "One at a time" still holds. The Gemini source wakes on abort and, in dev builds, logs a refused call's HTTP status and reason (`[gemini] refused · HTTP 429 · …`, never the key). The card's Re-check button now disappears when its 30-minute window closes (before, tapping it gave "Check used today").
    - **Ruled out in tests:** the second look (never resolving, rejecting, late, throwing), an unanswered quick tap, a stale camera file, scoring errors on live data (his 17 Oct 11 readings, 544 check → re-check → next-check runs, 0 errors). 12 new tests in `fitCheckLiveFlow.test.ts` set up like his phone.
    - **If it happens again,** the Metro lines tell the causes apart: `[gemini] refused · HTTP 429` (the dev key's quota), `failed: timeout`, `→ busy · nothing sent`, `[check] still not finished after 40 s: freed the slot`.
    - **Open copy (his #233 list, item 8, default yes):** 'busy' still shows no words; "Still reading your last photo. Send this one when it's done." is ready to build.
246. **Theme round 2 critiqued by 4 agents (visual design, HCI heuristics, user psychology, accessibility) and the mockups revised (Oct 14; founder: "critique this design with design and HCI agents"). `mockups/theme-round2.html` is now the revised version; renders `screenshots/theme-round2/v2-*.png` (git-ignored).** Scores were 6.5–8/10 per screen: a real step up on today's app, not finished.
    - **What all or most critics agreed on, now fixed in the mockups:** (1) the privacy line overclaimed ("Your photos stay on this iPhone" while a blurred copy goes to Google): it now says so, with "See what I send" (the blurred copy from his last check) and the switch renamed "Score my fits"; (2) "What your stylist knows" read like a file on him: facts in the second person ("You wear tees untucked"), no money guesses, a guess is asked as a Yes / Not really question; (3) the tape-measure figure and copper height on the profile framed his body: removed, basics are one ink3 line, shown only if entered; (4) copper leaked onto status: Pro is the indigo woven label with copper stitches on its own row, no copper height, no rivet dots (they also failed contrast at 2.41:1 on light photos and meant nothing without a legend); (5) too many small tracked-caps labels: one per section, dates and details in 13 pt sentence case, nothing in mono under 12 pt; (6) Lookbook's stats outshouted the hero and "Most fits: Put together" was an average in disguise: two counts at 30 pt, the hero score at 36 pt, past months show only a count, an "Up next" card (the career fair, a fact he told his stylist) pulls him forward; (7) no way to share: share buttons on the look page and the hero; (8) the share card showed the occasion ("Date") and a lone score: it now leads with the look and a fit breakdown of the pieces, the URL at 13 pt / 80%, offered at 7.0+ or after a re-check win; the before/after names the fix; (9) Closet led with a chore: questions are one 52 pt row; (10) white pieces vanished on white tiles (1.20:1): a fine outline on light and dark pieces, dark tiles one step lighter with a border; (11) the next buy suggested sneakers he owns: never a kind he owns, marked "+" (dashed keeps "no photo yet", renamed from "Only described"); (12) Settings lacked Restore purchases, the Privacy policy and Terms (App Review 3.1.1, 5.1.1(i), 3.1.2): added; all 3 notifications get switches; deletes in red, confirmed; (13) the gear was drawn as a sun: a real gear; (14) the done fix was struck through (reads as cancelled) and showed +0.75 next to a +1.0 header: a check and "Done: …" with one number; (15) "Worn 4×" counted fit checks: "4 fit checks"; (16) the score reveal's random digits looked like a slot machine: a tape slides to the number; the full show only for the first 5 checks and re-check wins, then a 0.5 s lock; below 6.0 no animation or haptic, the score and its fix land together; VoiceOver hears the score once and skips the animation; the capture flash becomes a soft 40% dip.
    - **Accessibility checklist for the build (not drawable in a static mockup):** stats rows and the 3-column grids turn into lists at AX3+ (closet 2 columns at AX1–2), the Lookbook 1 column at AX3+; the hang-tag/name and Edit get their own rows at large text; 44 pt targets for Edit, See all 9, the Yes/No buttons and the segmented control (bold selected label + border, or native); VoiceOver labels: "5 feet 10 inches", "was 8.0, now 9.0", "Occasion: date. October 22", the style card as one element with "first pick", closet tiles with Rename/Remove actions, the fit tape read as "Regular, 2 of 4".
    - **Ideas kept for later:** "Moves you've got down" (fixes he keeps doing) as progress that isn't a score; a "Hide scores in Lookbook" option; a transparent sticker export of the share card; Stylist answering "what did I wear on my last date?".
    - **Caveat:** the psychology critic's citations (privacy calculus, objectification theory, Kluger & DeNisi on feedback, Etkin on measurement) are from memory and weren't link-checked.

The rest keeps its score." (layer and top say "is"; with no single slot, "Re-check" / "Only what you changed is read again. The rest keeps its score."). 247. **A drawn figure comes back, for advice (founder, Oct 14: "use it as an avatar… when recommending fits… inside the closet Outfits section… it is for advice… frame it well in onboarding… ask if they're skinny or heavy, in a more polite manner… make it clear we're there to help"). Mockups `mockups/figure-avatar.html`; renders `screenshots/theme-round2/fig-*.png`.** Reverses part of #84 ("no avatar") and the #246 removal of the profile figure.
    - **Why the old reasons don't carry over:** #84 rejected AI avatars and AI try-on (reviewers: they distort bodies and garments). This is a code-drawn, faceless figure in one neutral grey wearing his own drawn pieces, so nothing is generated or flattered. #246 removed a *measured* body (a ruler and his height in copper); the figure keeps no ruler and no numbers.
    - **Where it shows:** Closet → Outfits ("Try next": outfits he can make, on his figure, with a reason line; worn outfits keep his real neck-down photo and real score, "Your go-tos"), an outfit page (figure, pieces, why it works, Wear this tomorrow / Swap a piece / Ask about it), the morning card's idea, the You style card (wearing his best look of the month). **Never** on a score card, next to a score, or in the Why sheet.
    - **Onboarding Basics:** "A bit about you · So the cuts and fits I suggest work for you. This never changes your score, and you can change it any time." Build as five figure tiles, Slim · Average · Athletic · Broad · Bigger, plus "Rather not say" (a neutral average figure); fit Fitted / Regular / Relaxed; height optional; birth year to its own step. Then the stylist introduces the figure: "This is you for outfit ideas… It's only for advice. I score the clothes, never you." Today's build options are Slim / Average / Broad, so this adds two and a skip.
    - **Guardrails:** no face, no skin tone, no muscles or realistic proportions, no weight or measurements; advice talks about clothes ("a straight leg balances a roomy top"), never "slimming" or hiding; build never changes a score (unchanged).

249. **(Renumbered from a second #247.) "Which one is you?" works (S23; the founder's re-check dead end, Oct 14, #248).** Before: two people in a photo gave a toast with no way to answer. Now a question card in Stylist shows his photo with an outline on each person; a tap picks him (outline turns copper), the others are covered in flat grey in every copy sent (faces still hidden), and the check (or re-check, with its parent and rules) runs as usual; Cancel counts nothing and deletes the temporary photo. Native `preparePerson` in `KittedPrep.swift` re-runs prep on the same file for the tapped box (overlap ≥ 0.5); a picked photo's hash is photo hash + person, so picking someone else gets its own reading.
    - **Fewer false asks (Claude's call, the helper's):** ask only when another person's box is ≥ 25% of the main person's area; smaller people are now covered, not just face-blurred. Apple's person instance mask (iOS 17+) drops a duplicate box of him (≥ 80% his pixels), covers a person-less box (poster, window) without asking, and promotes a missed main person. On 704 saved photos "Which one is you?" goes 3 → 1 (the one left is a real second person at 35% of his size); 17 photos now have background people covered; noPerson and couldNotFrame unchanged. Harness: `kmeasure people`.
    - **New copy:** `whichOne.hint` "Tap yourself. Everyone else is hidden before it's sent." plus VoiceOver lines. Demo screen 79. **Needs a reinstall** (native prep changed); his own Oct 14 photo wasn't kept (prep refused it), so its cause (himself twice, a reflection, a second person) is unknown.
    - **Known:** the grey can nip his outline where another person's box overlaps him; the colour measuring after the closet call still sees two people on his unblurred kept photo (cut-outs come from the covered copy, so others' clothes stay out of his closet).

## Oct 14 · The You tab built (theme session; numbered from #250, as parallel sessions keep colliding below it)
250. **You, Style profile, What your stylist knows, Settings, Plan & usage, Notifications, Acknowledgements and Privacy & data are built (founder, Oct 14: "go ahead with the build order, start on You"). Built by 3 parallel builders on separate files; `npm run check` green (510 tests); seen on the Simulator in light and dark (demo screens 17, 80–82, 84–89; `screenshots/you-build/`, git-ignored).** Follows the spec (02 D17, Y2–Y7) with the approved revised design (#246).
    - **You:** a gear (top right) to Settings; the style card (picks numbered 1·2·3, first filled; one ink3 basics line, no height if none; the whole card opens the editor; a comment marks where the #247 figure goes); a Pro row with a new `WovenLabel tone="pro"` (indigo, copper stitches); "What your stylist knows" with source lines ("You told me · Oct 20", "From a fit check · Oct 12"); a guess is asked as "Do you …?" with Yes / Not really; the Today card ("1 free check today" / "Today's check: done · next at 4 am"). Seed facts rewritten in the second person; the money guess is gone.
    - **Style profile:** height, build (still 3: the 5-build set waits on the founder's OK of the words, #247), fit, birth year read-only, styles (up to 3, numbered; same `[style, weight]` writing as onboarding), budget with "Prefer not to say" (asked, never guessed); Save when changed. **"How bold" left out:** `profile.boldness` feeds scoring today (the bright-colour clash and the focal allowance in `parts.ts`), so editing it would move scores; it needs a decision (to-do).
    - **What your stylist knows + the fact sheet:** delete with Undo ("Forgot it."), edit inline, guesses "That's right / Change it". Delete is a long-press menu plus a VoiceOver action (no swipe: the app has no `GestureHandlerRootView`). No "Show the check" link: facts don't store their check id.
    - **Settings:** Notifications · Plan & usage · Privacy & data; Appearance (System/Light/Dark, saved, applied at launch) and Units; Manage subscription (opens Apple's subscriptions page) and Restore purchases (mock store); Acknowledgements (Archivo, OFL text); footer `Kitted 0.1.0 (1) · Scoring v1.8.0` from real constants. **Hidden until filled:** Report a wrong score, Contact support, Privacy policy, Terms (`app/src/config/links.ts`: `SUPPORT_EMAIL`, `PRIVACY_URL`, `TERMS_URL`, all null; App Review needs the policy and terms before submission).
    - **Plan & usage, Notifications:** the status card and today's counts; the three switches and the idea time are saved (KV `notify.*`, `ideaTime`) for P6 to read; Try Pro hidden while the paywall is a placeholder; no trial state exists yet (Pro shows "Kitted Pro").
    - **Privacy & data:** the honest promise ("Your photos are saved only on this iPhone. To score a fit, a copy with your face blurred goes to Google's Gemini. Google keeps it up to 55 days to check for misuse and doesn't train on it."), **See what I send** (the newest upload copy and its close-ups from `Caches/upload`), **Score my fits** (off clears consent and cancels a read in flight; on re-opens the consent sheet), photo count and size, and red Delete all photos / Forget what your stylist knows / Delete all my data with confirmations. Delete-all wipes every table, KV, all photo folders, logs and the Keychain device id, resets appearance, keeps the age block, and returns to Welcome.
    - **Consent is now enforced everywhere it should be:** fit check start, Try again and "Which one is you?" stop before prep with `noConsent` (they used to prep the photo and rely on the source refusing); a missing profile no longer counts as consent; the closet call uses the same rule.
    - **Known gaps:** the Stylist "Turn on fit checks" card (S26) isn't built (with scoring off, the camera shows the consent sheet); the morning card hides with consent off although the footer says outfit ideas still work (spec contradiction, left); `Caches/upload` copies are never pruned (suggest a week-long prune in Swift, like `Caches/detail`); not yet tried on the phone.
    - **New copy to skim (founder):** `en.ts` blocks headed "You tab (Oct 14)"; two lines are the builders' own: the free plan's status line "A fit check with its re-check and {questions} questions, every day" and "Closed" for a re-check whose window passed.
251. **The score reveal and the re-check win are built (founder, Oct 14: "go", build order step 2; spec #242 + #246 (16)). Seen on the Simulator frame by frame at quarter speed and recorded at real speed in dark (`screenshots/reveal/score-reveal-dark.mp4`, `recheck-win-dark.mp4`, git-ignored). `npm run check` green (543 tests).**
    - **First check:** "–.–" waits in the numeral's own slot; a tape of half-step values with ruler ticks slides in and settles on the score (`Easing.out(cubic)`), never passing through a wrong final value; one Rigid haptic at the lock; the word rises, the summary follows, then the fixes, the first gain's copper last. The tape window has shaded edges (card colour → clear) so digits fade into the card like a tape measure's housing instead of being cut (Claude's fix after the first Simulator pass showed sliced digits).
    - **Re-check win:** the old score (drawn at full size, then scaled into the small slot; given its own width after it first rendered as "8." with the 0 cut off) shrinks and fades to ink3, the copper arrow slides in, the tape rises to the new score, Success haptic (the only one in the app) and the +1.0 pill springs in, the done fix's badge fills and checks (no strike-through, #246), the summary rises. No flourish, copper motion or haptic when the score didn't rise.
    - **Rules:** once per check (`details.revealedAt`, written inside the closet write queue; plus an in-run guard), only for a check that was still reading in this run; tap to skip; the full show for the first 5 full shows (KV `reveal.fullShown`; quiet low scores don't use them), then a 0.5 s lock; below 6.0 one quiet 200 ms fade with the fix, no haptic; Reduce Motion: cross-fades, haptic kept; VoiceOver: final state at once and one announcement. Starts only when the score row is on screen (measured, 3 s fallback); only opacity and transforms move, so the card never changes height.
    - **For recording:** `-demoReveal 1` re-arms demo screens 7 (first check), 10, 11 and **12 (the seeded 8.0 → 9.0 re-check, FC3R: the only demo re-check that rises today)**; `-demoRevealSlow N` slows every timing. e.g. `xcrun simctl launch <udid> com.trykitted.kitted -demoScreen 12 -theme dark -demoLatency 0 -demoReveal 1`.
    - **Known:** demo screen 10's story re-check reads "change not seen" under today's scoring, so it shows no win; a new demo before/after pair would fix it.

## Oct 14 · The consent loop fixed (founder: the agreement "keeps on looping" after he picks a photo)
252. **Consent is asked once, inside the camera, before any photo; "Agree" is always kept (founder, Oct 14: "this agreement thing should only pop up right when they press the camera… and then never again").**
    - **Cause:** two consent rules disagreed. The camera's gate (`hasConsent`) let a store with no profile through, so the camera opened; #250's upload guard (`mayUpload`) refused it, so the photo came back `noConsent` and the consent sheet opened after he'd picked a photo. "Agree" then saved nothing (`giveConsent` only updated an existing profile), closed the sheet and reopened the camera: the loop. His phone has no profile because that install skipped onboarding (`skipOnboarding` + the firstRun scenario, which seeds no profile).
    - **Fix:** (1) `giveConsent` makes a profile with blank basics when there is none, so an agreement is always saved; (2) `hasConsent` is now the same rule as `mayUpload` (a saved agreement), so the camera asks exactly when a photo would be refused; (3) the consent screen shows **in the camera's place** on the first open ("Agree" turns it into the camera with no extra animation; "Not now" or X closes); (4) if a photo is ever held back anyway (scoring turned off mid-way, Try again), it waits: "Agree" on the sheet scores that same photo instead of sending him back to the camera, and closing the sheet discards it. Demo screens skip the gate (bundled photos never leave the phone).
    - **Checked:** a new test for the no-profile case; the web preview with `resetData` + `skipOnboarding` (his phone's state): camera tap → agreement → Agree → camera → auto-capture scored with no second ask → camera reopened with no ask. 544 app tests, typecheck and lint green. JS only: on his phone through Metro, no reinstall.
260. **App icon brainstorm (founder, Oct 14: "brainstorm the app icon first, you can use agents"). 1 researcher, 3 designers (brand mark, object/metaphor, score/measure: 12 concepts), 2 critics (a brand designer, a simulated 5-guy panel), then 3 refined finalists. Board `mockups/icon-concepts/index.html` (`node tools/mockshots/icon-board.js` rebuilds it), finalists and a test sheet in `mockups/icon-concepts/finalists/`.** The founder picks.
    - **Research:** of 23 rival icons, 16 sit on plain black or white, 7 use a tee or hanger, 7 are a single letter; looksmax apps own neon purple and faces, AI apps own sparkles. **Nobody uses indigo/denim or a sewing motif** (stitches, rivets, labels): free territory. A lone K is crowded: Kick (green pixel K, popular with young men), KAYAK (white K on an orange-copper gradient, close to a copper K), Klarna. iOS 26: few bold shapes, no baked effects, must survive tinted/clear (the shape carries recognition; a near-white element).
    - **What both critics dropped:** every tape-measure/ruler/score concept (they read as "measuring your body", "you're an 8", or a finance/stats app; a ruler with an 8 invites an easy joke), the hang tag (sales/upsell), the coil (a snail or "@"), plain K's (Kick/KAYAK).
    - **Finalists:** (1) **Button on the pocket**: a copper shank button with an indigo two-piece K at the end of a chalk pocket seam on indigo twill (the seam keeps it from reading as a coin); strongest silhouette at 29 pt; risk: still coin-like at a glance; use a button-only crop for the TikTok avatar. (2) **Woven label**: the KITTED neck label on a collar curve, copper stitches, a two-piece K; reads fastest as clothes; risk: could look like a waistband, small copper. (3) **Label + check**: the label with one bold copper check; says "clothes, checked"; risk: looks most like a to-do app, no K.
    - **Claude's recommendation:** (1) as the app icon, (2) as the brand mark for labels and share cards; confirm with a 10-minute story test before locking ("What does this app do?" open question, one icon per story; drop any icon a third of people misread). Apple's Product Page Optimization can A/B test alternate icons after launch if they ship in the build.

## Oct 14 · The re-check camera note names every piece that's read again
253. **"Re-check · pants" showed on a card whose fixes were for pants, top and layer (founder, Oct 14: "the camera app says that the only thing that's going to get rechecked is the pants").**
    - **Cause:** the note (#248) took only the card's first fix (`recheckFocus`: `fixes[0]`), carried over from the old chat line (02 D10), which was written when a card had one main fix. The re-check itself was right: `recheckMerge` re-reads every piece any fix on the card touches (`cardFixTargets` of all the fixes), and also takes any piece swapped for a different kind or colour; the rest keeps its first reading. His 11:20 card (Swap to your Faded black jeans · Swap to your Orange plaid flannel · Add your Navy chore coat) re-reads pants, top and layer, while the camera named only the pants.
    - **Fix:** new `fitCheck.recheckSlots()` returns every slot the fixes touch, top to toe; the camera says "Re-check · layer, top and pants" / "Your layer, top and pants are read again. The rest keeps its score." One piece keeps the old note; a fix that touches every piece (sleeves) or no fix keeps "Only what you changed is read again." `recheckFocus` now returns a slot only when there's exactly one. No scoring change. New copy `cam.recheck.bodyList`, `cam.recheck.and` (built with these words; on the founder's skim list). Test in `fitCheckService.test.ts`; 545 app tests green. JS only. Not seen on screen: no demo screen opens the re-check camera.
254. **The re-check camera says the whole fit is read again (founder, Oct 14: naming pieces "is very misleading"; supersedes #253's piece list).** A re-check sends the whole photo and the AI reads every piece; what counts: the pieces the card's fixes are about (taken fresh, better or worse), any piece swapped for a different kind or colour, and shoes newly in the photo; everything else keeps its first reading, and a small change the card didn't ask for (sleeves rolled) counts only when it clearly costs points (recheckMerge, unchanged). So any note naming pieces read as "nothing else is looked at". Now one note on every re-check: "Re-check" / "I'll read your whole fit again and score what you changed. Everything else keeps its first reading." The piece-list copy from #253 (`cam.recheck.bodyList`, `cam.recheck.and`) and the per-piece notes are removed; `recheckSlots`/`recheckFocus` stay for the old chat line. Seen in the web preview on a live re-check camera; 545 app tests green. JS only.
261. **App icon, woven-label refinement (founder, Oct 14: "the woven one does look better, but all three kind of look tacky… work on the woven label… keep at it").** Diagnosis of the tacky look: literal denim-twill stripes, a collar curve (reads as a waistband), loud orange stitch dashes, a chunky clip-art K, too big and busy. Two designers made 8 variants (`mockups/icon-concepts/woven/a/` quiet: flat label, tone-on-tone, loop, woven texture; `woven/b/` bold: label-as-icon, tag edge, copper thread, wordmark crop; board `woven-board.png`), then 2 blends (`woven/c/`, sheet `woven/c/sheet.png`): **c1** the quiet flat label scaled to 80% of the icon with a refined K and 4 slim copper stitches at each end; **c2** a chalk band crossing edge to edge with the refined K and stitch columns at both ends. Both flat (no gradients, shadows or textures), read as a sewn label at 29 pt, and keep the label and K in tinted mode. The heavy-K variants (b1, b4) were set aside: they drift toward a plain bold K (Kick, KAYAK). Claude's pick: c2 for the home screen (strongest at 29 pt), c1 if the founder wants the calmer one. The founder picks.
262. **App icon: a single K on navy (founder, Oct 14: liked c2's dark version, but the label concepts felt "a bit too much… tacky… or too dim… let's just go with a K with a navy background… refine it if you have a better idea"). `mockups/icon-concepts/k/` (sheet `k/sheet.png`, generator `gen.py`).** The K is drawn on the real Archivo Expanded Bold K from the app's font file (the wordmark's letter), wide, flat terminals, diagonals slightly heavier for small sizes, optically centred at 55.5% of the icon height; outlined paths only. Five variants, each light/dark/tinted: **k1** pure (soft navy grade; cleanest, most generic); **k2** a copper gusset in the arm-stem joint (a speck at 29 pt); **k3** two-tone navy (the c2-dark band, no stitches; smallest K); **k4** stencil cut (one 34 px cut between the stem and the arms, like a cut-and-sewn pattern piece; ~3 px at 29 pt, still visible, holds in tint); **k5** tailored (diagonals taper; only shows from 60 pt). Navy separates all five from Kick (green on black), KAYAK (white on orange) and Klarna (black on pink). Designer's pick k4 (ownable at no legibility cost; it degrades to k1), then k1. Claude agrees: k4. The founder picks.
263. **The app icon is k1: a single white K on navy (founder, Oct 14: "k1"). Built into the app and seen on the Simulator's home screen in light and dark (`screenshots/icon-k1/`, git-ignored).** `app/assets/expo.icon` (Icon Composer) now holds one layer, `k.svg` (the K drawn on Archivo Expanded Bold's K, `#F1F2F6`), over an `automatic-gradient` fill of navy `#1D2851`; translucency off so the K stays solid; iOS derives the dark, tinted and clear looks. Apple doesn't publish the `icon.json` schema and no confirmed example of a gradient with a dark override turned up (glama.ai Icon Composer MCP notes), so the bundle uses only keys the previous file already used. Also replaced Expo's placeholders: `icon.png` (1024, k1), `splash-icon.png` (the rounded icon), `favicon.png`, and the Android adaptive foreground/background/monochrome, with the Android background colour set to the navy. 03 §1 updated. Reaches the founder's phone with the next `tools/phone/install.sh` (an icon is native). The other concepts stay in `mockups/icon-concepts/` (#260–262).
248. **Scoring v12 from the founder's review of his 12 Oct 14 checks (default in Python and `@kitted/core`, parts data 1.9.0, fix catalog 1.6.0; 22,181 scoring and 25,796 fix parity cases exact; conformance 442; report `rubric/research/parts/v12.md`; review `screenshots/phone/20261014-180424/review.md`, tool `tools/phone/review.py`).**
    - **Bug, [COL-8]:** a bright tee with a small graphic was charged "the big, loud print" −1.5; now a bright graphic is loud only at a medium or large print or a large logo. His red tee with slides goes 6.0 → 7.5.
    - **Fitted legs (his taste over v10's softening: "all of these jeans were fitted… the silhouettes were bad"):** slim −1.0 again (ripped −1.5, skinny −2.0), and jeans read "straight" at roominess ≤ 2 are judged slim (PRO-13, PRO-11, FIT-3, FIT-6, SHO-9; not sweats, suit trousers or under tailoring). Fix rows that swap to a straight leg also set a regular fit (`pants_straight_fitted`).
    - **[COL-12] light on light (his call: "the cream shirt on cream pants looks so awkward"), −1.0 Color:** top (or layer) and bottoms both white, cream or khaki with nothing dark among the clothes; not a crisp white top over khaki (Claude's refinement: a textbook contrast), not suits; light grey left out; brown and burgundy count as dark. His creators Tim and Wanhee like tonal outfits with a range of shade, which needs the lightness reading; this is his taste call.
    - **[CON-6] wrinkles by garment and look (his call, researched in `rubric/research/notes/wrinkles-streetwear.md`):** knit tops never charged (a tee under tailoring −0.2); casual wovens free in streetwear, workwear, vintage sport and outdoor, −0.2 elsewhere; an oxford with jeans at Everyday or Class −0.2; dressy pieces −0.4; at an interview or event every woven keeps −0.4.
    - **Effect:** gold held-out r 0.53 → 0.49 (nearly all from the wrinkle change: the AI panel docks wrinkled tees on weak outfits); **the founder's 20 blind ratings r 0.36 → 0.46**, within 0.5 12 → 13 of 20; steadiness 216 → 218 of 223; 8+ share 16%. Of his 12 checks, 3 move (the red tee up 1.5; cream jeans read straight at roominess 2 and cream oxford + khaki slim chinos each down 0.5). Creators' pairs unchanged at 5 of 6.
    - **Open:** khaki over white and cream over khaki still charged; the red tee + slides at 7.5 may be generous; "Not quite right?" can't clear a fitted "straight" read by choosing straight (relaxed or wide does); "the fitted jeans…" as card wording is his call.
249. **Scoring v13: the wrinkle cost on tees and hoodies is a setting (founder, Oct 14: his pick of three).** [CON-6]'s knit-top amounts are now TUNE knobs (`wr_knit_clean`, `wr_knit_tailored`, `wr_half`; the `WR_HALF` constant is gone) and v13 sets: 0 when the outfit reads as streetwear, workwear, vintage sport, outdoor or no clear style; 0.2 when it reads as minimal, smart casual or classic prep and none of the loud-native looks; 0.2 for a tee under tailoring; only clear reads count. Parts data 1.10.0; 23,593 scoring and 26,300 fix parity cases exact; conformance 449. Nothing measured moves (gold, his 20, steadiness, saved checks, his Oct 14 checks, creators): only 10 of 450 gold readings have a wrinkled knit top and none reads as a clean look. Example: a wrinkled white tee with navy chinos and loafers 7.5 → 7.0. Open (his call): a plain tee + dark jeans + white sneakers reads as no clear style and stays free.

## Oct 14 · The waistline from the body (waistline session; founder's idea: the body as a ruler, the clothes set the visual waistline)
264. **The waist ruler doesn't beat the AI; nothing changes in the app (report `rubric/research/notes/waistline.md`, research `rubric/research/notes/waist-research.md`).**
    - **Built (harness only):** `kmeasure waist` (`extraction-test/vision/WaistRuler.swift`).
      - The body is only a ruler: the hip→shoulder axis in "torsos", with ANSUR II landmarks (navel and iliac crest ≈ 0.30 torsos above the hip joints, crotch ≈ 0.20 of the thigh below). Never reported, scored or stored.
      - The clothes: where the top's color stops and the pants' start across a torso strip, with a belt band and the visual split.
      - The legs: the pants' shin/thigh width.
      - Runner `waist_test.py`; paper test `waist_paper.py`; what-if switches `pr1_short` and `pr1_ruler_veto` in `parts_proto.py`, both off, so the live engine is unchanged.
    - **Truth:** 314 photos (his 33 phone checks, Oct 7, mirror, stock, 225 gold) labeled twice, blind, by Claude subagents from face-hidden copies (`extraction-test/waist_labels/`). The two passes agree on "the waist shows" only 79%, and the AI agrees with each 78–79%: the AI is at the labels' ceiling. Scored on the 182 photos both passes agree on. The cut was tuned on dev and frozen; test was scored once.
    - **Result (held out):** the ruler is right 80% when sure vs the AI's 82%, unsure 41% (bar ≤ 30%), 100% repeatable. On his photos 77% vs 64%, but only 13 answers. As a veto it fixes 0 and breaks 3 of 176 runs. The dark-on-dark edge fallback was a coin flip (AUC 0.47). The leg ratio was 48% when sure vs the AI's 61–66%, unsure 60% (repeats #223).
    - **Why:** the clothes edge is found well, but the hip joints, the ruler, sit 3–7 cm off, often at the waistband itself in mirror selfies. That error is the whole gap between "at the belt" and "covers it".
    - **His idea holds descriptively:** the visual split (upper share) is tucked 0.34 · waistband clearly shows 0.37 · partly 0.44 · covered 0.45 · mid-fly 0.49, each spreading about ±0.07.
    - **The Oct 14 misfires (the navy hoodie over wide jeans, the graphic tee over cream jeans) were read right:** the hem ends at a waistband that shows. The question is the rule.
    - **Paper test, both rejected:**
      - A, the credit only for a tuck or crop: gold held-out r 0.49 → 0.45, his 20 blind ratings r 0.46 → 0.39, 8+ share 15% → 5%.
      - B, the ruler cancels "at belt": unchanged on gold; on his checks it lowers 3, the hoodie 8.0 → 7.5, against both labelers.
    - **Decided (Claude, no taste involved):** adopt nothing and keep v13's waist credit. Absolute joint-based proportion measuring is done (top length tested three times: #170, #222, here).
    - **Open:**
      - His taste call: should a plain tee or hoodie that just reaches a visible waistband earn "gives it a waist"? Default: yes, as now, since the panel and his ratings agree.
      - Worth one test when his one-outfit variants exist: the change in the visual waistline between two photos of the same outfit (tucked vs untucked), where the joint error cancels. That's what a re-check needs.

## Oct 14 · The Stylist chat planned (founder: "plan out the chat, the chat system and stylist… an in-depth plan")
265. **The chat's plan is written: `handoff/07-stylist-chat.md` (new reference doc; 02 S25 and S30 point to it). Nothing in the app changed.** Built from three surveys: the chat code today (send replies "Questions are coming soon…", `consumeQuestion` never called, no answer source, no server), every earlier chat spec (02 S25–S27/S30, app-build-spec `/v1/ask`, brainstorm 02 and 11, the age and consent rules), and every piece of user data the app stores (profile, facts, closet, checks, ideas, weather, usage).
    - **Shape, decided by Claude (no taste involved):** "the code knows, the AI talks". Every fact and number an answer stands on is computed on the phone by `@kitted/core` (outfits via `pickOutfit` with a piece pinned, "+N outfits" via `rotation`, a check's Why rows and fix targets) and sent as evidence. The model words it and may attach one card from a fixed list (outfit, start a check, one tap question, buy, link, saved fact); code builds every card from validated handles. **Why:** the only thing this chat can do that free ChatGPT can't is answer about his clothes and checks with numbers that agree with the card (`market/market-research.md`).
    - **What's sent with a question:** today's chat only, picked looks, fit preference, budget, live facts, the closet as one line per piece with a handle, today's checks in full, 7 days short, today's idea, the weather in words, the date. **Never:** birth year or age, height, build, name, location, photos or readings, deleted facts, earlier days' chats. Memory is the facts list he can see, not transcripts.
    - **Memory:** a `remember` action with a fixed key list (sizes, budget, dress code, owned-not-photographed pieces, color and fit likes); always shown with "Saved to what I know · Undo"; body, health, age and the like are never stored. Chat-mentioned pieces join the closet as unphotographed and stay out of "+N" claims until a check sees them.
    - **Provider:** Gemini (consent already names Google; one processor and one key). The model, `gemini-3.8-flash` or a Lite tier, is picked by a test, not by name.
    - **Counting:** only an answer that arrives counts. Refusals, failures, distress and the stylist's own follow-up taps don't.
    - **Starter chips:** they stay, inside the opened composer while it's empty (closes the open question from #180).
    - **One question in flight;** the message is stored once, when complete (`useLive` reloads the whole chat table on every patch).
    - **The buy list:** free for everyone. 02 S30's "full list is Pro" broke "everything that runs on code is free" (CLAUDE.md wins).
    - **Money:** an estimated 0.08–0.15¢ per question (brainstorm 02; to be measured in step C2). A yearly Pro user at the 50-a-day cap every day could cost about what he pays (~$1.20–2.25 vs ~$2.12 a month after Apple's cut), before Gemini's Jan 2027 price rise. Guards: the cached prefix, short answers, today-only history, the spend stop, a per-device token budget.
    - **Founder calls, each with a default:**
      - D1, the model's voice (a blind pick on 20 pairs);
      - D2, consent adds "your style picks" (bumps CONSENT_VERSION to 2);
      - D3, numbers for a "what if" on a check he already has (yes, "on that photo");
      - D4, the paused line.
    - **Build order:** C0 plumbing → C1 the pack → C2 one real answer (dev key) → C3 router, evidence and cards → C4 memory → C5 the test → C6 the server route → C7 polish. About 6–7 days of building; C0–C4 reach his phone through Metro, with no server needed.

## Oct 14 · Where proportions stand (founder: "but what are we going to do about the proportions")
266. **Measured on paper, at no cost: Proportions is no longer stuck at 7.0.** v13 on the 200 scored gold photos (run 0): exactly 7.0 on 32% (v6: 60%); spread 4.0–8.5; it follows the panel's overall score at r 0.52 (v6: 0.22).
    - **What fires:** the waist break on 74 photos (+1.5), a narrow leg on 24, the short layer on 29, the tailored line on 6.
    - **The weak spot:** the biggest single rule in the score sits on the softest reading. The waist break fires on 37% of photos, and two blind labelers agree on "the waist shows" only 79% (#264).
    - **Still unreadable:**
      - the stack at the shoe (69%, #238), which all four creators call make-or-break;
      - where a jacket ends, and a top running long;
      - the top's width against the pants.
    - **Decided (Claude):**
      - (a) No more absolute phone measuring. Recommend stopping the scripts, not pausing them (the founder's to-do item).
      - (b) The founder's one-outfit variants are the next truth for all of it: the stack (cuffed, rolled, hitched), the waist break's +1.5 on real tucked/untucked pairs, slim vs straight, jacket on/off. Also the one script test left: the change between paired photos.
      - (c) In the chat, "shape" is its own kind of question (`07-stylist-chat.md` §5.1). The evidence says what the photo couldn't show, and the stylist may ask about it for advice only, never points.
    - **Re-checks:** a fix the photo can't confirm (a cuff) is already handled by the "What changed?" sheet (S21).

## Oct 15 · Chats on every device; height and build to the stylist (founder: "if the user signs into a different device, they shouldn't lose all their data"; "I doubt sending … build … without … their name would be a bad thing. Check")
267. **Two changes to the chat plan (`07-stylist-chat.md` §3, §7.1, §10, new §10b).**
    - **Height and build are sent to the model when he gave them** (reverses #265's "never"). He was right.
      - **Not identifying:** no name, no photo, and requests go through our proxy with our key, so Google can't tie them to a person.
      - **Not health data:** a coarse clothing-build word and a height aren't "health status" under the strictest US law, Washington's My Health My Data Act (RCW 19.373.010). Weight and body composition likely would be, so weight is never asked for, stored or sent.
      - **Gemini's paid terms:** no training on it, abuse logs up to 55 days, as for questions.
      - **The real risk is the output:** the stylist talking about his body. Hence "use it, never say it": the build changes which advice it gives (size for the shoulders, the inseam), the answer names only clothes, the sentence gate drops any sentence that names his build or height back to him, and the red-team set gains build-profile body questions.
      - **Consent:** D2's wording now names height and build.
      - **Still never sent:** birth year or age (no gain, and it invites talking down to him), name, location.
    - **His data on another device:**
      - **Today:** everything is in `Documents/` (SQLite, photos, scans, cut-outs) with no backup opt-outs, so iCloud Backup and Quick Start already carry it to a new iPhone. Untested.
      - **Recommended after the $99 program:** sync every table to his own private iCloud database through Apple's CKSyncEngine (WWDC23 10188, iOS 17+, built for apps with their own SQLite store). No account, no sign-in, we never hold the data, so the "our server stores no photos" promise holds. About 3–4 days.
      - **Not recommended:** our own accounts. We'd hold photos and chats, App Review 5.1.1(v) would require in-app account deletion, onboarding would gain a sign-in step, plus storage costs and the security burden.
      - **Open:** whether data in a user's private CloudKit database counts as "collected" for the privacy label. Apple doesn't say. Apple's definition is data sent off the device that the developer or partners can access, and data collected only by Apple needn't be declared. Ask App Review at a Meet with Apple session before submitting.
      - **Accepted:** free limits stay per device (two devices get two free checks a day).
    - **The founder's call (to-do list):** iCloud sync vs accounts. Default: iCloud.

## Oct 15 · Our own database for accounts and sync (founder: "If the app does good, we're definitely moving to Android … I'd rather settle things right now … What if we use Firebase or Supabase?")
268. **Plan: `handoff/08-accounts-and-sync.md`. It replaces #267's iCloud sync, which can't reach Android.**
    - **Recommended (the founder's call, E1):** Firebase for accounts, the synced rows and photos. The Cloudflare Worker stays the AI proxy (no cold starts) and checks Firebase's sign-in and App Check tokens.
    - **Why Firebase:**
      - Google is already named in consent as the AI processor.
      - It has the least code to write: guest accounts that upgrade with Apple or Google, live listeners for the changes-down half, resumable uploads.
      - App Check covers App Attest and Play Integrity for free, which replaces our own attestation work.
      - Android is first-class.
    - **Ranked below it:**
      - Supabase: $25/mo from day one (the free tier pauses after a week idle), no offline sync without PowerSync ($49/mo), adds a company, but the easiest exit.
      - Cloudflare-only: the cheapest at scale, but the most security code written by us.
      - iCloud: no Android.
    - **Cost:** ~$0, ~$8, ~$42 a month at 1k, 10k, 50k monthly users. Assumptions: 60% sign in, ~40 MB of photos per signed-in user after a year, prices from the providers' pages on Oct 15.
    - **The real margin risk is Gemini, not storage:** ~$42, $415, $2,075 a month at the Jan 2027 price, against ~$60, $600, $3,000 revenue. Flagged for its own review.
    - **Duties our own database adds:**
      - in-app account deletion (App Review 5.1.1(v)) plus a web deletion page (Google Play);
      - a privacy label of "Data linked to you" (Photos, User content, User ID);
      - the Play Data safety form;
      - a consent and policy rewrite (the "our server stores no photos" promise ends for people who sign in);
      - per-account access rules;
      - a breach plan;
      - a lawyer review.
    - **BIPA:** it excludes photographs, and the 2025 Seventh Circuit Samsung Gallery ruling turned on face data that stayed on the device. So: never run face detection or recognition on our servers.
    - **Settled by Claude:**
      - The phone's SQLite stays the real copy, with background sync and newest-edit-wins per row; chat only appends.
      - Sign-in is optional and never in onboarding. It's offered after the 3rd check and in Settings.
      - The full photo stays on the phone that took it; other devices get 1600 px.
      - Synced data of accounts unused for 18 months is deleted after a warning.
      - **Settle-now prep before the beta (~1 day):**
        - migration V7: `updated_at` on every table and soft deletes;
        - IDs stay UUIDs (already true);
        - the Worker's limits keyed on an account ID;
        - the policy written with optional backup in mind.
    - **Build:** ~10–12 days (A1 accounts 2, A2 sync 4–5, A3 rules 1, A4 Worker 0.5, A5 deletion 1.5, A6 privacy 1, A7 ops 0.5), as v1.1. Android itself is a separate 3–5 weeks (native modules: face blur, body tracking, dictation, location, camera guidance).

## Oct 15 · Accounts decided; the Gemini bill priced per user (founder: "All right, add these to the common files … how much would it cost for Gemini calls per user? Check that")
270. **E1–E3 of #268 are decided as recommended** (numbered 270: a parallel waistline session took 269 the same morning) (the founder's "All right"): Firebase for accounts, data and photos, with the Worker staying the AI proxy; the ~1-day prep before the beta and the build as v1.1; "Back up photos" on by default once signed in. E4 (creating the Firebase project) stays on his list.
    **The Gemini bill, priced from the app's own token logs and Google's pricing page (Oct 15): `market/ai-costs.md`.**
    - **Per call** (`gemini-3.8-flash`: $0.75 in / $3.75 out per million tokens, doubling on Jan 1, 2027):
      - scoring read with close-ups: 4,118 / 764 tokens, 0.60¢;
      - second look: 0.23¢;
      - closet call: 1,567 / 364 tokens, 0.25¢;
      - **= 1.08¢ a check now, 2.16¢ from 2027.** #268 used only the read (0.83¢), which was too low.
    - **Chat question** (estimate; the prompt is ~8,700 tokens because the rules plus the guide are ~5,600):
      - 0.34¢ cached on 3.8 Flash (0.68¢ in 2027);
      - 0.16¢ on 3.5 Flash-Lite ($0.30 / $2.50, no rise announced);
      - ~0.10¢ with the guide trimmed to the question's sections.
    - **Per user, per month, in 2027:**
      - free light $0.28;
      - free every day $1.27;
      - Pro typical $2.12 (his yearly plan pays $2.12 after Apple's 15%);
      - Pro at the caps $23.
    - **Per 100 monthly users** at 2% paying: about $31 of AI against about $6 of revenue; free users are ~85% of it.
    - **Settled by Claude:**
      - The chat starts on 3.5 Flash-Lite with the guide trimmed by kind (§0 always plus the router's 2–4 sections). 3.8 Flash only if the C5 test shows Flash-Lite clearly worse (`07-stylist-chat.md` §9, D1 default updated).
      - Try the closet call on the Flex tier (half price; nobody waits on it).
      - Log the hedge rate. The 5 s backup request may double the scoring read's cost when no garment has streamed yet; it's probably rare (garments stream early), but unmeasured.
    - **The founder's calls:**
      - (a) ~$1–2 to test the scoring read on 3.5 Flash-Lite (≈0.32¢ vs 1.19¢ in 2027), against the gold set and steadiness;
      - (b) the free tier and the Pro caps, after (a). Everything on Flash-Lite would be ~0.48¢ a check and ~0.10¢ a question: break-even at 2% paying, so it still needs better conversion or a lighter free tier.

## Oct 15 · The waistline from the body, round 2 (founder: "there must be a way to measure a person's body and estimate where their waistline is … put some effort into this")
269. **A body ruler good to ±2–3 cm, and the measured waistline carries signal the AI misses. It revises #266(a): keep the waistline measure alive; the tape shoot decides it. Nothing in the app changed** (report: `rubric/research/notes/waistline.md`, top section).
    - **Downloads (founder OK'd, Oct 15):**
      - ANSUR II men (public domain CSV, `inputs/ansur2/`, git-ignored);
      - MediaPipe Pose Landmarker heavy (Apache 2.0; venv `extraction-test/.venv-mp`, 331 MB; model `inputs/mediapipe/`).
    - **Body data:** shoulders + knees predict the navel to ±2.1 cm (1 SD) and the hip bone to ±1.9, as well as the true hip joint does. So the ruler drops the hip points, the least reliable ones and the ones the clothes move.
    - **Built (harness only):**
      - `vision/BodyRuler.swift`: rulers from 2D shoulders/knees (straight), shoulders/knees/ankles (projective, which corrects camera tilt and height, with and without a tilt prior), and Apple's 3D body pose;
      - `kmeasure waist --pitch` (the photo as a camera tilted ±10°, an exact homography);
      - `waist_mediapipe.py` (MediaPipe's 2D and 3D rulers);
      - `waist_rulers.py` (tests);
      - `waist_paper2.py` (the score on paper);
      - `WAIST_TAPE=1` and `waist_tape.py` (the tape shoot; the detector was checked on a painted strip, within 0.004 of the true line).
    - **Precision, no labels needed:**
      - Apple's 2D and MediaPipe's 2D agree to SD 1.9 cm.
      - The same outfit across his photos reads within ~1 cm.
      - A ±10° tilt shifts a reading by a median 1.6–3.1 cm.
      - Apple's 3D pose is too noisy (4–6 cm) and is dropped.
      - The systematic offset is unknown (1–4 cm between rulers).
    - **The labels:** "does the waistband show" still doesn't improve (AUC 0.80–0.92 for every ruler, the old one included). It depends on the pants' rise.
    - **The waistline itself:**
      - On 105 gold photos the measured line (`sk_linear`) tracks the judges' Proportions at rank correlation −0.46, against −0.29 for the AI's length.
      - Inside the AI's "at the belt" group it's −0.48.
      - Proportions by measured line: near the navel 7.5, between the navel and the hip joint 7.4, at or just below the hip joint 6.9, well below 6.0.
    - **On paper:**
      - C1 (the line replaces the AI's length): no gain.
      - C2 (C1 + a measured long top costs 1.5, what-if `pf2_measured`, off): held-out panel r 0.49 → 0.51, Proportions 0.50 → 0.58, his 20 unchanged at 0.46. Every new ruler points the same way; the old hip ruler doesn't.
      - 8 of his saved checks would move down, e.g. the hoodie 8.0 → 7.5, the untucked brown shirt 7.0 → 6.0.
      - Within the noise of 91 photos, so not a proposal yet.
    - **Decided (Claude):**
      - Adopt nothing yet.
      - Keep the waistline measure (sleeves, hems, legs, "waistband shows" stay stopped).
      - Ask the founder for the 10-minute tape shoot (P1 on his list). If the projective rulers land within ~3 cm of the tape on both mirror and propped shots, C2 goes to him as a before/after. If not, the offset is calibrated once from his photos and checked on a friend.

## Oct 15 · The waistline, round 3: arms as a backup, the pants silhouette, the hem brief (founder: "add the arms as a backup"; "the lower part … close to as wide as the top"; "a prompt for another chat for the hem")
272. **Arms work as a backup only when they hang dead straight. The pants silhouette loses to the AI at naming the cut, but confirms the balance rule, which goes into the stylist's advice. The hem gets its own brief. Nothing in the app changed** (report: `rubric/research/notes/waistline.md`, round 3).
    - **Arms (ANSUR II):** a hanging elbow is at 110.5 cm, a wrist at 84.8. Shoulders + arms place the navel to ±2.2–2.3 cm.
      - In photos a loose "hanging arm" check spreads tucked shirts over 39 cm.
      - A strict one (elbow bend < 10°, within 12° of the body line; the default now) matches the knee ruler (spread 17.4 cm; AUC 0.89 / 0.86), but qualifies on ~1 photo in 5 and rescues only ~4 knee-less photos.
      - Built into `body_fit`, with a hip fallback (gold measured 147 → 152).
    - **Tilt prior lightened 0.2 → 0.02** (offline sweep, then a clean rerun, `results/waist4`). `body_fit` now moves a median 1.5 cm per 10° of camera tilt (no one-sided lean), but is noisier across photos of one outfit (2.6 vs 0.9 cm).
      - Judges: equal to `sk_linear` (−0.48 vs −0.47).
      - Paper: `sk_linear` held-out 0.51 / Proportions 0.58; `body_fit` 0.50 / 0.53.
      - **The tape shoot picks between them.**
    - **The pants silhouette** (`BodyRuler.silhouette`, widths in ruler-cm; `waist_silhouette.py`, `results/waist3/silhouette.md`):
      - Naming slim / straight / wide: 67% held out vs the AI's 75% on the same photos (the hem width runs slim 10.0, straight 12.4, wide 15.7 cm). Width alone can't name the cut (#189, #223 again).
      - Pants width against the top tracks the judges' Proportions (+0.42), but the AI's leg reading tracks it better (+0.56) and is already scored.
      - Decided (Claude): the cut stays on the AI. The balance rule and the measured waistline go into `rubric/stylist-guide.md` §4 as advice ("this tee ends at your hips, so your legs look shorter: tuck the front or pick a shorter, boxier tee"), never about his body.

      - the hem against the ruler's ankle bone;
      - the stack as width and fold lines in the last 15 cm;
      - the shoe as a second ruler;
      - the hem's outline;
      - left vs right;
      - inseam minus leg length with a tape measure as hard truth.

## Oct 15 · The hem from the body (founder: "start 'The hem from the body' … use the body ruler we built for the waistline … try the creative ideas … don't change the app until I've seen the before/after")
273. **The body ruler measures where the hem sits, and does it steadily. But none of the six ideas reads the stack better than the AI, alone or combined. Nothing in the app changed, so there is no before/after** (report: `rubric/research/notes/hem-ruler.md`; code `extraction-test/hem_ruler.py`; results `extraction-test/results/hem4/`).
    - **Built (Mac harness only):**
      - The joints: Vision's joints from the waist runs, plus MediaPipe's knees, ankles, heels, toes and outline.
      - The ruler: knee → floor (ANSUR knee 48.0 cm plus a stated 3 cm sole), because shoulders + knees extrapolated to the floor miss by −10 to +9 cm.
      - The hem: where the pants' color ends.
      - The ideas: 1, 2a, 2b, 3, 4 and 5 from the brief. Idea 6 needs a tape measure.
      - The test: the 78 hem3 photos, with cuts chosen on the tuning half and tested once.
    - **The ruler works:** the same pants on different days read within 0.1–1.7 cm, and tilting the photo ±10° moves the hem a median 1.0 cm.
    - **Hem height doesn't track the stack:** test AUC 0.35. The pants end where the shoe starts, whether there's a break or a stack.
    - **No idea beats the AI on folds above the shoe:**
      - The best AUCs on the test half are 0.57–0.69, and none holds on both halves except left/right fold contrast.
      - Left/right fold contrast is noise: 1 of 2 stacks found on tuning with 4 false alarms, 2 of 5 found on test, unsure on 12 of 19 stacks.
      - All of them combined: test AUC 0.51, against the AI's 57–59% on the same photos.
      - Drags: the AI is at 91%, and no idea comes close.
    - **Also learned:**
      - Finding the hem by the outline narrowing disagrees with the color finder by a median 9 cm.
      - Heel → toe as a shoe ruler is foreshortened in front-on photos (9–19 cm for ~29 cm shoes).
      - Black denim and jogger cuffs defeat the fold bands, as in #190.
    - **Decided (Claude):**
      - The stack stays a tip.
      - The hem height stays in the harness as a possible veto (a hem far above the shoe can't be a stack).
      - The direct measure is length, not position: the pants' inseam minus his leg's inseam. That's a closet fact, entered once per piece, not a photo fact. It's worth a field on the piece page later; added to the founder's list as optional.
      - His hem variants remain the truth any reader needs.
