Same size and layout. On the share screen a "Light | Dark" switch shows under Solid; it starts in the phone's own appearance (the screen itself is always dark, so it asks the system). 350. **The founder: "You can start planning out the implementations and also the test now."** The plan and its checks: `handoff/14-share-card.md` (steps 0–6; checks A1–A8, M1–M6, N1–N4, S1–S11, P0–P8, written before the code). A reviewer read the draft against the installed native code and found three real bugs in what #346–349 built, fixed now:
    - **The image would have been 3× too big:** view-shot's `width`/`height` are points at the device's scale (`rendererFormat.scale = 0`), so `width: 1080` on a 3× iPhone draws 3240 px. Now px ÷ `PixelRatio.get()`.
    - **Save could drop the transparency:** the legacy `saveToLibraryAsync` re-encodes through `UIImageWriteToSavedPhotosAlbum`. Now `Asset.create(file)` (adds the PNG file itself; add-only access is enough).
    - **Temp files were never deleted** (and `fileName` is ignored on iOS): now `releaseCapture` after a save, and 30 s after the share sheet closes.
    - **Settled:** a ready gate on the captured copy's images and font (resets on every switch; after 4 s "Couldn't make the image", never a different card); switches locked while busy; Open Settings when Photos is denied; the Mac checks read the copies in the Simulator's Photos, never the temp file; the Lookbook look page gets the share button.
    - **Step 0, before any build:** whether Instagram keeps a PNG's transparency (photo sticker, and Copy → Paste from the share sheet) decides the Transparent route. Test file `screenshots/share-card/test-sticker.png` (1080 × 600, RGBA).

    - **Other checks:** Apple Vision found no face in the neck-down card. The add-only prompt shows his words, Save says "Saved to Photos.", and Share opens the sheet with Copy in it.
    - **Found:**
      - the PNGs are 16-bit wide color (5 MB for the photo card; left as is for now);
      - the preview jumped when the status line appeared (fixed: it keeps its room).
    - **How it was built:**
      - xcodebuild into the existing Kitted DerivedData folder (`-derivedDataPath`) instead of a new one, to save ~3 GB on a near-full disk;
      - the installed dev app is now this branch's build, a superset of main's: main's JS still runs on it through Metro.
      - This branch's Pods were deleted afterwards.

352. **The founder: "build step 2 (the ready gate, lock while busy, Open Settings) and the Lookbook look page's share button, then the rest of the Simulator pass."** Built and checked (`handoff/14-share-card.md` §8):
    - **The ready gate:** it counts the captured copy's image loads and the font; it resets on every switch; after 4 s it says "Couldn't make the image", never a different card.
    - **Lock while busy:** one press at a time, and the switches lock while it runs.
    - **Open Settings** when Photos is denied.
    - **The look page's Share** sits in its header and opens the shown check, so a re-checked look opens its Before · After · Change.
    - **Three layout fixes the Simulator showed:**
      1. the preview is sized to the controls' measured height, so the largest text sizes and the Open Settings row never push the card under the title, and the screen scrolls past a 240 pt minimum;
      2. a lone share icon on the fit-check card is right-aligned;
      3. the look page's icon is plain, like the ⋯ beside it.

## Oct 19 · Overnight: a crash screen, the Latest pill, the morning card's Swap and location, outfit counting at large closets

353. **The founder (going to sleep): "Start any required tasks… make decisions… give me a report in the end. Branch off."** Four [code] leftovers of the gap audit (09 §20 items 6, 30, 34–35, 37) that no other session had; scope agreed by message with the §22.1 session. Plan and checks first (09 §23), results in 09 §23's results. Branch `claude/autonomous-work-414ef0`; merged Oct 19 (PR #25, founder: "You may merge PR 25.").
    - **A crash shows a screen.** expo-router's own `ErrorBoundary` at the root draws "Something went wrong." / "Kitted hit a problem on this screen. Your fits and closet are safe on this phone." with Try again and Go to Kitted (new outward words, on his skim list). Every uncaught JS error and every crash screen leaves one line in `Documents/logs/errors.log`, in Release too (time, where, name, message ≤200 characters, ≤5 frames ≤160 characters; ≤100 KB). Nothing is sent: a crash-reporting service is a package and his call. `pull.sh` prints it; Delete all my data removes it. Why no package: `ErrorBoundary` and `ErrorUtils` are built in, and a beta tester's crash then at least leaves a trace on the phone.
    - **The Latest pill (02 G3, 07 §6.1),** as specced: scrolled up more than a screen, or scrolled up while something new came in. A tap goes to the newest row and follows again. **Not built: Stop while an answer streams** (not in 07; answers are ≤200 tokens and take ~2 s).
    - **Swap never brings back a piece he swapped away** (kv `idea.swapped.<id>`, per device; cleared by a new occasion or style), and when nothing else works the card keeps its outfit and says "Nothing else in your closet works here yet." (02's `swap.none`; it used to blank the card). **Location denied:** a quiet "Turn on local weather" link to iOS Settings in the Allow row's place (iOS won't ask twice); back with it on, `freshen` gives the card its weather (it skipped a card with none) and re-plans it if he hasn't touched it.
    - **Outfit counting:** measured first: at 50 pieces Next best buy took 0.8 s on the Mac, at 100 pieces 5.3 s, blocking the JS thread. Now each judged outfit is remembered (by occasion, styles and the pieces' garments, so an edit is never stale), and a new piece's gain counts only the outfits it makes. 0.04 s and 0.12 s; the same answers as a frozen copy of the old code. The first count of a big closet is unchanged (~1 s at 100 pieces): that's the scoring itself, left for later.
    - **Lessons:** React retries a render that throws once, so a "throw once" trigger recovers unseen; the React Compiler keeps a render-time read of a module value (`getDemoArgs()`) from the first render, so read it in a component that mounts after it's set. On a case-insensitive disk `latestPill.ts` and `LatestPill.tsx` clash: tsc refuses.

357. **The founder: "You can go ahead and test it out on a simulator."** The two pieces of #353 not seen on a screen were tested on the Simulator (09 §23 "on the Simulator after the merge"): Swap kept every swapped-away shoe out and, with none left, kept the outfit and said so; the denied-location link shows and opens Settings. **And it found a real bug that the tests missed:** after #353 no toast appeared on Stylist (Logged for today, Undo toasts, the Swap line). The Latest pill's always-on animated style (reading the toast's room) and a re-render per scroll left the toast's entering animation stuck, the #118 symptom. Fixed and merged Oct 19 (PR #26, founder: "You may merge PR 26."): the pill's style mounts only with the pill, the pill hides while a toast shows, and the screen re-renders only when the pill turns on or off. Lesson: a reanimated style that reads a value another animation is driving can starve an unrelated entering animation on iOS; keep animated styles inside the component that's actually drawn, and check a toast on any screen whose animated styles change.

## Oct 19 · Kit's outfit ideas rotate like the morning card

358. **The founder: "Do they actually check the outfit history of the person? What if they repeatedly recommend the same thing again and again?"** The morning card did (rotation, Oct 14); Kit's outfit ideas didn't: the chat's picker got no history, so "What should I wear on a date?" could name yesterday's outfit and, for today, disagree with the card. His go: "give Kit's outfit ideas the same history as the morning card, and make its first pick for today agree with the card. Tests first." Plan, checks K1–K8 and results: `handoff/07-stylist-chat.md` §22. Merged Oct 19 (PR #27, founder: "You may merge PR 27.").
    - **The same history:** the card's history builder moved, unchanged, into `historyFor` (`features/morning/service.ts`); Kit calls it for the card of the moment he asks in. The chat snapshot now carries the wear log.
    - **Kit's own earlier outfit cards count as shown (Claude's call):** otherwise the same question on consecutive days could name the same outfit; today's don't count, so the same question twice in a day gets the same answer (the guide: "the same advice, shorter").
    - **The first pick for today is the card's outfit** when the occasion matches, and a question with no occasion takes the card's (never Staying in). Not when a piece was deleted or is one he said he avoids (his words beat the card). The evidence line says "same as today's card".
    - **Two bugs found on the way:** "Another" left out the first id, the layer when there was one, so it could keep the Pick's top; and in `@kitted/core` a pinned layer could be dropped for a no-layer outfit with a fresher top (always on a hot day). Both fixed, with tests.
    - **Lesson:** a worktree whose `node_modules` is one symlink to the main checkout's runs the app's tests against the main checkout's `@kitted/core`. For a core change, make `node_modules` a folder of symlinks to the main one's entries, with `@kitted/core` pointing at the worktree's `packages/kitted-core`.

## Oct 19 · Long-sleeve tees in the closet

359. **The founder: "closet does not detect full sleeve tees and does not have the option for them. it also does not have a graphic representation for them."** The closet call already read every top's `sleeve_length`, and the matcher used it to keep pieces apart, but nothing he sees did: a long-sleeve tee was "Grey tee", drawn with short sleeves, and "What it is" had no such kind. Plan and checks: `handoff/09-ui-review.md` §24.
    - **A long-sleeve tee stays a `tee` in the reading (Claude's call):** the scoring schema has no long-sleeve kind, and adding one is a reading change (the founder's OK and a steadiness test) for no score gain, since the rules treat both the same. So no score, rule or Python file changes. The sleeves are part of the piece's look: `ClosetArt.sleeves`, the closet call's read under the drift guard (his pick wins), else the scoring read's `sleeve_end` (`features/closet/sleeves.ts`).
    - **What follows it:** the name ("Grey long-sleeve tee", also graphic tees, polos and jerseys), the drawing (`flats.ts`: slimmer sleeves to the wrist and a stitched hem, so it's apart from a sweatshirt's ribbing), the morning card on a hot day (core `ideas.ts`: not counted as a cool short-sleeve top), and pieces already in his closet at the next launch (`refreshColors`).
    - **"What it is":** "Long-sleeve tee" right after "Tee" in the Kind list (Flannel shirt moves to the front of More kinds so the first group stays 8). It's a pseudo-kind: written as `tee` plus his confirmed `sleeve_length: long`, with his photos' sleeve reads taught to the matcher; "Tee" or "Graphic tee" on one makes the sleeves short again.
    - **Found:** the demo photo p01a's white tee was already read long by the real closet call, rightly (its cuff shows); it's now "White long-sleeve tee".

## Oct 19 · Try-ons vs what he wore: several checks in a row

360. **The founder: "It is still recommending a piece I just checked again. Could it be because I am running multiple checks a day?"** His flannel check offered "Swap to your green Champion sweatshirt → 7.5", the top of his check an hour earlier: a check's fixes never looked at other checks. Asked to think about the morning card first, then decide (Claude's call).
    - **What several checks did to the morning card and Kit:** every check counted as worn, so the tops he tried on and took off lost to every fresher top for about a week (the card leads with the top he's gone longest without, up to 7 days).
    - **The rule:** a check followed by another check (with pieces) within the hour, the same day, was a try-on; the last of the run is what he wore (`features/fitcheck/tryOn.ts`). Stable: whether a check was a try-on is settled within the hour after it, and the check being scored always counts as following, because its own pieces come after its card.
    - **The card and Kit** count only what he wore (`historyFor` uses `wornChecks`).
    - **A check's fixes:** a piece from an earlier try-on that day stays, tagged "Free · from your earlier check" (while he's deciding, "the sweatshirt you just had on goes better with these jeans" is the useful thing to hear); a piece from an outfit he wore earlier that day is left out (as the evening card never repeats the day's top); yesterday's pieces as before. Scores never change.
    - **Not changed:** a piece's "Worn n times" and Closet's Your go-tos still count try-ons (a follow-up).
    - **Checks first:** `app/src/__tests__/tryOn.test.ts` (TO1–TO5); the wiring checks failed before it, all pass, `npm run check`'s steps green (1,389 app tests). New outward words: "Free · from your earlier check". Merged Oct 19 (PR #28, founder: "You may merge PR 28.").

361. **More kinds in "What it is" (the founder, Oct 17: "the full sleeve shirt is not included as an option. Half sleeve button-ups are not included … just add more options like this"). Six closet-only kinds: Button-up shirt, Short-sleeve shirt, Henley, Turtleneck, Zip hoodie, Jean shorts (`app/src/features/closet/kinds.ts`; plan and checks K1–K11 in `handoff/09-ui-review.md` §25).**
    - **Why closet-only, not new reading kinds:** a new kind in the reading's list would change the photo read (it needs the founder's OK and a paid steadiness test) and every scoring list in `rubric.py` and `@kitted/core`. So each new kind keeps the reading kind it scores, matches and builds outfits as (button-up → oxford, short-sleeve shirt → camp shirt, henley → tee, turtleneck → sweater, zip hoodie → hoodie, jean shorts → shorts) and only draws and names as itself. Stored as `profile.kindVariant`, mirrored into `art.kind`.
    - **Merged onto #359 (Oct 19):** another session built the long-sleeve tee the same weekend, read from his photos (`sleeves.ts`). Its version is kept: this branch's own long-sleeve tee, and its rule that the reader's sleeve read never draws, were dropped. The two shirts confirm their sleeves through #359's path. This branch's drawing change generalizes #359's (`TopFlat.sleeves` short, long or sleeveless on any top), so camp shirts draw short.
    - **The tops' first group goes from 8 to 9** (3 rows of 3) so both button-ups sit up front with both tees; Flannel and Oxford sit in More kinds. #359's check K1 (first group stays 8) now expects 9.
    - **Open, not built:** the reader never names these kinds itself; shoe kinds unchanged (hiking boots, boat shoes would draw like work boots and loafers, and boat shoes would judge as dressy as loafers).

362. **A Closet hold no longer opens the piece page (founder, Oct 17: "if I release right after the haptic, before the options come up, it takes me inside instead").** Cause: the Closet tile (and a photo on the piece page) is a React Native `Pressable` with only `onPress`, inside the native SwiftUI context menu (`PieceMenu.ios.tsx`, `PhotoMenu.ios.tsx`). The menu cancels the tile's touch only once it has opened, and a `Pressable` without `onLongPress` fires `onPress` on release however long it was held; so a finger lifted between the menu's haptic and the menu showing counted as a tap. Fix: under both native menus the tile gets a no-op long press at 350 ms (`features/closet/menuHold.ts`, `delayLongPress` passed through `ClosetTile` and `PiecePhotoTile`), so a release after a hold opens nothing and a tap stays a tap. The web/Android fallbacks are unchanged (`PhotoMenu.tsx` already has its own long press; `PieceMenu.tsx` has no menu). Lookbook rows and You's fact rows use React Native's own long press, which already swallows the release. Checks `src/__tests__/menuHold.test.tsx` H1–H4 drive the real `Pressable` (RNTL `userEvent.press` / `longPress`); H2 fails without the fix. App tests 1,293 pass, typecheck and lint clean. Not seen on a device: the native menu's own timing needs a real finger (the Simulator was another session's). Numbered #337 on its branch; renumbered at the merge (#337 is held by the tuck PR). ## Oct 18 · UI tools: UI UX Pro Max in, 21st.dev and shadcn/ui out

363. **The founder: "check if these can be installed for making a better UI … ux ui promax, 21st dot dev, ui dot shadcn"**, then "Yes, install UI UX Pro Max as a project skill and try it on one screen." Decided:
    - **shadcn/ui and 21st.dev: no.** Both make web components (React DOM, Tailwind, Radix); none of it runs in a React Native app. The React Native port of shadcn (React Native Reusables) needs NativeWind and would replace `app/src/design-system/`, which is locked (handoff/03). 21st.dev's Magic MCP also needs an account and API key and outputs the same web stack.
    - **UI UX Pro Max: installed as a project skill** at `.claude/skills/ui-ux-pro-max/` (v2.15.0, commit 56ea9cf, MIT, 3.2 MB): `SKILL.md`, its two reference checklists, the CSV data and four Python search scripts (stdlib only; read before install: no network, no shell, no file deletes outside its own temp file). Paths made repo-relative; a Kitted note on top says: always `--stack react-native`, use it as a review checklist (touch targets, contrast, safe areas, VoiceOver, motion), never as a source of new colors, fonts or styles, never `--design-system --persist`; the HIG and §C14 win. No npm package, nothing in the app bundle.
    - **Tried on the You tab:** its checklist passes the screen on contrast (ink3 4.9–6.5:1 in both themes), touch sizes (gear 44 pt with its slop, "See all" 44 pt) and VoiceOver labels. One real find, from its "no nested tap targets" rule (and a web console error): the style card is a button with a second "Edit" button inside it. "Edit" is now a plain link-styled label; the whole card stays the one target, as VoiceOver already treated it. Looks the same; lint and typecheck pass; not seen on the Simulator (the Mac was at ~100 MB free, rule 9).
    - **Verdict:** useful as a checklist and for React Native specifics; it adds little over the app's own design system and the UI review (09). Worth a sweep of the same nested-button pattern on other cards. Numbered #340 on its branch; renumbered at the merge (#340 is the morning card's hello). Merged Oct 21 (PR #18, founder: "Review and merge").

## Oct 17 · Tuck it in, not swap it: shirts by type (on paper)

336. **The founder: "based on a shirt type… tell the user this is the type of shirt you should wear tucked in, so tuck it in… instead of telling them to change it entirely", with his own before/after on the phone (21:25 and 21:26, Going out: a brown check button-up worn open over a navy tee, light wide jeans, white sneakers; untucked, then tucked). Nothing in the app changed; the plan waits on his go.**
    - **What his cards said:** both 7.0. Before: "Button your cuffs", "Swap to your beige chukkas", "Swap to your heather blue graphic tee"; no tuck. After: "Swap to your heather blue graphic tee", "Swap to your black high-tops".
    - **Why:** the reading calls an open button-up a layer (`outerwear`: flannel_shirt before, overshirt after, tucked `full` after). The waist break [PRO-4] needs no layer (`O is None`), and the `tuck` fix only edits the `top` slot (the tee underneath). So the shirt's tuck can never earn or be offered.
    - **On paper (a what-if knob, reverted; scripts in the session's scratchpad):** (1) a button-up worn open (oxford, dress, flannel, camp collar, denim shirt, overshirt; one layer only) and fully tucked sets the waist like a tucked top. His after 7.0 → **7.5** (Proportions 7.0 → 8.5); his before stays 7.0. Gold: 0 photos move (no tucked open shirt in it), held-out r 0.499 → 0.499, his 20 unchanged (r 0.55, 12 within 0.5); his 43 saved checks: only the after moves. Of ~600 layer readings (gold + phone) only his after says `tucked: full`, so no false alarms but one true example. (2) a `tuck_layer` fix (tucks the tee and the shirt; named by the shirt): his before card leads with "Tuck in your flannel → 7.5", then "Button your cuffs".
    - **Proposed, defaults (his go and copy OK first):** (1) and (2) as above, ported with parity. (3) Shirt types, advice only, never points: when a tuck doesn't move the number (over slim pants, or a straight leg's +0.15), a shirt cut to be tucked gets a Finishing touch with no number, and a swap that replaces that same shirt with no more gain than the tuck stops showing. Default list: a dress shirt whenever it's untucked; an oxford or polo when it hangs to mid-fly or lower. Camp collar, flannel and overshirts are cut to be worn out: no tuck nag, only the scored fix when it moves the score. Draft lines: "A dress shirt is cut to be tucked in. Tuck it in for a cleaner line." / "Your {name} runs long untucked. Tuck it in for a cleaner line."
    - **Survey of today's fixes on button-ups (49 untucked button-up readings, gold + phone):** the free tuck already leads whenever it moves the rounded score (17 of them). It doesn't when the pants are slim (no waist break, e.g. his Oct polo + slim jeans: "Swap the jeans for dark straight jeans") or the leg is straight (+0.15 to +0.18, a Smaller step at most), or the shirt is worn open (the case above).

## Oct 17 · Tuck it in, not swap it: ported

337. **The founder: "Yes, port the tuck changes with your default shirt list and lines." Ported to Python and `@kitted/core` with exact parity: parts data 1.12.0 (still engine v14), fix catalog 1.7.0. Not merged.**
    - **Score ([PRO-4], `pr1_tucked_layer`, in v14's tune; v1–v13 off, so `CASUAL_FITS_V14 = false` rolls it back too):** a button-up worn open as the one layer (oxford, dress, flannel, camp collar, denim shirt, overshirt) and read fully tucked earns the waist break in place of a tucked top, and no layer shape credit (PR2b, PR5, PR6) on top. Faults and tips still treat it as the layer. Two layers, or a jacket, never count.
    - **Fixes:** `tuck_layer` ("Tuck in your {name}", tucks the tee and the shirt, named by the shirt). Never offered for an overshirt (a shirt-jacket, cut to be worn out; found by the app test on his Oct 8 slides check, where it offered "Tuck in your overshirt"); the score still credits an overshirt read tucked, since his tucked check shirt read as one.
    - **A shown tuck hides a swap** of a piece it tucks only when the swap raises a part the tuck raises (another way to fix the shape) for no more gain. First built as "any swap of that piece"; the core test on stock s13 caught it hiding "Swap the patterned top for a solid color", which fixes Color, so it was narrowed.
    - **Tuck tips by shirt type** (`tip_tuck_dress`, `tip_tuck_long`, first among the no-number tips, and also next to fixes when no tuck fix shows and no free Smaller step does): "A dress shirt is cut to be tucked in. Tuck it in for a cleaner line." (a dress shirt not tucked); "Your {name} runs long untucked. Tuck it in for a cleaner line." (an oxford, polo or knit polo hanging to mid-fly or below). Never at the gym or home.
    - **His saved checks (43, the Oct 17 pull included):** one score moves, his tucked after 7.0 → 7.5. Five cards change: the before leads with "Tuck in your flannel → 7.5"; the after loses "Swap the runners for loafers"; the polo over slim jeans and the oxford over slim chinos keep their fixes and gain the tuck tip; the striped oxford under his zip hoodie gets "Your oxford runs long untucked…" instead of "Tuck in the layer underneath…". Gold: 0 photos move.
    - **Proof:** parity 25,946 scoring cases and 28,803 fix cases exact; conformance 472/472 (3 new PRO-4 cases); core 784/784; app 1,288 of 1,289 in the full run, the miss the O12 timing test at 301 ms of 300 under the parallel suite (passes alone, twice; #335 saw the same).
    - **Merged Oct 21 (PR #15, founder: "Review and merge"), onto main after #359, #361, #4 and #7 (none touched the scoring or the fixtures; parts data stays 1.12.0 and the fix catalog 1.7.0, above main's 1.11.0 and 1.6.0).** The four generators re-run on the merge with the Oct 17 pull reproduce the PR's fixtures byte for byte; `npm run check` green (core 785, app 1,481). On the newest pull (Oct 21, 11 checks since the wipe, #339) no score moves; one card changes: the navy tee tucked under a brown overshirt with light jeans and chunky sneakers (7.5, Everyday) reads 7.25 → 7.7 underneath, so "Button your cuffs" now shows as a fix to 8.0 (it was the Smaller step). Lesson: the generators take the newest phone pull, and a pull after the app-data wipe drops the friends' checks a core test names (d1967f); re-run them with the pull the fixtures name (a `screenshots/phone/` of symlinks up to it).

365. **The founder: "Can we refresh the recommendation cards… when the color of a piece has been updated and that piece has been recommended for the day… make it update on its own?"** His Oct 20 evening card said "Grey fleece"; at 1:24 am he set the fleece to olive.     - **Re-run on his data:** the card's own picker chooses the same outfit with the grey and the olive fleece (predicted 7), so a refresh wouldn't have changed tonight's card. Olive over green is a separate color call (default: keep).
    - **Recommended (Claude's call, his OK pending):** the card keeps a fingerprint of each piece as planned; when Stylist shows, a changed, removed or merged piece re-plans a card he hasn't touched (as the weather refresh does), keeps a swapped or set card if it still passes the outfit rules, and never touches a worn, checked, staying-in or earlier card. A changed outfit shows "Updated for your {piece}." (new words).

Two calls made while building: the card keeps watching the first outfit's pieces after a re-plan, so undoing his edit brings that outfit back (R8 found it); and "still works" adds the picker's predicted score (not below 7, or below where it began) to its filters, which let sweatpants at Class through. Olive over green stays as it is. Merged Oct 21 (PR #31, founder: "…merge it and put it on my phone"), after main came in through a sparse checkout (main's `.claude/skills` never written to the worktree; the app's merge refused them for a stale origin pin).


## Oct 21 · Ready for the Apple approval day

366. **The ask (from the session planning the beta, Oct 21): make the day the $99 program is approved about 2 hours of console work, code only, nothing installed or spent.** Checks first in `handoff/09-ui-review.md` §27; drafts and the approval-day checklist in `market/app-store-connect.md`. Decided:
    - **Build profiles: `app/eas.json`** with `development` (dev client), `development-simulator`, `preview` (Release, internal) and `production` (store, EAS counts the build number, `appVersionSource: remote`). Each names its EAS environment, so `EXPO_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_API_KEY` is an EAS variable (plaintext: it's public by design) rather than a file; the pre-commit key check keeps it out of git anyway. `app.config.ts` refuses a `preview` or `production` build without it: such a build can't make the guest account, so it could never score or answer. No profile sets `KITTED_DEMO`, so the demo tools stay off in Release (#323).
    - **The dev launcher's local-network strings: nothing to add.** `expo-dev-launcher`'s plugin already adds a build phase that strips `_expo._tcp` and its `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` outside Debug (the gap audit, #320, read the generated plist, which is the Debug one). New `npm run check:app -- <Kitted.app> [--paid]` proves it on any built app, with the privacy manifest and the entitlements.
    - **The privacy manifest was short.** Pods with a manifest in a resource bundle are merged in at pod install, but the prebuilt `ExpoFileSystem` and `ExpoMediaLibrary` frameworks drop theirs, so the built app (the phone's Debug build, read Oct 21) had no disk-space reason at all and would have drawn ITMS-91053. Declared now: UserDefaults CA92.1, file timestamps C617.1 (the app's own clean-ups of files in its folders), system boot time 35F9.1, disk space E174.1. **And the collected data** (empty before, item 5 of #320): photos (face-blurred, to Google), other user content (questions, to OpenAI), user ID (the guest account), product interaction (the server's daily counts), coarse location (to the weather service; declared on the safe side), other data (height and build with questions). All app functionality, none tracking. Linked: only the user ID and the counts. The nutrition label draft is the same list, and a test keeps them equal.
    - **The paid-team switch: one block, `APPLE` in `app.config.ts` (`paid: false`, `teamId`).** On: the paid team, the entitlements for Declared Age Range, App Attest (development for the dev client, else production), WeatherKit and Sign in with Apple, `extra.applePaid` (read by `src/config/apple.ts`, like the demo flag), and a location string that names Apple Weather (new words, on the skim list). Off is exactly today: a Personal Team can't sign those entitlements, so they must stay off until approval.
    - **WeatherKit with NWS behind it (CLAUDE.md §4b).** Native `weatherKitHourly` and `weatherKitAttribution` in `kitted-location` (no package; WeatherKit is Apple's framework); `src/services/weather/weatherkit.ts` turns WeatherKit's hours into NWS-style periods so both go through the same `mapHourly` (a test feeds the same forecast both ways and gets the same card weather), tags `source: 'weatherkit'`, rounds the coordinate to 2 decimals, caches an hour, and on any failure answers with NWS. Only used with the switch on. The card then shows Apple's mark with "Weather" and "Data sources" (02 S1d item 7's own words, already in `en.ts`) and VoiceOver says "Weather: Apple Weather" (new). The WeatherKit REST API was not used: it needs a signed JWT, so a server key.
    - **StoreKit: `app/storekit/Kitted.storekit`**, one group, `kitted.pro.monthly` at $9.99 a month with a 1-week free trial, nothing else; a plugin (`plugins/withStoreKitConfig.js`) points the Debug scheme's Run at it. It's used only for a Run from Xcode, so it waits for a purchases library; the identifier's path follows the schemes Xcode writes and isn't confirmed until the file is opened in Xcode.
    - **Stopped where a package is needed (listed for the founder's OK):** the EAS CLI, `expo-apple-authentication`, a purchases library (RevenueCat), `@react-native-firebase/app-check` for App Attest. **Not built, no package needed:** Declared Age Range's Swift call (Texas requires it for the App Store release, not for TestFlight).
    - **Checked:** `npm run check` green, `guard:release` passed, and a local Release build (switch off) passed `check:app` and scored a never-read library photo on the Simulator through the live server (7.5; the server's log shows the guest account's `read`, `look` and `closet` calls, about 1.7¢). The Debug app went back on afterwards. Not seen: the switch on, WeatherKit answering, the StoreKit path in Xcode.
    - **Lesson:** the installed Simulator app is the only sure copy of the Debug build (DerivedData had been cleared); copy it out of `simctl get_app_container` before a Release install.

## Oct 21 · A little peek under a jacket is a layer, not a fault

367. **The founder, on his Oct 21 check (olive fleece zipped to the waist over a black tee, light-wash wide jeans cuffed, olive work boots; 7.0, "Tuck in your tee → 7.5"): "the black t-shirt not getting tucked in, like peeking underneath the jacket, is actually a good thing. The jacket forms some kind of proportion break while the t-shirt seeping through adds grounding color. It would have actually been too matchy-matchy if I tucked in my black shirt." Then: "Yes: a little peek under a jacket costs nothing and keeps the short-jacket credit; only a shirt that clearly hangs below costs 0.5."** Built as v14's `peek_clearly_only` (parts data 1.13.0; v1–v13, and so the CASUAL_FITS_V14 rollback, keep the old reading).
    - **Before:** [PRO-9] fired on look U's "a little" and "clearly" alike: −0.5 on Proportions and no short-jacket or short-top credit. His check lost 1.5 on Shape (8.0 → 6.5) for a thin band of tee.
    - **Why:** the editorial research already said a small peek is fine and only a shirt hanging well below is the fault (`rubric/research/notes/v5-editorial.md` L4, `v5-silhouette.md`); the creators' A5 is about an extra-long tee. And "a little" is look U's weak answer: right 71–82% when it fires vs "clearly" 100%, every false peek a "no" read as "a little" (look1, #199). His Oct 19 green crewneck sweatshirt had nothing under it and was charged anyway.
    - **After (his 55 saved checks, the two pulls of Oct 18 and Oct 21):** 2 move, both 7.0 → 7.5: the fleece check (Shape 8.0, "a short fleece over a loose leg: a clean, strong shape", no tuck fix) and the Oct 19 sweatshirt (Shape 8.5). No other score moves. Gold set: no second look there, so unchanged.
    - **Not done (advice only):** his "grounding color" point. Rewarding a dark band between a jacket and shoes of the same color needs colors the reading gets right, and this check's reading called the olive fleece charcoal and the olive work boots grey suede chukkas.
    - **Code:** `_second_look(sl, t)` in `parts_proto.py`, `secondLook()` in `@kitted/core` `parts.ts`, the tune key in both; conformance PRO-9-little and PRO-9-clearly (474/474); parity regenerated on the pull the fixtures name (`20261017-212744`, 25,946 cases); core 787 and app 1,509 tests pass. Stored checks keep their scores; sending the same photo again re-scores it from its stored reading (#342), free.

## Oct 17 · A second look when the AI is unsure (a side session; plan, checks and the free test)

368. **A narrow second look when the reading is shaky on a detail that would move the score (founder's idea, Oct 17: "ask again, and use this beyond shorts"). Plan, checks U1–U10, results: `rubric/research/notes/unsure-look.md`; script `extraction-test/unsure_look.py`; truth `unsure_labels.csv`. Nothing spent, nothing in the app or the engine changed. Numbered #333 on its branch, renumbered #368 at the merge (main had given #333 to the casual fits' round 2).**
    - **Which details (free, gold1's two runs, v13):** swapping one field between runs, shoe type moves the score on 12 of its 26 flips and the AI's flag catches 24 of 26 (all 12 that move it); leg shape moves it on 9 of 37 and the flag catches 0. Hem (6 of 53), top length (6 of 30) and top type (10 of 18, the quick tap's job) aren't worth a look; hem and top length already failed narrow looks (#199).
    - **Triggers, by code from the stored reading:** U-T1 shoe flagged and a same-family look-alike moves the score (22% of readings, all 12 score-moving flips; the flag alone fires on 36%); U-T2 a neighbouring leg moves the score (31%, all 9). Together 46% of readings, 59% of the founder's saved checks (mostly "straight" where slim costs 0.5).
    - **Claude's design:** fold `shoe` and `leg_shape` into the second look L the app already sends on every check (the same lower close-up, in parallel): ~0.05¢ a check, no added wait, stored with the check so the same photo keeps its score. The look settles silently; the person is asked only as today (a question on 46% of checks would be far past the taps' ~4%). Corrected while planning: a closet setting reaches a new score only through the "Your …?" tap, never silently (#316 (c)), so the closet isn't a silent override here either.
    - **Truth:** two blind subagent labellers from the close-ups only (they could open nothing but the images), before any answer existed; agreed values only. They agree on the shoe type on 39 of 57 photos and the leg on 42.
    - **The ceiling (free) is small:** a perfectly steady look takes "runs score the same" from 174 to 185 of 223 (+5 points), leaves the 5 big swings (each several fields at once) and doesn't move the held-out panel r (0.490) or the founder's 20 (13 within 0.5); the truth swapped in does no better against people. The wobble is spread: of 49 photos whose runs score apart, the shoe alone closes 7, the leg alone 7.
    - **The run (waiting on the founder):** 57 gold photos (31 for shoes: 19 flips + 12 controls; 30 for legs: 20 flips + 10 controls), arm N = the shipped L prompt + the two questions, 3 runs, ~27¢, hard stop 30¢ from real usage plus the worst case in flight, run by run. Bars as #198/#199: steady ≥ 90% on 3 runs and +10 points over the main read, score-right ≥ 85% and +10 points, can't see ≤ 30%, L's own covers_shoe still ≥ 90% steady and ≥ 85% right, steadier settled scores with no new 1.0+ swings, panel r no more than 0.02 lower. Checked end to end with a fake model, no network.
    - **Claude's call:** run it (cheap; the folded design is nearly free at run time); if it fails, stop chasing steadiness through the reading and lean on the closet's "Your …?" tap.
    - **Lesson:** settle only where a trigger fires and two photos of the same outfit can land further apart (one fires, the other doesn't); the test reports a symmetric "the look always wins" variant for that reason. And zsh reads `$b:h` as a path modifier: write `${b}:path` in git loops.
    - **The run (Oct 17, the founder via the parent session: "Yes, run the second-look test, up to 30 cents."): 26.6¢ spent (171 calls, 0 failed, 0.155¢ each), and it fails, so nothing ships.** Shoes: 55% the same on 3 runs (bar 90%; runs 0–1 65% vs the main read's 39%), score-right 82% vs 76% (bar 85% and +10). Legs: 73% the same on 3 runs (77% vs 33% on two), score-right 97% vs 80% (passes). L's own covers_shoe unharmed (91% steady, 93% right). Settled scores: 174 → 178 of 223 the same on both runs, but full-point swings 5 → 7 (a look that wobbled on a steady derby, ig-056; a trigger firing on one run only, rd-016); panel r 0.490 → 0.473; the founder's 20 unchanged.
    - **Decided (Claude):** no change to the app, the prompt or the engine. Steadiness through the reading is closed for now (#220, #278, this); the lever is his own answer through the closet's "Your …?" tap and the "Not sure" row. A leg-only look stays an idea, not a proposal. The ~3¢ run on his own close-ups was not made (needs a new OK).

## Oct 22 · Wear this, then a fit check in something else

369. **The founder (Oct 22): "I said that I was gonna wear the pick that the app made for me. But I didn't. Instead wore a different outfit yet the app did not recognize that, but instead just rated it the way it was."** His evening: Wear this on the card (olive fleece, charcoal tee, washed black jeans, cream chunky sneakers) at 9:14 pm, then a fit check at 9:16 in a black hoodie, the same jeans and black Puma running shoes (6.5, going out). The score was right (it scores the photo); two things weren't:
    - **The card's folded row said "You wore my pick"**: `CheckedRow` chose the line from the card's state (`wearing`) alone.
    - **The next cards and Kit counted the pick as worn:** Wear this writes the day's wear log with the card's outfit, the check never touches it, and `historyFor` counted both, so the fleece and the cream chunky sneakers looked worn that night (and he hadn't worn them).
    - **Fix (`app/src/features/morning/woreOwn.ts`):** a check shows the pick when it has at least 75% of the pick's pieces (by closet id, following merges; a 4-piece pick worn without one piece still is; his check had 1 of 4). The folded row says "You went with your own" when a check after Wear this isn't the pick. In `historyFor` a Wear this is skipped when a check he wore (not a try-on, #360), later the same day and part of the day, isn't the pick; the check stands for what he wore. Read at display and pick time: nothing stored changes, Undo on Wear this works as before, and his Oct 22 card reads right without a migration. A check whose pieces aren't in yet (the closet call comes after the card) counts as the pick, as before, so the line never flips back.
    - **Not changed:** the score, the wear counts (they come from fit checks only), Kit's own outfit cards' Wear this (`OutfitIdeaCard`, same wear log, covered by the history fix).
    - **Checks:** `app/src/__tests__/woreOwn.test.ts` (W1–W3, his evening replayed); app 1,518 tests, tsc and lint pass. JS only; on his phone through Metro once merged. Not seen on the Simulator (the Mac's memory was near full).

370. **The You tab's batch of 8 (founder, Oct 22: "Yes, build all 8 of tonight's batch, checks first, then show me screenshots."). Checks YT1–YT8 in `app/src/__tests__/youTonight.test.ts`, written first; screenshots `screenshots/you-tonight/` (git-ignored, web preview).**
    - **(1) Today's card can be acted on:** Check my fit while a check is left (free or Pro; goes to Stylist and opens the camera, as Closet's empty state does), else Re-check while today's re-check is open (Stylist opens `/camera?mode=recheck&parent=<id>`, the card's own route), else no button. `features/you/today.ts`; the quota snapshot gains `recheckId` (`openRecheckId`, the newest open one); the unused `recheck` Stylist action now carries `checkId` and is handled.
    - **(2) Privacy & data is a row on You** (lock, chevron), still in Settings: one tap instead of three (09 §2).
    - **(3) No profile:** You shows the blank style card and the editor opens on `blankProfile()` (no looks, basics, birth year or consent); the first change creates the row, an untouched one writes nothing.
    - **(4) A 4th look in the editor** buzzes (warning), shakes the pill (a dim with Reduce Motion) and reads "That's 3. Tap one to take it off first." out, like onboarding.
    - **(5) The editor's header says Done, always on:** leaving already saved (#306), so "Save" looked required. Done saves and goes back.
    - **(6) VoiceOver:** the birth-year row reads "Birth year, 2005"; "Score my fits" passes its on/off hints (written, never passed).
    - **(7) One red, one corner:** a `danger` color token (#C9302C / #FF6961, 4.5:1+ on a card, a test checks), used by Privacy and the piece page instead of two local copies; every card on You and its pages uses `radius.cardSmall` (20, the token's own "plan row, today row, list frame"), and the fact rows' dividers are inset like Settings'.
    - **(8) "resets at 4 am" everywhere** (was "resets 4 am" / "Resets 4 am" / "Resets at 4 am"): `you.today.resets`, `limit.resets` and `you.plan.usage` in the deck (02 §5).
    - **No new words:** the buttons reuse "Check my fit", "Re-check", "Privacy & data" and "Done".
    - **Checked:** the 19 new checks and 1,528 app tests, the typecheck and the lint (the core workspace wasn't rerun: core is unchanged). Seen in the web preview from this branch, light and dark: Check my fit opens the camera, a 4th look is refused with the shake, Done saves and goes back, the Privacy row opens Privacy. **Seen on the Simulator Oct 23** (once memory pressure was normal; screenshots `screenshots/you-tonight/sim/`): You in light and dark with its icons (camera, lock, chevrons), the Privacy row opening Privacy with the shared red, the editor in dark with Done and a 4th look refused ("That's 3…"), Done saving the 3rd look and going back, and You at the largest text size (the button stacks its icon, the Privacy row wraps). Check my fit switched to Stylist; whether the camera opened before the demo's instant stand-in capture couldn't be caught on screen (it opened in the web preview, and it is the Closet button's own path). Not heard with VoiceOver; Re-check's button is covered by tests only (no demo screen has an open re-check).
    - **Lesson (Simulator):** a worktree's Metro with a borrowed `node_modules` can miss new files under watchman ("could not be found within the project"); `config.resolver.useWatchman = false` in the temporary `metro.config.js` fixes it. Once the dev build has opened another port, relaunches can come back on the last tab and ignore `-demoScreen`.
    - **Lesson:** two worktrees borrowing the main checkout's `node_modules` share Metro's cache for `expo-router/entry.js`, so a second Metro can bundle the other worktree's app: start it with `--clear`.

## Oct 23 · One fit check at a time, start to finish

371. **The founder (Oct 23): "the fit testing can get very overwhelmed easily … If a rating is taking place, maybe we should pause other ratings."** His evening, from the phone's data (`screenshots/phone/20261023-221546/`): at 10:08:21 pm a new check (the green Champion sweatshirt and the cream chunky sneakers, the fixes his 9:51 card had offered); at 10:08:29, while it was still reading, he tapped Re-check on the 9:51 card and the camera opened; its photo came at 10:08:33, two seconds after the first score landed. Both scored 7.5 and the data is fine, but the two ran on top of each other: the first check's reveal, its closet call (10:08:31–36) and its photo measuring ran while the re-check's 12 MP photo was being prepped (face hiding, close-ups) and read, then the re-check's own closet call. The one-check slot (#219, #305) covered only the prep, the read and the score; the closet call and the measuring after it ran outside it.
    - **Fix (`app/src/features/fitcheck/service.ts`):** (1) a check waits, before its photo is prepped, for the last check's closet call and measuring (`closet.settle()`, capped at `TAIL_MS` 10 s; the closet's own deadline is 12 s), in a new check, a re-check, a snap, Try again and Score it; (2) a photo taken while another check holds the slot **waits its turn** instead of being refused and deleted: `start()` keeps one waiting photo and runs it when the slot frees, after a 2 s breath (`REVEAL_GAP_MS`) so the last score's reveal plays first; (3) a further photo meanwhile is still refused ("Still reading your last photo. Send this one when it's done."); (4) `cancel()` (a demo reset, Delete all my data) drops the waiting photo (`'dropped'`: nothing prepped, sent or counted; its file is deleted, nothing said). Try again, Score it and "Which one is you?" still refuse while busy (buttons he can tap again; the question stays open).
    - **What he sees:** a photo taken while a check is going shows "Got it. I'll read this one right after your last check." (`cam.waits`, `fitCheck.busy()`), and its card appears a few seconds later, after the first one's closet line has landed. The waiting time is within the slot's 40 s cap (prep + 14 s read + 1.5 s look + 10 s tail + 2 s breath).
    - **Not changed:** scores, the counts (a waiting photo counts when it's read, like any check), the re-check rules (a waiting re-check is still its own card's re-check, checked when it starts).
    - **Checks first:** `app/src/__tests__/oneAtATime.test.ts` (O1–O9: never two reads at once, the closet tail holds the next prep, the cap, his Oct 23 re-check replayed, a third photo refused, cancel drops the waiting one, the breath, `busy()`, Try again still refused) and `busyPhoto.test.ts` O10–O10b (the line, the dropped photo's file); the two older "second photo is refused" tests now expect it to wait. `npm run check` green (1,529 pass). JS only; on his phone through Metro once merged. Not seen on the Simulator (the Mac had ~300 MB free).

## Oct 23 · A closet swap promises what that piece really does
372. **The founder (Oct 23): "I followed its recommendations. Yet I still got a 7.5."** His evening (going out, picks streetwear · minimal · workwear, born 2004): a black tee ending at the belt over light relaxed cuffed jeans with grey suede shoes, 7.5 (raw 7.54). The card offered "Swap to your green Champion sweatshirt → 8.0" and "Swap to your cream chunky sneakers → 8.0". He put on the sweatshirt, kept the same shoes, re-checked: 7.5 (raw 7.28), and the card said he'd done both. The re-check's score was right; the promise and the "done" weren't.
    - **The bad promise:** a closet swap kept `hem`, `tucked`, `length` and `sleeve_end` from the piece it replaced ("how he wears it comes from the photo"). Length isn't how he wears it: it's the piece's cut. So the predicted sweatshirt "ended at the belt" like the tee and kept the waist credit (PR1, +1.5 Proportions), raw 7.97 → 8.0. His sweatshirt's own closet reading is mid fly; scored that way the swap is 7.27, 7.5, the re-check's number to within 0.01.
    - **Fix (fix catalog 1.8.0; `parts_fixes.py`, `partsFixes.ts`, `applyPartsFix`):** `keepOnSwap` is now hem and tucked; the new `ownOnSwap` (length, sleeve_end) comes from the swapped-in piece's own reading, and from the replaced piece only when the piece has none (missing or na). Exact parity regenerated on the pinned pull (20261017-212744): 28,803 cases; 1,264 cases' fix rows change, both ways (a swap to a piece that really ends at the belt can now show, one that doesn't no longer does). Scores never change: this is the fix engine only.
    - **The false "done":** the re-check counts a fix done when its slot changed. His same suede shoes were read as grey work boots, then tan chukkas, so "Swap to your cream chunky sneakers" counted. Now (`partsRecheckOutcome`'s new `closet` argument, `swapLanded`) a closet swap counts only when the slot now holds that piece, by kind or by color family; without the closet it's as before. A real swap the reader names differently (cream chunky sneakers read as white leather sneakers) still counts by color, and one it misses entirely goes to "What changed?" as any missed fix does.
    - **Not changed:** stored cards keep their fixes (his Oct 23 card still shows the sweatshirt row); the re-check's merge, which re-reads a slot a fix names (so the shoes were re-read, #342), stays.
    - **Checks:** `app/packages/kitted-core/test/oct23Recheck.test.ts` (his readings and the two closet pieces); core 794 and app 1,518 pass, tsc clean. JS only, on his phone through Metro once merged.

Tests first (BY, AY, UN failing before the code), `npm run check` green, seen on the iOS Simulator in light, dark and the largest text size. Two largest-size fixes from the Simulator: the sheet's explanation moved out of its sticky header into a scrolling note (new line `For sizing and fit advice. None of it changes a score.`), and the height stepper's − / + capped at 1.3× (You → Style profile had the same overflow).

## Oct 24 · Do height and build help Kit? Tested, no

374. **The founder (Oct 24): "I think we are collecting unnecessary information when asking about height and build … check if it is [useful] or if it isn't. If it is, we tell the user during onboarding what it's used for. If not, we remove it." Then, on Claude's offer: "Run the small test first … blind, under 1 cent."**
    - **The code:** only Kit's question context reads height and build (one line each, "never say it back"). Scores, outfit ideas, Next best buy and outfit counts don't. The drawn figure (#247) isn't built. Kit's guide (`rubric/stylist-guide.md`) has no advice that changes with height or build.
    - **The test (0.94¢, `extraction-test/results/ask-basics1/report.md`):** 7 fit, size and length questions × 3 profiles (none, 6'4" broad, 5'6" slim), on Kit's own model through the app's pipeline, judged blind. The advice was the same on 5 of 7 questions; the judge could place only two answers, both from the 6'4" broad profile; the shorter guy never got different advice; one tailored answer said his body back ("gives a taller frame").
    - **Found:** the sentence gate's `BUILD_BACK` misses phrasings like "a taller frame"; and Kit once named the 3rd buy instead of the top one from identical evidence.
    - **Recommended (Claude), waiting on the founder:** remove height and build (the About you card, the sheet, You → Style profile, Kit's pack, consent's and Privacy's wording, the App Store privacy label); keep "Fit you like" (taste, not body), so the card becomes one quick fit question. Sending less needs no new consent.

## Oct 24 · The anchoring tip on a light-on-light card
376. **(Numbered #373 on its branch, renumbered at the merge as main gave #373–#375 to the onboarding work.) The founder (Oct 23): "should we add some kind of rule of color balancing? … a cream shirt … maybe wearing black shoes to anchor it down", then, on Claude's recommendation: "Add the anchoring tip: no points, on the card when light on light fires and in the stylist guide."**
    - **Why a tip, not points:** the sources split on shoes (Effortless Gent: light top, dark bottom, light shoes; Tim Dessaint: a shoe close to the pants' color streamlines; the Oct 7 color research, `rubric/research/notes/color.md` V2: "advice only"). In the casual formula the pants do the anchoring, so a dark-shoe rule would charge a white tee, dark jeans and white sneakers, the most common good fit. [COL-12] stays as it is (shoes don't lift the charge); one Color point is worth 0.2 of the score anyway.
    - **Built (fix catalog 1.9.0; `fixes.json` → `parts`, `parts_fixes.py`, `partsFixes.ts`):** two `always` tips with the new `light_on_light` condition, read from the scored card's own charge (`CL2_light_on_light`), so the tip follows the rule exactly. `tip_anchor_shoes` when the shoes are light too (white, cream, khaki, light grey): "Light on light washes out: darker pants, or dark shoes like brown leather, would ground it."; else `tip_anchor`: "Light on light washes out: darker pants, like navy chinos or dark jeans, would ground it." New flag `anchor`: like the tuck tips (#336) it also shows next to fixes, unless a fix shown already clears the charge (the darker-pant swap, a dark jacket added), so the card never says the same thing twice.
    - **Found while building:** on most light-on-light outfits the card already leads with "Swap the chinos for navy chinos" with a number (the swap lifts the rounded score), so a tip shown only without fixes would almost never appear; hence the beside-fixes rule.
    - **What changes:** parity regenerated on the pinned pull (20261017-212744): 29,068 cases, exact; against the previous fixtures 28,657 unchanged, 146 gain the tip (it takes the place of the Smaller step or the generic Next level line, as every tip does, #232), 0 change otherwise. None of the founder's saved phone checks is light on light, so none of his cards change. Scores never change. 5 new conformance outfits (`COL-12-tip*`, 479/479 pass). `rubric/stylist-guide.md` §5 has the line for Kit.
    - **Checks:** `npm run check` green (core 799, app 1,548). JS only, through Metro once merged.

## Oct 23 · Swap one, the S28 sheet

377. **(Numbered #370 on its branch, renumbered at the merge as main gave #370 to the You tab batch and #371–#376 to later work.) The founder (Oct 23): why does the card's Swap use Apple's default menu, would a custom one be better? Yes: S28 (02 §3.3) was always the design, and the action sheet (#353) was the stopgap.** It listed the pieces as text ("The navy chinos"), code picked the replacement, he never saw it, and a mis-tap lost that piece from the card for the day (#353 keeps a swapped-away piece out). Checks, results and screenshots: `handoff/09-ui-review.md` §28; mockup `mockups/swap-one.html`.

Right for the weather first, then the score. No score or count is shown.

Tests: core `swapOptions.test.ts` (7), app `swapSheet.test.ts` (8); R12 and U3 amended.

    - **New words, his skim:** "Instead of your {piece}", "Swapped in your {piece}.", VoiceOver "{piece}. Shows what else works here." and "{piece}. Swaps it in.".
    - **Lesson:** a worktree Metro that borrows the main checkout's `node_modules` shares Metro's cache with other worktrees and can bundle another branch's files; give it its own cache (`cacheStores` with a `FileStore` in the scratchpad, in a temporary uncommitted `metro.config.js`).

## Oct 24 · The card's outfit: slides stay home, and its look is the outfit's look
379. **(Numbered #378 on its branch, renumbered at the merge as main gave #378 to the Stylist tab bar.) The founder (Oct 24): "today on my phone, I just got recommended black slides with a flannel and jeans. I don't think that's right … it's branded as streetwear."** His day card (pulled `screenshots/phone/20261024-151727/`; fixture `app/packages/kitted-core/test/data/phone-oct24-card.json`; born 2004, picks streetwear · minimal · workwear, Everyday, 60° and clear): the charcoal fleece over the brown plaid flannel, mid-wash jeans and black Adidas slides, predicted 7.0, under a Streetwear menu.
    - **Three causes, found by replaying his closet and history through `pickOutfit`:** (1) the picker ranks "a top he hasn't seen lately" above the score, and the flannel had never been on a card; (2) shoes seen in the last 2 days lose 0.75, and before any scoring only 4 pairs are kept, sorted by that, so his cream chunky sneakers (7.5 with the fleece) and chukkas lost to the slides (7.0): at Everyday, under an otherwise clean outfit, slides cost no more than chukkas ([SK1] softened in v8); (3) the card's style menu only reached the picker through the score, and since #209/#211 picks never move a score, so the menu changed nothing: the outfit read as no look at all, labelled with his first pick. 276 of his outfits read as streetwear (the best 8.5).
    - **Changed (the picker only; scores never change):** slides (`slides_sandals`) out of Home and Gym count like a wrong-weather piece, a last resort used only when no other shoe works; the card's look (`PickInput.look`, the card's style) ranks the outfits that read as it right after weather and occasion, ahead of rotation, which still varies the outfit among them; when the rotation-cut shortlists can't make the look, the picker searches the whole closet once more; a look the closet can't make leaves the pick as before. Kit's own outfit ideas pass no look (unchanged), but get the slides rule. The Swap sheet (`swapOptions`, #377, merged the same day) lists slides after every other pair out of the house, still offered.
    - **Not changed:** the scoring rule for slides (a photo of slides is scored as before; the gold panel set it, #227), and the rotation weights.
    - **On his data:** today's card becomes the charcoal fleece over the flannel, mid-wash jeans and the cream chunky sneakers, 7.5, reading as streetwear. Today's stored card stays as it is (an untouched card re-plans only when the weather or a piece changes, #365); tomorrow's uses the rule, and so does the Swap sheet.
    - **Checks:** P1–P8 and P4b (the Swap sheet) in `app/packages/kitted-core/test/cardPick.test.ts`, written first (P1, P2, P4b, P5, P6 failed before). JS only, on his phone through Metro once merged.

## Oct 28 · The outfit card draws no "add a piece" tile
380. **The founder (Oct 28): the dashed "+" tile on the morning/evening card does nothing when tapped, and he wants it gone: no add-a-piece option on the card.** The tiles (`You'd need: a layer` / `a warm coat` for a missing slot the plan wanted; `A top` · `Bottoms` · `Shoes` on a thin or empty closet) were plain labelled boxes with no tap handler, so they looked like buttons and weren't.
    - **Built (app only):** `HeroStrip` moved to `app/src/features/morning/HeroStrip.tsx` and draws only the pieces he owns, which now fill the strip (2 pieces = 2 halves); with no pieces there's no strip, and the card keeps its title, why line and Check my fit. `MorningCard` no longer computes missing slots; `missingLabel` and the five `idea.need.*` strings are deleted. Core still returns `missing` on the outfit (`ideas.ts`, owned by the parallel cold-weather session, untouched); the card just doesn't read it.
    - **The cue that stays:** the why line already names a missing layer or coat ("It's cold out, and I haven't seen a layer of yours yet." / "Below 35°, a warmer coat would help."). No new UI was added; a thin closet's card says "I only know {n} of your pieces…".
    - **Checks:** A1–A4 in `app/src/__tests__/cardNoAddTile.test.tsx`, written first (they failed before: no strip file, the card passed `needs`). `npm run check` green (1,595 tests). Seen on the Simulator: demo 25 (4 pieces) and 26 (2 pieces, no Shoes tile) in light, dark and the largest size (2 pieces as one row of 2), and 27 (empty closet, no strip). `handoff/02-screens.md` S1d updated. JS only, through Metro once merged.

## Oct 28 · Cold days get a warm outfit: an open shirt isn't warmth
381. **The founder (Oct 28): it was 38° and the card recommended a button-up shirt over a t-shirt, which isn't warm enough.** The card (pulled from his phone Oct 28; fixture `app/packages/kitted-core/test/data/phone-oct28-card.json`; born 2004, picks streetwear · minimal · workwear): Oct 27's evening card, read at 1:46 am, Going out under Streetwear, 38° now and feels-like 34°: the cream striped overshirt over a red tee, mid-wash jeans and the cream chunky sneakers, "Keep the cream striped overshirt on; it gets cold late." and "Below 35°, a warmer coat would help." He owns two fleeces, a green hoodie, two more hoodies, a sweatshirt and a navy crewneck.
    - **Cause:** the reader calls a button-up worn open "outerwear" (an `overshirt`), and the card's warmth check (`isWarmLayer`) counted any outerwear as warm. So on a cold day (feels-like under 50°, where the bands require a warm layer) an overshirt over a tee passed, and won when it scored or rotated best. Not the bands, the forecast window or a stale card: his Oct 23 evening card (feels-like 38°) had done the same with the brown plaid overshirt over a navy long-sleeve tee. Replayed on his Oct 24 closet at 38°, about 3% of cards (mostly Going out and Date, where the shirt scores well) had no real warm piece.
    - **Changed (the picker and the cold tip; scores never change, the bands stay 80 / 65 / 50 / 35):** `isWarmLayer` now excludes shirt-weight pieces (overshirt, flannel, oxford, dress and camp-collar shirts, rugby, tees, polos, tanks, jerseys) even when read as outerwear; `isLightLayer` keeps the old meaning (any outerwear or a knit top) for the mild-day layer credit, the "no layer on a warm day" rule and the hot-day ban. On a cold day the picker ranks outfits by how short of warm they fall (a coat below 35°, then a fleece, knit or jacket, then a shirt layer, then nothing) ahead of everything else, instead of a hard filter; it searches the whole closet again when the shortlists left out every warm piece; the layer shortlist puts warm layers first on cold days. Found on the way: when the hard filter removed every outfit (his only warm piece swapped away, say), the picker re-planned with no weather at all and the card lost its cold words; now it keeps them. The reason line names the warm piece ("Keep the navy crewneck on", not the overshirt over it). With nothing warm in the closet the card still plans an outfit, wears the shirt layer if he has one, and says so: "It's cold out, and your {layer} won't be enough on its own. A sweater or a jacket would help." (or "It's cold out, and I haven't seen anything warm of yours yet, like a sweater or a jacket."). A fit check in an open shirt at 38° now gets the cold tip, reworded "Cold out: bring something warm, like a sweater or a jacket." Swap one keeps its rule with the stricter warmth.
    - **On his data:** the reported card becomes the green hoodie over the red tee, washed black jeans and the grey work boots, "Keep the green hoodie on; it gets cold late." plus the coat line (34°, no heavy coat). A 38° morning gives the green hoodie over the navy crewneck or the red tee, mid-wash jeans and boots or the cream chunky sneakers. Across 2,000 replayed cards on his Oct 24 closet (38° day and evening, 31°, 45°, 58°; 4 occasions × 5 looks × 10 days × with and without history), only the 4 overshirt-only cards change.
    - **Not changed:** a denim jacket, blazer or rain shell still counts as warmth at 35–50° (a real jacket); fleeces aren't "heavy" (below 35° the coat line still shows for him).
    - **Checks:** W0–W8 in `app/packages/kitted-core/test/coldCard.test.ts` (W1–W4, W6 failed before; W0 is his card), the copy in `app/src/__tests__/morningCopy.test.ts`; `npm run check` green (824 core, 1,592 app tests). Seen on the Simulator: demo screen 25 (sample weather, feels-like 42°) keeps the navy overshirt over the navy crewneck and now says "Navy crewneck for the cold." (was "Navy overshirt for the cold start; it comes off by afternoon."; screenshot `screenshots/cold-card/demo25-after.png`, git-ignored). The no-warm-piece line is checked by tests only. JS only, on his phone through Metro once merged.

## Oct 28 · Swap one follows the card's rotation
382. **The Swap sheet (#377) ordered its choices by weather and predicted score only, ignoring what he wore or was shown lately, so it could put yesterday's top first while the card itself rotates (#358, #360, #369).** Now `swapOptions` (core `ideas.ts`) orders the way `pickOutfit` ranks: wrong for the weather or the occasion first (slides out of the house, #379); then an option that rebuilds an exact top + bottom + shoes from the last 7 days goes after; then a top he's gone longest without (days since, capped at `ROTATE.topFresh` = 7, as `topFreshness`); then the score, less `W.recent` (0.75) for a bottom, shoe or layer seen in the last 2 days; then the stable tie-break. The history is the card's own (`pickInput` already passed `historyFor`): earlier cards, Wear this unless a later check overrode it, worn checks only (a try-on within the hour doesn't count, #360).
    - **Order only:** the same pieces are offered, with the same outfits and reasons; nothing is hidden, and scores never change. Undo still restores the card exactly (it puts the stored rows back).
    - **Not taken from the picker:** the card's look (`look`) and the item bonuses (wet boots, hot-day short sleeves); Swap keeps the outfit's other pieces, so it ranks only the one slot. The cold-weather rules (#381, another session) weren't touched.
    - **Checks, written first:** core `test/swapOptions.test.ts` "follows the card's rotation" (a top worn yesterday goes after its twin unworn for a week, and after every top not worn this week; past 7 days no effect; a shoe or bottom from the last 2 days loses 0.75, 3 days none; an option rebuilding this week's outfit goes after; history only reorders, over 4 occasions × every piece), app `swapSheet.test.ts` S9 (a piece worn yesterday moves down, nothing hidden; a try-on keeps the order; Undo after a reordered swap). The three ordering checks and S9's first failed on the old code. `npm run check` green. JS only, on his phone through Metro once merged.

## Oct 28 · A re-check that adds a piece, and what "What changed?" may do

383. **The founder (Oct 28, on his phone): check 1, green cargos and a navy shirt, 7.0; he put on a green zip hoodie and re-checked: 7.0, "nothing changed"; then the option to say he made the change took it to 7.5. Two questions: why didn't the re-check notice, and should a tap raise the score on his word?** Evidence: the Metro log (`app/.expo/dev/logs/start.log`) and his phone's database (pulled `screenshots/phone/20261028-020942/`; fixture `app/src/__tests__/fixtures/oct28-hoodie-recheck.json`; born 2004, age band 22–25, picks streetwear · minimal · workwear, fit relaxed, Everyday, scored for streetwear).
    - **The read and the merge were right.** The re-check photo was read with a clear green hoodie as the outer layer (the tee "navy", peeking a little below it), and the anchored merge kept it ("new item: hoodie"). The score held because under parts v14 the hoodie changes no part: his first check was 6.8 raw (Shoes 5, the black performance runners), and the hoodie version is 6.8 too. So 7.0 was the honest number.
    - **Bug 1, the card:** "not seen" was "no fix of the first card done and the score didn't move", blind to a piece the photo added, so it said "I still see the same outfit." **Fixed:** the re-check stores the pieces it added (`recheck.seen`, a kind the first reading didn't have in that slot; a same-kind piece in another shade isn't named, since under a new layer that's mostly the same shirt read another shade), it's never "not seen" with one, and the card reads "Put together. I see your green hoodie now. It doesn't change the score."
    - **Bug 2, the 7.5:** "I did make the change" (and the "What changed?" sheet) applied the first card's fixes to the *first* reading, with no check against the new photo. His only fix was "Swap to your grey work boots", so he got 7.5 for boots the photo clearly showed weren't on, and the hoodie dropped out of the reading. That breaks "the AI sees, the code judges".
    - **Decided (Claude; code-only, it follows the locked rules):** "What changed?" stays, only as a correction of what the re-check read. It offers a first-card fix only when the new photo didn't clearly read that fix's pieces (out of the photo, missing from the read, or read only "likely"/"unsure"; checked against the re-check photo's own read, linked as `recheck.rawReadingId`), and applies it to the re-check's reading, so what the photo did show (the hoodie) stays. With nothing to offer, the "not seen" card's second button is "Not quite right?" (correct a misread piece, like any check) and "Didn't catch the change? Tell me" is hidden. After a confirm the card says "Scored from what you told me." (was "You confirmed it."). The service refuses a fix outside that set too.
    - **On his data:** the re-check now says it sees the green hoodie, stays 7.0, and offers "Not quite right?" instead of "I did make the change" (the runners were read clearly). His stored Oct 28 cards don't change.
    - **Left as a scoring question (to-do, P3):** a plain zip hoodie over a tee earns nothing today (no generic layer credit since v8, "every point earned"). Default: keep it.
    - **Checks:** A1–A7 in `app/src/__tests__/recheckAddedLayer.test.ts`, written first (all 7 failed before), replaying his two stored readings through the service. Code `app/src/features/fitcheck/recheckSeen.ts`, `finalizeRecheck` / `confirmDraft` / `confirmableFixes` in `fitcheck/service.ts`, `RecheckCard.tsx`, `TellMeSheet.tsx`. Nothing in `@kitted/core` changed (`recheckMerge` / `recheckOutcome` stay in parity with Python). JS only, on his phone through Metro once merged.

## Oct 28 · Closet → Pieces in head-to-toe sections
384. **The founder (Oct 28): the pieces sorted into categories shown under titles on the same scroll, not behind a button or a filter, "and the UI has to be nice".** Closet → Pieces was one grid, newest first, so a shoe sat between two shirts.
    - **Built:** `closetSections` next to `closetGridItems` in `app/src/features/closet/merge.ts` groups the grid by the piece's slot, head to toe: **Jackets and layers · Tops · Bottoms · Shoes** (the app's own words: the Added sheet says "Layer", the Outfits empty state "Bottoms" and "Shoes"; "Jackets and layers" because a beginner may not read "Layers" as jackets). Empty sections are left out; inside one, newest first as before; a piece with no known slot joins Tops so none is dropped. Full grids, not sideways shelves (shelves hide pieces); titles scroll with the page (not sticky), to start.
    - **Thin closet:** under 4 pieces, or all in one section, there are no titles: one plain grid, still head to toe (a tee, chinos and sneakers read as one outfit, and three "1"s would be heavier than the pieces).
    - **The title (two treatments tried on the Simulator, `screenshots/closet-sections/after-15-light-a.png` vs `-b.png`):** picked **A, the card-header tag** (`TagLine`: spaced capitals in ink3, the count on the right), the same as Outfits' "Try next · 20 not worn yet" in the same tab, so the tiles stay the loudest thing. **B** (a semibold headline with the count beside it in ink2, like Apple's Music/Photos section titles) reads stronger but makes the Pieces half louder than the Outfits half; one switch, `PIECE_HEADER` in `app/src/app/(tabs)/closet/index.tsx`. 24 pt between sections, 8 under a title. Ink, never copper.
    - **Accessibility:** each title is one VoiceOver header, "Tops, 6 pieces" (checked on the web preview: four headings). At the accessibility sizes the grid keeps 2 per row and the title stacks over its count in sentence case, as every `TagLine` does.
    - **Not changed:** the ScrollView stays first (the tab bar's shrink), the To confirm card stays on top, taps and long-press menus (`PieceMenu`), Outfits, the piece page, scores.
    - **Checks:** P1–P8 in `app/src/__tests__/closetSections.test.ts`, written first. `npm run check` green (1,597 tests). Seen on the Simulator in light, dark and the largest text size, on the full demo closet (screen 15), with To confirm (38), a thin closet (new demo screen 250, 3 pieces, no titles) and a small one (251, 4 pieces, titled); a tap still opens the piece page. JS only, on his phone through Metro once merged.

## Oct 28 · A hoodie layered over a tee earns the layer credit (parts v15)

385. **The founder (Oct 28): a zip hoodie layered over a tee should earn something.** His Oct 28 re-check (a green zip hoodie open over a navy tee, olive straight cargos, black runners; born 2004, age band 22–25, picks streetwear · minimal · workwear, Everyday) held at 7.0 because the hoodie moved no part (#383). Report and before/after: `rubric/research/parts/v15.md`.
    - **Why it earned nothing:** a layer earns points only through shape since v8 ("every point earned"). The two layer shape credits missed it twice: the short jacket ([PRO-6], +1.5) is a bomber, denim, leather or track jacket by type, and a hoodie isn't on the list; the read-length credit ([PRO-14], +1.0) needs a loose leg (his cargos read straight) and a layer read as ending at the belt (the reader gave the hoodie no length). Color didn't help either: the green hoodie took over the "quiet accent" the blue tee had (Oct 24's re-check, where the tee read blue, even lost Color's half point: 7.0 → 6.5). His runners hold Shoes at 5, so only a +1.5 on Proportions (+0.45 on the score) can move his card; a Style or Color credit of a normal size can't.
    - **Decided (the narrowest rule, on his principle):** [PRO-17], +1.5 on Proportions, the short jacket's size, for the track jacket's reason: a hoodie or sweatshirt worn as the one layer ends on a ribbed band at the hip by its cut. Over a tee or graphic tee only, full legs only, one shape credit (it takes [PRO-14]'s place), never with sweats or joggers, slim or skinny legs, a second layer, a top clearly showing below it, a length read past the fly, slides, or a stained layer. The interview hold still caps it at 5.0. Sweatshirt counts too because hoodie and sweatshirt flip between reads. Weather is out of scope (it never changes a score). Shorts and a hoodie over a collared shirt are left out (KD-22, a founder call).
    - **One bar failed on the first paper run and the rule changed:** with slides the hoodie layer lifted a hoodie, tee, jeans and slides to 7.5. Slides now block it (the shorts layer credit already needs real shoes). Running shoes don't (they pay their own −2.0 on Shoes; blocking them would undo his case).
    - **Paper, before the port (`extraction-test/hoodie_layer_paper.py`, stored readings, no spend):** his Oct 28 pair 7.0 → 7.5 (the tee alone stays 7.0); 24 test outfits pass (19 guards unchanged); gold set: 2 of 225 photos move (ig-009, a navy sweatshirt over a white tee with wide cargos and boots, 7.0 → 7.5, panel 7.5; pin-054 parts only), held-out r 0.470 → 0.473, all photos 0.561 → 0.562, his 20 blind ratings 12 within 0.5 → 12, steadiness 218/223 → 218/223, the 8+ share unchanged in every dressiness band (casual 12%, dressy 35%); his 68 saved checks: one moves, his Oct 24 hoodie re-check 6.5 → 7.0. The gold set barely tests the rule (3 photos have a hoodie or sweatshirt layer), so its numbers say "no harm", not "proven".
    - **Port:** parts v15 (parts data 1.14.0) in Python and `@kitted/core`, exact parity (27,748 scoring cases, 29,908 fix cases: 31 scoring cases and 42 fix cases changed, all with a hoodie or sweatshirt layer); a second switch, `HOODIE_LAYER_V15` in `app/packages/kitted-core/src/parts.ts` (false → v14's numbers everywhere; `CASUAL_FITS_V14` false still → v13). `rubric/v5-styles.md` [PRO-17] and KD-22, 13 conformance cases (492 for 151 rules). The re-check card leads "Layered and clean." when the piece he added earns a layer credit (it would have said "Fixed and clean."). #383's A1 and A3 now expect 7.5. `npm run check` green (844 core, 1,613 app tests). New checks only; his stored cards keep their scores.
    - **Color, the founder's follow-up (same day): "include color, not just the layer."** What the Color part did with his outfit under v14: the olive cargos count as a neutral (olive is in the neutrals, family green), the muted green hoodie is the only non-neutral, so it's "the one quiet accent" ([COL-5], +0.5): the same half point the blue tee earned without the hoodie, so no gain. It isn't a second accent (olive is a neutral); [COL-11] needs a bright piece (the hoodie reads muted); the shoes' tie-in can't fire (black never ties in); [COL-7]'s one-family tip needs every piece in one family (the navy tee breaks it). On Oct 24, with the tee read blue, there were two accents and no quiet-accent credit, so Color fell to 7.0.
    - **Decided: [COL-13], +0.5 on Color, in v15 under the same switch:** the one outer layer in the bottoms' color family with a top of another family between them, the shoes' tie-in ([COL-5]) for the layer. Guards: no color fault, never white/black/grey/navy/denim, bright pieces, sweats or joggers, tailoring; all three pieces in one family stays [COL-7]'s tip. Paper (`extraction-test/tonal_layer_paper.py`, v15 without vs with it): 17 bars pass (one, N9, was mis-built: the outfit I wrote had no color fault in the engine; rebuilt as a real loud print, and the plain red tee under the green hoodie, which does earn the credit, is reported as a taste note); 0 of 225 gold photos move (no gold photo has a layer in the pants' family, so the panel can't judge it); his 20, steadiness and the 8+ shares unchanged; of his saved checks only the Oct 24 re-check moves again, 7.0 → 7.5, so both photos of the same outfit now land at 7.5 (v14: 7.0 and 6.5). In the corpus two stock photos (a khaki overshirt over a white tee with tan chinos) move 6.5 → 7.0 at dressy occasions only.
    - **Where his 7.5 comes from:** Proportions 8.5 (the hoodie layer, +1.5), Fit 7, Color 8.0 (quiet accent +0.5, the tie-in +0.5), Shoes 5 (runners −2.0), Style 7: 7.35 raw. The color credit is worth 0.1, so the Why sheet names it but the number comes from Proportions. Parity regenerated (v15 vs v14: 404 cases, 80 with a new overall, all from the two rules); conformance 501 cases for 152 rules; `npm run check` green (853 core, 1,613 app tests).

## Nov 7 · CLAUDE.md becomes rules only; plan.md and specs replace the one big file
