## Nov 7 · The grill-me skill: interview before a spec

387. **The founder (Nov 7): add a "grill me" skill** that interviews him one question at a time, with options and a recommendation, until a feature is clear enough to build, then writes the spec. His draft was taken as the base, with his OK to improve it.
    - **Where:** `.claude/skills/grill-me/` in the repo (`SKILL.md` plus `references/`: the question bank, the spec templates, the plan and CLAUDE.md templates), so every session and worktree of Kitted has it. Not copied to `~/.claude/skills` (the rule: work only in this folder); copying it there would make it work in other projects too.
    - **Changed from his draft:** the project's own file names and spec rules win over the skill's defaults (here: `specs.md`, `specs/<feature>.md`, `plan.md`, specs locked after his yes), so it never starts a `SPEC.md` or `PLAN.md` beside them; a feature on a project with no master spec writes only the feature spec; Claude drafts the acceptance criteria and he confirms them, rather than being asked to write them; the ledger says how far along the interview is; in plan mode the spec goes into the plan and approving it is the yes; the made-up Cmd+Shift+M shortcut is gone; the ledger's "Decisions" label fixed to "Decided"; small, clear requests don't trigger it.
    - **Not checked:** no test runs (they cost usage), and skills load when a session starts, so it is first usable in a new session.

## Nov 7 · specs.md: the launch blueprint, from the founder's interview

388. **The founder (Nov 7): a spec for the whole app, written after a grill-me interview** (one question at a time, options with a recommendation). `specs.md` now exists in the repo root and describes the **App Store launch**; v1.1 ideas sit under Later. It overrides the older docs where they disagree.
    - **Accounts (overturns ruling 8, "none in v1"):** optional sign-in at launch, **Apple and Google only** (email code moves to Later). Signed in, scores, closet, style profile and what Kit knows sync through Firebase.
    - **Photo backup (overturns #268, "on by default"):** a separate switch, **off by default**; when on, photos are stored as he took them, readable only by his account, deleted with the account. The CLAUDE.md privacy hard line now says "no photos unless he turns on photo backup".
    - **No free trial (overturns the 1-week trial, #285).** The founder: "I just don't have a lot of capital to burn through, so I don't want to give promises that I'm not going to be able to keep." Claude's pushback (a trial's worst case is under $1 a person; free users are ~85% of the bill) was heard; the founder chose the free checks below. "Trial ending" leaves the notifications (2 kinds now).
    - **Free (overturns "1 check a day forever"):** 7 free fit checks (each with its re-check), at most 1 a day, used whenever he likes; then new checks need Pro. 3 questions a day, forever. Everything that runs on code stays free forever. Counted on the server against the Keychain device ID and the account, so a reinstall doesn't reset them.
    - **Pro (overturns ruling 25's 10+50):** $9.99 a month or $39.99 a year; "Up to 5 fit checks and 30 questions a day".
    - **Paywall moments (founder's idea, refined):** a skippable soft page after the first result; an upgrade button on the limit card when he tries a second check in a day; the full paywall at check 8, the photo saved either way. Not during onboarding (it would ask before the aha).
    - **Launch bars:** the re-check raises the score on 18 of 20+ real fix pairs; Kitted within 1 point of blind raters (the founder + a friend 18–22) on 8 in 10 of 40+ real photos, no outfit both rate 7.5+ under 6.5, per age band and look. **Success at 60 days:** subscribers cover the AI bill, and 4 in 10 new users see a re-check go up.
    - **Spend cap:** a daily cap in server config, starting at $25; when hit, free checks pause until 4 am (photo saved), Pro continues, the founder is alerted.
    - **Kit's scope:** clothes, shoes and accessories only; anything else gets a friendly decline that doesn't count. **Womenswear:** "not rated yet", not counted, the app open to anyone.
    - **His name:** an optional "What should Kit call you?" on the birth-year step; kept on the phone (synced if signed in), used only in code-written lines like "Morning, Alex."; never sent to Google, OpenAI or PostHog.
    - **In at launch:** scan clothes, voice input. **Later:** the two-shot check, the drawn figure.
    - **Packages OK'd for launch (installed only when each step is built):** RevenueCat, expo-apple-authentication, Google Sign-In, @react-native-firebase/app-check, expo-notifications, PostHog (chosen over our own event counts).
    - **Assumed, flagged in the spec:** scans have their own cap of 30 a day for everyone; sign-in placement and sync conflicts as built; no founding offer; a median check under 8 s on LTE.
    - **Now out of step with the spec (to fix in their own feature work):** the paywall's trial copy, the 10+50 caps, the 5 free questions, the "trial ending" notification, the onboarding paywall, the email-code sign-in screens.

    - **The audit (3 read-only agents, Nov 7):** 23 spec criteria already pass on main and are listed as such; the gaps became 30 tasks in 9 lanes. The biggest: the server has one global `tier` (no Pro per user), the spend cap defaults to $3 and pauses everyone at UTC midnight, App Check isn't sent, sync backs photos up by default, scans aren't read, no womenswear handling, no PostHog, no name field, no Declared Age Range.
    - **Found while reconciling:** 10 open PRs, three carrying spec work (sign-in #58, backup and sync #57, the Welcome example #59); DECISIONS-LOG numbers collide across branches (#386 on main and on PRs #57/#58; #388 here and on PR #59). New trap in CLAUDE.md: renumber a branch's entries on merge. "Delete all my data" clears the phone's ID, which would reset the free checks: default to Apple DeviceCheck (open decision in `plan.md`).
    - **The founder's to-dos reconciled:** done per him: App Store Connect set up, his profile given, the clothes photos shot. Still open: the domain and handles, the support email and links, rotating the Gemini dev key, the interviews, the competitor teardown.
    - **CLAUDE.md:** rule 1 gains the claim step and lane ownership; rule 3 says what a session may change in `plan.md`; `CLAUDE.old.md` moved to `handoff/archive/` (decided in #386) and its three pointers updated.

    - **plan.md formatting:** task details in italics after the plain task, bold IDs and priorities, the notes under Tasks as quotes. Every task stays on one line, so a session still finds and changes a task with one search and one edit.

## Nov 7 · /build-task: four agents, tests first and locked, a fix loop

391. **The founder (Nov 7): after the spec and the plan, code tasks run through a test writer, an implementer and a verifier, then a report.** He brought draft prompts from a chat with Claude and asked for them to be made better. His main point: "tests shouldn't be made easy in order for the evaluation phase to go easier. It should tick off the boxes, and if it can't, it should state it, and we should find a way to check those boxes off." He chose **one command per task** (`/build-task T11`) running **separate child agents** with an **auto-fix loop**, over four skills run by hand or one session doing everything.
    - **Built:** `.claude/skills/build-task/` (the coordinator and the three templates) and `.claude/agents/{task-planner,test-writer,implementer,verifier}.md`. Each agent starts with a fresh mind, so the test writer never sees the code to come, and the verifier never shares the implementer's reasoning. Up to 3 build-and-verify rounds; then it stops and says what's still failing. It pauses only for the founder's calls (taste, words, money, installs); the founder merges.
    - **Against easy tests:**
      - Tests are committed before the code, and the SHA becomes the lock. The verifier's `git diff` from the lock must be empty.
      - Every new test fails first for the right reason, and the failure line is recorded. A test for behaviour that already exists proves it bites with a deliberate bug.
      - The verifier makes one deliberate bug per criterion. If no test catches it, the tests are weak.
      - Banned: source-text tests (42 of 124 app test files are that kind today) for spec criteria, loose matchers, mocking the unit under test, snapshots, `.only`/`.skip`, and edited fixtures.
      - Nothing passes without proof. "Not verified" is never pass and names what would verify it.
    - **Two locks that run on their own:**
      - `.claude/hooks/protect-tests.sh`, attached only to the implementer agent (in its own frontmatter), refuses edits to tests, TESTING.md and fixtures. Checked on 13 sample calls: 7 test edits blocked, 6 normal actions allowed. Limit: a shell command it can't read is still caught by the verifier's git check.
      - `app/src/__tests__/testGuards.test.ts` fails `npm run check` on focused, skipped or snapshot tests (the paid `*.manual.test.ts` may skip). The existing suites are clean.
    - **Fixed in the drafts:**
      - Results go in REPORT.md, so TESTING.md stays locked.
      - UI is checked on the Simulator, not the web preview, with a UI inventory per state (light, dark, the largest text, VoiceOver).
      - The verifier reads the implementer's report last.
      - The test writer may add throwing stubs, so a red test means missing behaviour, not a missing import.
      - The repo's real file names and test places replace the drafts' `SPEC.md`, `PLAN.md` and `tasks/TEMPLATES`.
    - **Defaults the founder accepted with the plan:** task folders are kept after merge as the record (CLAUDE.md's one exception to "no other status file"); the hook goes in; no Maestro yet, so the verifier drives the Simulator itself (Y15 asks for the install).
    - **Not checked yet:** agents and skills load when a session starts, so the first real run happens in a new session. Suggested first run: T63 (behaviour that exists → the "prove it bites" path).

## Nov 7 · Maestro: tap-through flows, replayed on every task

392. **The founder (Nov 7): yes to installing Maestro** (Y15). He ran the two Homebrew commands himself: trusting a package source is his call, not Claude's. Maestro 2.12.1 from mobile.dev's own tap (`mobile-dev-inc/tap`), not a script piped from the internet.
    - **Flows live in `app/e2e/flows/`**; `app/e2e/common/open-demo.yaml` opens any demo screen. Launched by Maestro, the development build stops on its own launcher ("Development servers"), so the sub-flow taps the running Metro server first (trap in the build notes). The first flow, `smoke.yaml`, opens demo screen 12 and finds all four tabs: passed in 27 s on the main Simulator.
    - **In `/build-task`:** the planner can pick "flow" as a check, the test writer writes flows with the tests, the verifier replays **every** flow on every task (a failing old flow is a code defect: this task broke something), and flows are locked like tests (the hook blocks the implementer from editing `app/e2e/`; checked: editing or deleting a flow blocked, running Maestro allowed).

## Nov 7 · The spec gate: no code task starts without its approved spec

393. **The founder (Nov 7): checking that a feature has a spec must be part of the workflow, before any planning or tests, and the workflow should send him into the back-and-forth that defines the feature.** Before this, `/build-task` judged "is the spec approved?" with nothing written to check, and when it stopped it only said "run /grill-me".
    - **Now:**
      - Each lane in `plan.md` names its spec (`Spec:` under the heading) and the interview task that writes it.
      - A feature spec carries `**Approved by the founder:** <date>` under its title, added only after his yes in chat.
      - `/build-task`'s first step is the gate: the file exists, has that line, and contains every criterion ID the task checks. If not, it tells him in plain words which feature isn't defined and how long the interview takes, and asks "Start the interview now?".
      - On yes, it runs `/grill-me` **in the same session** and writes the lane's spec. After his yes it adds the approval line and opens a PR for him to merge. Then it comes back and builds the original task from the updated main.
      - The planner refuses to plan from a missing or unapproved spec, as a second check.
    - **Why the main session, not a subagent:** a subagent can't hold a conversation with him; it runs on its own and returns a result. The interview has to happen where he's talking.
    - **Tasks that need no interview:** marked `spec: specs.md`, because their criteria are already exact there: T62 (the card's words, so its wait on T60 is dropped), T63, and the Launch lane. `specs.md` counts as approved (#388).

## Nov 7 · WORKFLOW.md, and a plain intro for each lane in plan.md

394. **The founder (Nov 7): format the tasks so he has more context and is on the same page; and put the workflow in one Markdown file, for him and for Claude Code, if that helps.** It did: the process was spread across CLAUDE.md, the coordinator, four agent manuals and the plan's legend, and nothing explained it start to finish.
    - **`WORKFLOW.md`** (repo root, reference, not status) covers:
      - the path as a text diagram, readable in the file pane
      - the documents and who changes each
      - what the founder does and what runs by itself, and how to start a task or an interview
      - the six ways tests stay honest, and when a run stops
      - how to read `plan.md`, and where the exact rules live
      - a short glossary (spec, test oracle, TDD, mutation testing, PR, worktree, lane, subagent)
    - **Kept from drifting:** it explains and links, and the manuals win where they disagree. CLAUDE.md says so.
    - **`plan.md`:**
      - each lane opens with one plain italic line: what it's for and what "done" looks like in the app
      - a key for reading a task line
      - the intro, the spec and the files each on their own line
      - every task still on one line, so sessions find and change a task the same way
    - **No tables:** the founder found tables hard to read in the file pane (#389), so lists are used.

## Oct 21 (landed Nov 7) · The colorblind pass: a warning triangle on the scan camera

396. **The colorblind pass (founder, Oct 21, after Claude reviewed his HCI course against Kitted: "do the pass", then "fix the scan camera's cut-off brackets… add the warning triangle like the rest of the app").** About 8% of men are red-green colorblind and the whole audience is men, so the rule from the course (CS 2484 class 11: never encode meaning in color alone; screenshot → desaturate) was checked across the app. Not a launch blocker; the check took one pass.
    - **How:** a code search for color-only signals, then 10 screens viewed normal, simulated deuteranopia (Machado 2009 in linear RGB) and grayscale: the score card, Why, the re-check win, the share card, Closet › Outfits, the Color sheet, the scan camera, the fit camera, the limit card in dark. The web preview carried most of it (same tokens); the Simulator had hung on low memory and was restarted once for the after shot.
    - **Already right:** scores always carry the number and word; `good` always has "+" or a check, `caution` always the triangle (tokens.ts); the most-to-gain part is an up arrow, not copper; the fit camera's guide is dashed vs solid; outfits and pieces are named with their colors; the Color sheet names every swatch (in deuteranopia Green, Burgundy, Red, Orange, Olive and Brown collapse into one mud, so the names carry it); the share card's before/after are a hollow ring vs a filled dot.
    - **The one miss, fixed:** the scan camera marked the piece running off the edge only by copper vs white brackets ("Step back so every piece fits." doesn't say which). It now also gets `exclamationmark.triangle` in copper on the photo-tag backing at the piece's center (`Brackets` in `features/scan/ScanCameraScreen.tsx`). `npm run check` green (1,509 app tests); before/after on the iOS Simulator in `screenshots/colorblind/` (git-ignored).
    - **Seen, not verified (not color):** the Why sheet's "+0.5" was cut to "+0…" on two rows in the web preview; check on iOS.
    - **Later, if beta testers ask:** Kit helping a colorblind user match colors (the measured colors already exist). Suggested, not on the list: one optional beta-signup question about telling colors apart.

## Oct 24 (landed Nov 7) · The camera is his way to the library; a walk of his Oct 24

397. **Asked (Oct 24): why his fit checks come from the library, why "Too dark" flashes on every open, why Mirror rarely finds him; and a visual walk of his Oct 24 on his own data.** Full write-up `handoff/09-ui-review.md` §29.
    - **From his `guide.log` (25 opens, Oct 18–24):** 21 opens ended in a library pick or a close with no body ever seen; the 3 real Mirror attempts found him in 1–3 s and he shot. On Oct 24 he sent 4 different outfits within 3 minutes: shot earlier with the iPhone Camera, scored in a batch. The camera is the only way to the library since #28.
    - **Built:** the dark tip waits for the exposure: no "too dark", no flash offer and no 3-2-1 in the first second after opening or a flip (`createExposureClock`, `T.exposureSettleMs` = 1000 ms; fit and scan cameras). His first frames always read ISO 294 at 1/60 s, luma 0.03–0.14. A really dark room still says so ~1.4 s in. Checks D1–D6 in `guidance.test.ts`, written first.
    - **Not changed:** Mirror's thresholds (every real attempt was found). His calls on the list: his photos without opening the camera, a quiet Propped until it sees him, the Mirror tip's words.
    - **The walk (Simulator, his database and photos, light, dark, the largest size):** fixed W1 (the re-check's "+1.5" pill was cut off at the accessibility sizes; `DeltaRow` wraps there) and W2 (the folded morning row showed the first check's photo next to the re-check's score). Found W3: a "Smaller step" can name the shoes he's wearing, because the advice is made before the closet call matches the pieces; open. Checks W1–W2 in `oct24Walk.test.ts`.
    - **Lesson:** on the Simulator the app starts the demo scenario's fixed clock (`state/boot.ts`); to look at a real phone's day, launch with `-demoNow <that time>`. The dev-launcher openurl keeps the launch arguments.

## Nov 7 · T10: the free-and-Pro spec, with the founder

398. **`specs/free-and-pro.md` written in a `/grill-me` interview and approved by the founder (Nov 7).** It's the oracle for T11 (limits) and T12 (the three upgrade moments).
    - **One paywall page, plans on it** (the founder): the two pages existed only to explain the trial; one fewer tap before a price. `PlansScreen` goes away in T12.
    - **One honest price, always: Yearly $39.99 (selected, "Best value", "$3.33 a month") and Monthly $9.99** (the founder). He first floated ~$5 a month yearly ("Save 49–51%" instead of 67%, which felt like a gimmick), then a $39.99 "one-time offer" with a higher price after. Claude pushed back: in this category the paywall is the 1-star review (GRWM 69% 1–2★), the cheap price would be spent at minute 2 when he's least ready, and at check 8 he'd see he missed it; Apple rejects fake one-time pricing. No percentage badge, no offers at launch; a price test after ~1,000 paywall views and Apple's win-back offer go to Later.
    - **Does $39.99 lose money?** (the founder asked). No, on a typical user: from Jan 2027 prices (`market/ai-costs.md`), a check is ~2.2¢ and a question ~0.04¢; a typical Pro user's AI is ~$1.34 a month against $2.83 kept from yearly ($8.49 monthly). A yearly user at the 5-check cap every day costs ~$6.80 a month (−$3.97); the cap bounds it. A free user now costs ~$0.30 for life (was ~$1.27 a month for a daily user under 1-a-day-forever). The "typical" use is an October estimate until T91 measures it.
    - **Words** (the founder): soft page `{n} free checks left.` / `Pro is here when you want more.`; `Subscribe for $39.99/year`; `Scores, fixes, Closet and Lookbook stay free.`; limit card `Score it now with Pro` with the free-checks count in its body; check 8 `That's your 7 free checks.` / `Go Pro and I'll score this one now.` with the photo `Saved. Not scored yet.`; subtitle `{n} free checks left` → `Last free check`; the 4th question gets a line, no button (three upgrade moments only).
    - **Pro ended** (the founder): no message; free checks he never used stay his (checks made as Pro never count, at most ~13¢ per lapsed user); You → Plan `Pro ended {date}`; the paywall says `Your Pro ended.` when he's next out. Pro holds through Apple's billing grace period (turn it on in App Store Connect with Y7).
    - **Settled by Claude:** the paywall's lines per entry point, the Pro-cap limit card, check 8's "Continue free" leaving the photo saved with `Score it with Pro` / `Don't score it`, You → Plan's `See Pro` (not "Try Pro", which hints at a trial), the store states (existing words kept), the fine print per plan, trial strings removed everywhere.
    - **For T20/T21:** the server counts free checks excluding checks made while Pro.

399. **The beta comes first, free, with no paywall (founder, Nov 8: "I don't want my friends to pay money in order to test the app out… 3 fit checks a day… the goal before launch is setting up RevenueCat").**
    - **The founder:** beta testers never pay; 3 fit checks a day; the paywall, the upgrade moments and RevenueCat (T11, T12, T13, Y7) move to before launch.
    - **Settled by Claude (the question limit, which the founder left open):** 20 questions a day. A question costs ~0.04¢ and a check with its re-check ~2–4¢ (`market/ai-costs.md`), so a tester at both caps every day costs about $4 a month; 20 sits between free's 3 and Pro's 30, and anyone who hits it is a heavy user worth hearing from.
    - **How:** a third server tier, `beta`, which the server's config answers for everyone until launch; the phone follows it, as it already does for the server's Pro tier. No lifetime count, and beta checks never count toward the 7 free checks, so every tester starts launch with 7. The beta reuses FP3's Pro-cap limit card with 3, and You → Plan says `Kitted beta`.
    - **Replaces** the open decision "everyone gets Pro's limits during the beta". Written as FP7 in `specs/free-and-pro.md` and approved by the founder (Nov 8: "Yes, merge and build"); T14 builds it, T85 puts it on TestFlight.

400. **T14 planned: the beta's limits (Nov 8).** Plan: `tasks/T14/IMPLEMENTATION.md`.
    - **Words (the founder, Nov 8):** on the beta, the morning card's used-up line is `Your fit checks reset at 4 am.`, and the saved card drops FP3's last sentence: `Saved. I'll score it when you open Kitted after 4 am.` The free versions are unchanged.
    - **Settled by Claude:**
      - Before the server's first answer, the phone assumes `beta` while the paywall flag is off, and `free` once it's on, so one launch switch flips both.
      - One gate, `paywallOn()`, reads the tier.
      - The server keeps its 2-question slack (22 there, 20 on the phone).
      - Day counts carry across a tier switch.
      - New strings go in `en.ts` `addedInBuild`.
      - Minimal edits outside the lane: transport, session, ask pipeline, usePlan, the You and Plan screens, the morning card, the demo scenarios.
    - **For T11 and T21:** T14 writes no lifetime count and no per-check marker. T11 must count only checks it marks as made on free, and T21 only free-tier reservations, so no beta or dev check ever counts toward the 7.
    - **Known gap (T21 owns it):** the server counts a finished read even when the phone calls it "not rated", so 3 not-rated photos could make the server refuse a real 4th on the beta.
    - **Deploy:** the server change needs a functions deploy, and the live `server/config` set to `beta` or cleared. Both are the founder's go.

401. **T14 built: lessons from running its tests (Nov 8).**
    - **Maestro can't tap a Liquid Glass button.** The camera's interactive "Choose a photo" ignores Maestro's instant tap, and `longPressOn` doesn't help either. Flows reach the same screen by its link (`openLink: "kitted://sheets/sample-photos?mode=check"`, `kitted://you`) when the button only navigates. The verifier checked the button's own route by hand.
    - **Maestro matches whole labels only.** A view with its own VoiceOver label hides the text inside it. So a check that something is absent, written as part of a label, can never fail. Write positive checks as the exact label, and absence checks as `.*words.*`.
    - **Metro started from a worktree without `--clear` served a stale screen** (an old `plan.tsx`). Start it with `--clear` when checking a worktree's screens.
    - **A demo screen that shows the morning card must be in `MORNING_DEMO_SCREENS`** (`features/morning/index.ts`), or `enabled()` blocks the card.

402. **The beta's limits are live on the server (Nov 8; the founder: "Check it after the deployment has been made").**
    - **Before the deploy:** the functions build cleanly, and the emulator tests pass, 79 of 79.
    - **The deploy:** `firebase deploy --only functions:api,functions:purgeUsage --project kitted-a5b45 --non-interactive`. Both functions updated, with no prompts. Nothing else changed: no rules and no indexes.
    - **The live check:** a fresh anonymous guest asked one question on `/v1/ask` (Flash-Lite, 30 tokens out, well under 0.01¢). The server answered 200 with `x-kitted-tier: beta`, and the test guest was deleted afterwards.
    - **So the catch isn't real:** the live `server/config` doesn't set `pro` by hand. The server's own default applies, and it's now `beta`.
    - **Left behind:** that guest's one-question usage day. The 7-day purge removes it.
    - **The script:** in the session's scratchpad (`livetier.mjs`), not in the repo. Re-run the same check after any server deploy or config change.

## Nov 8 · App Store Connect for the beta: the console half of T85

403. **The founder (Nov 8): "finish setting up Apple Connect for beta testing."** Claude read App Store Connect through the Chrome DevTools plugin (the built-in browser's Apple sign-in loaded only after a long wait, and Claude in Chrome found no browser for the account).
    - **Found:** the app record exists, `Kitted: Outfit Rater for Men` (Apple ID `6830467413`, SKU `kitted-ios`, bundle ID `com.trykitted.kitted` as an Xcode-managed App ID, Lifestyle, content rights answered, version 1.0 Prepare for Submission). Free Apps agreement active to Nov 3, 2027; Paid Apps agreement New (Y18, only for purchases). TestFlight had no builds, no groups and empty Test Information. The EU trader (DSA) notice doesn't apply: US only.
    - **Done:** an internal TestFlight group, "Kitted team", automatic distribution on, with the founder (Account Holder) in it. The EAS project `@matemind/kitted` (`a8fb4438-…`) created with `eas init --force` and its ID put in `app.config.ts` (`extra.eas.projectId`, `owner`), since a dynamic config can't be written by EAS. `eas.json` submit gets `ascAppId` and `appleTeamId`, so `eas submit` goes to this app without asking. `EXPO_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_API_KEY` added as a plaintext EAS variable in `production` and `preview` (public by design, #295); a Release build needs nothing else (it always calls the live server).
    - **Not done (needs the founder):** the first build (asks before an EAS build; Apple's login for the distribution certificate is typed by him), Test Information and Beta App Review contact (his name, phone, email), the privacy policy URL that outside testers need (Y2), and T14 and T80 before friends get it.
    - **Noted, not changed:** the app's `version` is `0.1.0` while App Store Connect's version is 1.0. TestFlight accepts it as its own version; before the App Store release they must match.
    - **Lesson:** Apple's sign-in can take many seconds to draw in the built-in browser, so a blank page with `authResult=FAILED` in the URL isn't proof it's broken. And `jest` in a worktree needs `--no-watchman` (`npm run check -- --no-watchman`) when watchman can't start.

404. **The first TestFlight build (Nov 8; the founder: "start the build for the test").**
    - **Built before T80, on purpose:** the paid team's ID is the same as the one the app already names (6652QVRQAL), so a store build signs without the paid-team switch. This build has NWS weather and no App Attest; T80 brings its own build. It also has no sign-in (release builds turn it off, `SIGN_IN_LIVE`) and no notifications (not built yet).
    - **Before the build:** main (T14, T25) merged into the branch; Y18 renumbered to Y19 and this branch's #400 to #403 (both clashed with main). `npm run check` passed (1,648 tests), and `guard:release` passed with the main checkout's `app/.env*` linked in for the run and removed afterwards.
    - **The build:** `eas build --platform ios --profile production --auto-submit`, run in the founder's terminal (his Apple login and 2FA). EAS made the distribution certificate and profile and stores them; `appVersionSource: remote` started the build number at 1. Version 0.1.0, build 1, EAS build `1e8af560`, uploaded to App Store Connect. Apple processed it within minutes ("Ready to Submit" for outside testers, expires in 90 days), and the Kitted team group shows it installed on the founder's iPhone 16 Plus (iOS 26.6.2).
    - **Lesson:** a worktree has no `app/.env*` (git-ignored), so `guard:release` there needs the main checkout's files linked in for the run. EAS doesn't need them; it reads its own environment variables.
    - **Lesson:** inviting testers doesn't cost a build. One build serves every tester and group until it expires (90 days); a new build is needed only when the app changes.

405. **The founder's beta feedback (Nov 8): You is "empty and pointless", there's no step-by-step guide, and he never saw setup.** Interviewed in the you-and-first-run session; `specs/setup-and-you.md` approved Nov 9.
    - **You is the settings home (SY1):** the style card, What Kit knows and Today stay, then the Settings page's rows (Notifications, Appearance, Plan & usage · Privacy & data · Help & about), the version line. No gear, no Settings page; the tab keeps the name "You" ("Settings" reads as tacky to him). In the beta the Settings page held only four rows, one of them already on You. This overrides `research-context.md` §C14's "Settings live behind a gear": Apple's HIG → Settings names no place for an iPhone app's own settings (read Nov 8).

"Show me around" brings them back and posts Kit's hello again. Why: HIG → Onboarding prefers short tips where they apply over one long flow, optional and findable again in a settings area; a tour would sit between him and the first score. Rejected: a swipe tour, both, a guided first check.

Setup's done mark lives in the app's database, which iOS deletes with the app, so reinstalling from TestFlight shows setup as a new user would see it. The age block is in the Keychain and survives. T36 (planned for this) dropped.

    - **`specs.md` (his yes):** F9 describes You as the settings home; F1 gains the tips line; sign-in's placement reads "in You".

## Nov 8–9 · A design stage for /build-task: a designer and a critic

406. **The founder (Nov 8): the workflow has no UI step; he wants "a designer and a critic" next to the spec writer, test writer, builder and verifier, now, before the beta's UI tasks are built.** He picked the shape and the CLAUDE.md rule in chat (Nov 8).
    - **The shape:** step D in `/build-task`, for a task that changes a screen and isn't planned yet. The `ui-designer` draws every state as phone frames in the mockup's own style (`tasks/<ID>/design.html`, PNGs in `design/`) and writes `DESIGN.md`. The `ui-critic` checks them against the HIG, §C14 and the design system (at most 2 rounds), then the founder approves the look. That approval also approves any NEW words. After the build, step 4b: the critic screenshots every state on the Simulator and compares it with the approved frames.
    - **Why two roles and no more:** each agent adds time (about 30–45 min per screen task) and a hand-off where detail drops. A copywriter adds nothing, because the words are the founder's. Accessibility sits inside the critic.
    - **The critic blocks only on rules:** a difference from the approved design, the HIG, §C14, 03 or a hard line. Its taste goes to the founder as optional suggestions, so the loop can't run on opinion. A blocker in the approved design itself (labelled "design") goes to the founder, and only his yes changes a locked inventory word.
    - **Safe for runs already underway:** the existing four agents only gained "if an approved DESIGN.md exists, use it" (the planner copies the inventory from it, the implementer builds to it). The test-lock hook also guards `DESIGN.md`, `design.html` and `design/`. A task planned before this carries on unchanged.
    - **Tools:** `tools/mockshots/design-shell.sh <ID>` starts a design page from the mockup's CSS, icons and frame script, with `tokens.ts` colors laid over the mockup's older ones. `design-shot.sh <ID> <n> <light|dark>` shoots one phone at 2x.
    - **Lesson:** an agent type is loaded when a session starts, so a session older than `ui-designer.md` or `ui-critic.md` can't call them by name. The skill now says to run `general-purpose` with the agent file as its instructions.
    - **Lesson:** the mockup's CSS still carries the pre-contrast-fix greys (`--ink-3` #848A99 / #6E7484, now `inkFaint`). The critic caught 3.14:1 text in the first dry-run frames. Designs must take colors from `tokens.ts`, which the shell now does.

407. **The dry run: T62's head-not-found case (F2.2), designed and approved (founder, Nov 9).** No app code changed.
    - **The designer:** 4 phones, then 5: the camera panel with the spec's line and Retake (Choose another for a library photo), a toast for the rare case after the camera closed, and both at AX5.
    - **The critic, round 1:** 4 blockers: stale greys under 4.5:1; the toast breaking at AX5 (radius 999 clamps and the text leaves its fill); no VoiceOver focus plan; a frozen photo showing a case the engine actually sends (#304, a head cut at the top edge is hidden and sent).
    - **The critic, round 2:** 1 blocker: the drawn head was blurred under "I couldn't find your face to blur it".
    - **The founder's answers:** no "This one doesn't count." under this panel; speak the line in Propped mode; a short toast, not a thread card, for the after-camera case. The toast stays at 5 s; 8 s is an open suggestion.
    - **Raised for T62's planner:** the photo prep reports one `couldNotFrame` status for several causes (undecodable photo, a cover over someone else, "Which one is you?" matching nobody, a lost blurred copy). Only the head case gets the spec's line; the rest keep "Couldn't frame this one. Try again."
    - **Raised for the founder (spec words, not changed):** "Get your head in the shot" misses the usual real causes (turned away, far, dim); "Face the camera…" would fit more. The older docs (02 S3 and S19e, 03 §8) still carry the old copy; the spec wins.

408. **The setup screens, from the founder's Nov 9 walk through the TestFlight build (T37; `specs/onboarding-screens.md`, approved Nov 9).** He said Welcome "uses a photo found on the internet", the birth-year step looks awkward as a chat, and the camera opening on its own after setup is wrong when he's somewhere he can't take a fit check.
    - **The photo:** `s14` is Unsplash's "man walking on a path in a park" by Babak Eshaghian (shot in Tehran). The license allows commercial use with no credit, but its terms (§5) don't cover recognizable people and there's no model release; the face crop lowers that risk without removing it. The Example check used the same photo with "A real photo". **Decided:** both get a code-drawn outfit built from Closet's flats, scored by the engine from that outfit's reading; Welcome plays its top fix once (after state with Reduce Motion). Why: zero licensing risk, it shows the real product ("the code judges"), and no one's body is on screen. The founder doubted he has a good enough photo of himself; a real photo with written OK is parked under Later. Ruled out: paid stock (still a stranger), an AI-made person (a fake man on an honesty-first app), a commissioned illustration ($150–600, weeks, drifts toward a cartoon look).
    - **Plain forms, not a pretend chat:** every setup step is a plain screen with a large title question; no echo or Kit bubbles; Kit's first words come in Stylist after setup. The Example check stays chat-shaped because it previews the chat.
    - **Age bands, not a birth year:** `Under 18 · 18–21 · 22–25 · 26–30 · 31 or older`, none picked, stored as `ageBand` + `ageBandAt` on the phone only. Why (research Nov 9): Apple doesn't require a US 18+ app to collect age itself; Declared Age Range returns ranges and App Review 5.1.4 leans against birthdates; the FTC's neutral-screen advice (COPPA FAQ D.7) is met by equal choices with nothing preselected, though its only worked example is a typed month and year, so bands are defensible rather than proven. Nothing in the app used the year beyond the 18+ check. The old footnote "Kitted is for 18 and up" hinted at the passing answer before he answered, so it goes. Declared Age Range (SY6) is required for new Texas accounts now (SB 2420, in force since June 4, 2026) and in California, Utah and Louisiana in 2027; with gates 18/22/26 it returns the testing bands.
    - **The name field is built now (T31's setup and Style profile part moves into T39),** so About you is built once.
    - **Setup ends in Stylist:** Kit's hello, then "Want to check a fit now?" with Check my fit / Later; the camera opens only on a tap, with the agreement on top the first time (SY5). Shortest path stays 6 taps to a live camera, so `specs.md` F1.1 keeps "6 taps" and adds "the camera never opens until he taps Check my fit" (founder's yes, Nov 9); F1.2 and the first-run list say age bands. The founder asked the notifications session to plan a reminder for someone who tapped Later (`firstCheckOffer.laterAt`).
    - **Its own spec file,** deferring to `specs/setup-and-you.md` for Kit's hello, the tips, the name's rules, the agreement's rules and Apple's age check, so two sessions don't edit one file at once.

411. **T38's design approved (founder, Nov 9, in chat: "Looks right").** Welcome's drawn outfit is the s14 reading's pieces (charcoal denim jacket, white sweater, grey drawstring trousers, grey chunky sneakers) without its photo; the card plays the engine's top fix once, 7.5 Put together → 8.0 Sharp, "Swap the chunky sneakers for low-profile sneakers".
    - **New words:** `onb.example.a11yOutfit` (VoiceOver only) "Drawn example outfit, {occasion}: {pieces}." with the pieces from Closet's names; the card's VoiceOver line is the spec's OS1 template.
    - **His picks:** the after score in ink, not copper (a number and a word carry it; Reduce Motion's still frame matches); "Full check" under the score before the swap; Closet's night-rim rule kept, so the charcoal jacket measures 2.3:1 on the dark background (raising `HALO_LUM` app-wide would be its own Closet task); the drawing stays at accessibility sizes, so Get started sits further down there.
    - **Lesson:** the design caught that 375-pt iPhones (SE, mini) are still supported on iOS 26; fixed positions overlapped there, so Welcome scales its drawing to the space.
